# ADR 04 — JavaScript interop boundary

| | |
|---|---|
| Status | accepted (2026-10-17) |
| Project | Blazix.Primitives |
| Decides | which behaviour lives in JavaScript and how it is partitioned; which DOM attributes JavaScript writes; the C#/JS contract including decision points inside JS-owned loops; the JS toolchain and delivery; portals; the development-first render mode |
| Affects | FLT (all JS-resident interaction and positioning IDs), PSH-PTR-09..28, PSH-ALG-07..12, PSH-DOM-17/18, PSH-EVT-09, PSH-I18N-02 and PSH-EDGE-10..13 (swipe), PSH focus guards and portal IDs, TRN-ANI-03..15, TRN-DOM-01/02/05/06/08/09, TRN-STA-04/05/07/09, CMP (all keyboard, focus, typeahead and registry-move IDs), UBT (all), UTL-DOM-01..12 and UTL-DOM-18..31, UTL-ALG-11, SLD-PTR-*, SLD-ANI-01..07, SCR-PTR-*, SCR-STA-06..18, SCR-ANI-*, DRW-PTR-*, DRW-ALG-*, NUM scrub-area IDs, TST (the `toast` module's IDs), OTP (the `otp-field` module's IDs), TAB indicator IDs, SEL list-alignment and scroll-arrow IDs; EVD-API-05, EVD-EVT-11, EVD-EVT-26, EVD-DIS-01, SLD-PTR-01; RND-STA-01 for mirrored fields (TST, OTP and the eight PSH swipe rows added 2026-11-18, Amendments 2026-11-18) |

## Context

ADR 01 fixed the residency rule (anything that needs a browser API is JavaScript; a frame-rate loop is JavaScript wholesale) and ADR 03 made a bundler unavoidable. The old port placed browser-API code in 36 per-component modules (about 30,600 lines; `floating.js` 5,182) whose shared parts became unmaintainable; the user named composite, floating and roving as the spaghetti. Its JS was hand-minified and the runtime loaded the `.min.js`, so source edits were invisible until regenerated. Its portal moved the DOM node into the container after render and moved it back before disposal, and that worked on both render modes. Blazor's renderer diffs its own tree and never reads the DOM, so an attribute C# never renders is never touched by Blazor, but a change to a C#-rendered `style` string replaces the whole attribute. On WebAssembly a JS listener may invoke a `[JSInvokable]` method synchronously and read the result inside the dispatch; Server cannot.

## Decision

### 1. Partition: one module per mechanism, plus component loops (G2)

| Module | Owns | Doc |
|---|---|---|
| `positioning` | engine assembly, Base UI middleware, anchor tracking, output writes | FLT §5, ADR 03 |
| `interactions` | press-to-toggle, dismissal (outside press, Escape, reference press), focus-to-open, cursor anchoring, hover with safe polygon, delay group, floating tree | FLT §4.7, §4.9, §4.10, §5.3 |
| `focus-manager` | initial focus, modal trap, focus-out close, focus restoration, return focus, outside marking (`aria-hidden`/inert), tabbable queries | FLT §4.8, §4.5 |
| `portal` | container resolution, DOM move and restore, focus guards | FLT §4.4, PSH |
| `transitions` | animations-finished watcher (TRN-ALG-03), next-frame flips of `data-starting-style`/`data-ending-style`, `hidden="until-found"` and `beforematch` (TRN-ALG-07), completion notification | TRN |
| `composite` | roving focus, list and grid navigation, typeahead, registry move detection, per-key `preventDefault` | CMP |
| `button` | keydown-to-click synthesis with modifiers, pointer-type classification, disabled press suppression, focusable-when-disabled, press-and-hold timers | UBT |
| `scroll-lock` | document scroll lock | UTL §4.8, UTL-ALG-01/02 |
| `dom` | platform flags computed once, owner/shadow-DOM helpers, rect/computed-style reads, `ResizeObserver`/`IntersectionObserver` subscriptions used by PSH auto-resize and viewport, Collapsible height, Tabs indicator, Select alignment, Avatar image load | UTL §4.10, §4.11, §4.16 |
| `swipe` | the shared swipe tracker | PSH §4.7.5 |
| component modules `slider`, `scroll-area`, `drawer`, `number-field`, `tabs`, `select` | that component's ALG sections and loops, composed from the modules above; the scopes of `scroll-area` and `number-field` are itemised in Amendments 2026-11-18 (amended 2026-11-18) | component docs |

Rules: a mechanism module never imports a component module; a component module contains only its own component's ALG-tagged behaviour and composes mechanism modules; each mechanism module maps to exactly one architecture doc's JS-resident requirements. Adding a module amends this ADR. Everything not listed is C#: the popup store, controlled state, event details, field and form infrastructure, timers whose result is state, and all parts. Composite keyboard navigation is deliberately JavaScript: it reads native `disabled`/`aria-disabled` and visibility, calls `focus()`, and must `preventDefault` only for keys it handles; C# receives active-index and highlight changes.

Each JavaScript module has one C# **twin** that owns the interop handle and exposes typed callbacks; parts talk to twins, never to `IJSRuntime` directly.

### 2. Attribute ownership: single writer, JavaScript for frame-timed values, C# mirroring (O2)

Every DOM attribute and style property on a part's host element has exactly one writer. The writer is JavaScript when the value changes at frame timing or is produced by a JS loop; otherwise it is C#. A per-part **ownership list** names the JS-owned attributes; the initial list:

| Part(s) | JS-owned |
|---|---|
| every `*.Positioner` | `style` (transform/top/left, `--anchor-*`, `--available-*`, `--transform-origin`), `data-side`, `data-align`, `data-anchor-hidden` |
| every `*.Arrow` | `style` (position), `data-side`, `data-align`, `data-uncentered` |
| every animated part (TRN-DOM-04 list) | `data-starting-style`, `data-ending-style`; popups additionally the `transition: none` of TRN-DOM-05 |
| `Collapsible.Panel`, `Accordion.Panel` | `hidden="until-found"`, measured `--collapsible-panel-height/width` |
| `Drawer.Popup`, `Drawer.Backdrop`, `Drawer.Indent*` | swipe movement variables, `data-swiping` (Popup and Backdrop only; amended 2026-11-18), `data-swipe-dismiss`, `--drawer-swipe-progress`, `--drawer-height`, `--drawer-swipe-strength` |
| `ScrollArea.Root` | `data-scrolling`, `--scroll-area-corner-height/width` (amended 2026-11-18) |
| `ScrollArea.Viewport` | `data-scrolling`, `--scroll-area-overflow-x-start`, `-x-end`, `-y-start`, `-y-end` (amended 2026-11-18) |
| `ScrollArea.Content` | `data-scrolling` (added 2026-11-18) |
| `ScrollArea.Scrollbar` | `data-hovering`, `data-scrolling`, `--scroll-area-thumb-height/width` (added 2026-11-18) |
| `ScrollArea.Thumb` | `data-scrolling`, `transform`, the overscroll `--scroll-area-thumb-height/width` (amended 2026-11-18) |
| `ScrollArea.Corner` | `width`, `height` (added 2026-11-18) |
| `Slider.Thumb`, `Slider.Indicator` | the whole `style` after attach: `--position`, `--start-position`, `--relative-size`, inline `visibility`; in `'center'` mode `position`, `inset-inline-start` or `bottom`, the Thumb's `top` or `left`, `translate` and `z-index`, the Indicator's `width` and `height` (amended 2026-11-18: centre-mode properties added, `--end-position` dropped) |
| `Tabs.Indicator` | `--active-tab-*`, `hidden` until measured |
| `Toast.Root` | swipe movement variables, `data-swiping`, `data-swipe-direction` |

Consequences of the rule: C# excludes JS-owned state fields from the `data-*` projection (the custom mapping returns nothing) but keeps the value in the state object through a twin callback fired only when the value changes, so `ClassValue`/`StyleValue` functions still see it; on Server that mirror lags one round trip, which is the RND-STA-01 relaxation for mirrored fields. On an element with JS-owned style properties C# never renders `style`: it passes its merged style string to the twin and JS composes `element.style` (one dated exception, `Drawer.Popup`'s `--nested-drawers` and `--drawer-frontmost-height`, Amendments 2026-11-18). Prerender emits no JS-owned attribute; the configured `side`/`align` are not rendered until the first positioning pass. One named exception, the **optimistic convergent write**: at exit-animation end JavaScript may set `hidden` or remove the element from view before C#'s unmount batch arrives, permitted only because C#'s next render is already determined and renders the same value.

### 3. Contract and decision points (U2)

* **C# to JS.** `attach(elementOrId, options) → handle` returning an `IJSObjectReference` per attached part with `update(options)` and `dispose()`; no module-level `WeakMap` registries. Options are plain records serialized to JSON. The host element is located by the part's generated id when no `ElementReference` exists (substitute callback, ADR 02).
* **JS to C#.** The twin exposes `[JSInvokable]` methods; JavaScript calls them through a `DotNetObjectReference`, one message per state change and never per frame. `details.event` (EVD) is a C# snapshot record (`Kind`, `Type`, key and modifier fields, `PointerType`, `Detail`, `Buttons`, a target descriptor, `DefaultPrevented`, `TimeStamp`); EVD-API-05's `instanceof` form becomes the `Kind` field, and the `base-ui` placeholder is `Type = "base-ui"`.
* **Decision points.** Where a C# handler could change the outcome of a JS-owned dispatch (Escape close: EVD-EVT-11, EVD-DIS-01; drag start: SLD-PTR-01's default-prevented clause; consumer `PreventBaseUIHandler()` in `onKeyDown` against composite or button handling: EVD-EVT-26; button activation; Drawer's snap-point change and swipe dismissal: `snap-point`, `dismiss`; OtpField's edit: `edit`, the last three added 2026-11-18), the module calls one helper, `decide(point, eventInfo, fallbackPolicy)`. On WebAssembly it invokes the twin synchronously, runs the C# chain, and returns the verdict; the requirement is met as written. Elsewhere it returns the fallback policy. Fallback policies: **prevent and stop** for Escape when the owning popup is the innermost open one; **start** for drags; **run** for composite and button handling, and for `snap-point`, `dismiss` and `edit` (amended 2026-11-18). Only synchronously completing consumer handlers can veto; async handlers run but cannot veto (documented, same as React).

### 4. Toolchain and delivery (T1)

TypeScript sources inside the library project; esbuild runs from an MSBuild target before the C# build (`node` required at build time only) and emits one ESM entry per module of §1 into `wwwroot/js/` with shared chunks (floating-ui is a chunk fetched when the first positioned popup attaches). Outputs are not committed; CI fails if the JS build did not run. Twins lazy-import their module on first use. `Blazix.Primitives.lib.module.js` is the JS initializer: it computes the platform flags once (UTL-ALG-11) and registers any custom event types. JavaScript unit tests (the FLT/UTL algorithm tests that are pure JS) run under a JS test runner in the same toolchain (ADR 06).

### 5. Portals

The `portal` module moves the portal node into the resolved container (`body` by default; a selector, element or shadow root via `Container`) after the part's first render and moves it back to its original parent before Blazor removes it (dispose), the pattern the old port proved on both modes. Blazor's logical tree is untouched, so cascading values, parameters and Blazor event bubbling keep working; the node is rendered `display: none` until moved so nothing paints in the wrong place.

### 6. Development order

Implementation and the browser test suite target Server first and WebAssembly second, because Server is the design floor (ADR 01) and surfaces every timing dependency that WebAssembly hides.

## Consequences

* ADR 01's relaxation table is updated: the conditional TRN-STA-04/05/07/09 rows are met (JS-owned flips); the Escape rows carry the "prevent and stop" fallback; new rows record consumer vetoes of JS-owned loops on Server and the mirrored-field lag of RND-STA-01.
* The ownership list is lint-checkable against the projection mappings and must be kept in each component doc's §5 table (a `Writer` column) when the docs are revised for implementation.
* JS modules are reviewable against one architecture doc each; the JS line count is expected to be of the old port's order but partitioned.

## Verification items

* Server preserves the order between JS-to-.NET invocations and Blazor event dispatches on one circuit. **Verified 2026-10-20 (both modes, the twin base):** `tests/browser/harness.spec.ts` "@verifies ADR-04/1 Server preserves the order between JS-to-.NET invocations and Blazor event dispatches on one circuit": the probe module's element listener reports through the twin before Blazor's document-level dispatcher sees the same click, and five clicks arrive in C# strictly alternating. [issue 6](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/6).
* A render queued inside a synchronous invoke on WebAssembly flushes before the JS listener returns (otherwise `decide()` must not rely on rendered state). **Verified 2026-10-20 (WebAssembly):** `tests/browser/harness.spec.ts` "@verifies ADR-04/2 on WebAssembly a render queued inside a synchronous invoke flushes before the JS listener returns": the C# `Decide` handler calls `StateHasChanged`, and the module reads the new attribute value before its listener returns. On Server the same module takes the fallback path (`data-verdict-source="fallback"`), as §3 prescribes. [issue 7](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/7).
* Moving a rendered node and restoring it before disposal never produces a Blazor `insertBefore`/`removeChild` on a foreign parent when the portal node has siblings in its logical parent (the old port avoided this by rendering the portal node as the only child of its wrapper; keep that shape). **Verified 2026-10-27 (both modes, M4 track 1, [issue 8](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/8)):** `tests/browser/portal.spec.ts` "@verifies ADR-04/3 Moving a rendered node and restoring it before disposal never produces a Blazor insertBefore/removeChild on a foreign parent when the portal node has siblings in its logical parent", on the test-only `FixturePortal` with the wrapper shape of the Amendments 2026-10-27 (decision 5): the node is the only child of a `display: contents` wrapper, Blazor inserts and removes siblings around the wrapper and edits the node's children while the node is moved, the portal is removed and rendered again, and the page reports no error; the node leaves with its wrapper (a `childList` observer) before `dispose` arrives.
* The esbuild MSBuild integration works on a machine without `node` when `BlazixSkipJsBuild=true` and prebuilt outputs are present (packaging path). Verified locally 2026-10-20 with `node` off `PATH` (steps in `src/Blazix.Primitives/js/CONTEXT.md`); **Verified 2026-10-20 by the manual job `.github/workflows/skip-js-build.yml` (M0 Track 4), dispatched on `main` at 5c5b76d: run 39442087672, success.** With `PATH` reduced to `/usr/share/dotnet:/usr/bin:/bin` and `node`/`npm` asserted absent, `dotnet pack src/Blazix.Primitives -c Release -p:BlazixSkipJsBuild=true` built the package from the `js-outputs` artifact of the first `main` run (15 entries asserted by `assert-package.mjs`), and a build with the outputs removed failed with the `The JavaScript build output ... is missing` message. The job is run before every tagged release (ADR 14 §1). [Issue 9](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/9) closed.

## Considered options

* Per-component modules (old port); one monolithic module (same organisation, worse loading).
* C# renders all state-derived attributes (Server lag on every transition start and side flip; `style` collision unsolved); JS owns every attribute on popup elements (removes those parts from bUnit).
* Uniform asynchronous contract (simpler, forfeits WebAssembly fidelity that costs one helper).
* Plain JS without a bundler (excluded by floating-ui); single committed bundle (invisible-edit hazard, pay-for-everything loading).
* Portals through Blazor sections (`SectionOutlet`): requires a consumer-placed outlet, breaks cascading values unless re-provided, and cannot express an arbitrary `container` element.

## Amendments

**2026-10-18, handover at attach (settles the ADR 03 prerender conflict, COL-ANI-14 / ACC-ANI-03, TRN §10 Q9, collapsible §16 Q11, accordion §16 Q14; decided in the second-pass review discussion).**

Decision 2's single-writer rule is refined to *initial writer C#, steady-state writer JavaScript*. For every JS-owned attribute or style property, C# renders the initial value once, in prerender and in the first interactive render: the consumer's merged `style`, the disabled-transition declarations (`animation-name: none`), the seeded `--available-width`/`--available-height`, the requested `side`/`align`. From the moment the twin's `attach` succeeds, the value C# rendered is frozen in the part's render output (so Blazor, which diffs against its own previous output, never writes the attribute again) and JavaScript owns the attribute: it composes `element.style` from the consumer's merged style string, passed on `attach` and on every `update(options)`, plus its own declarations. Consumer `ClassValue`/`StyleValue` functions are unaffected: the part evaluates them on every render; before attach the result is rendered, after attach it travels in `update`. The ownership list gains an "initial value rendered by C#" column. The sentence "prerender emits no JS-owned attribute; the configured `side`/`align` are not rendered until the first positioning pass" is withdrawn; prerender emits the values ADR 03 lists. The optimistic convergent write is unchanged.

**2026-10-18, module placement rule and five new modules (settles the ownership gaps found by the second pass: otp-field §16, toast §16, checkbox §16 Q12, context-menu §16 Q14, navigation-menu §16 Q14, tooltip §14, FFI §10 Q9, checkbox-group §16, slider §16 Q15, tabs §16 Q15).**

Decision 1's table is governed by a placement rule instead of being a closed list: *a behaviour that needs a browser API lives in the mechanism module of its architecture doc; an architecture doc whose JS-resident requirements have no module gets one; a component whose requirements include a frame-rate loop, a same-task measurement, selection, clipboard or composition handling, or a keyboard model that shares an algorithm with a JS loop gets a component module.* Adding a module still amends this table.

| Module | Owns | Doc |
|---|---|---|
| `form` (mechanism) | native submit interception (always prevented, re-issued by C# after validation; FFI-EVT-01, FFI-FRM-04, FRM-EVT-01), validity read and custom validity messages, disabled / form-owner eligibility, native label lookup, focus-first-invalid with `select()`, the Enter-to-submit path of FFI-KEY-02, `getFormValue` DOM reads | FFI |
| `otp-field` (component) | per-slot keydown, paste, composition and input handling: selection ranges, value distribution, focus movement, DOM value restore; reports one value change per edit (reworded 2026-11-18, Amendments 2026-11-18) | otp-field |
| `toast` (component) | Toast's own swipe gesture and its movement variables (the shared `swipe` tracker is Drawer's) (widened 2026-11-18, Amendments 2026-11-18) | toast |
| `context-menu` (component) | `contextmenu` prevention, the document suppressor, the 500 ms long-press timer, the one-shot document `mouseup`; reports the open request with the anchor point and pointer type | context-menu |
| `navigation-menu` (component) | the viewport/popup size pass (frames, ResizeObserver, MutationObserver, close-size freeze) and the Content move into the Viewport through the `portal` module's move primitive | navigation-menu |
| `menu-filter` (component) | Menu's filter mode: the Input's composition, committed-value writes, key router, `data-highlighted` and WebKit `aria-selected`; the Trigger's relay; the List's `mousedown` default, key replay and item texts; the Popup's background `mousedown`, refocus and cross-axis close key | menu (filter mode) |

Placements under constraint C1: Slider thumb keydown moves into the `slider` module next to the drag loop, so `roundValueToStep`, `getSliderValue` and `validateMinimumDistance` exist once; the module moves the thumb optimistically and reports the value change, and a cancel rolls back through `update` (the drag loop's existing semantics). The `tabs` module measures the two Tab rects on activation and reports `activationDirection` with the index. Nested tooltip-trigger detection (TIP-PTR-17..22) lives in `interactions`. The ownership list of Decision 2 gains rows for `OtpField` slot values (JS-written on edit; C#-rendered initially and on `update`), `ContextMenu` anchor point (JS), and `NavigationMenu.Viewport`/`Popup` size variables (`--popup-width/height` and the viewport variables; JS); the existing `Toast.Root` row is unchanged.

**2026-10-18, dispatch-time decisions are JavaScript-only; no Blazor event directives on parts (settles UBT-KEY-03/04, OTP-FOC-08, OTP-KEY-13, OTP-EVT-16, OTP-PTR-02, OTP-KEY-01, OTP-KEY-19 and the Radio Enter suppression).**

1. Anything that must happen inside the browser's event dispatch — `preventDefault()`, `stopPropagation()`, pointer capture, clipboard and selection reads, composition handling — is done only by the JavaScript modules. Parts register C# handlers only for events whose result is state; events a module owns reach C# as twin callbacks, never as a second Blazor handler on the same element.
2. Consumer vetoes have two channels, by origin. From JavaScript: a consumer listener on the part's element or a descendant that calls `preventDefault()` is honoured because every module checks `event.defaultPrevented` wherever the Base UI source does; this holds on both render modes and keeps UBT-KEY-03/04 and the OTP veto requirements as written (including the non-cancelable focus/blur cases, where the flag is set although nothing is cancelled, as React's synthetic event imitated). From C#: `PreventBaseUIHandler()`, carried by `decide()`; synchronous on WebAssembly, the "run" fallback on Server. The `otp-field` module's mousedown, keydown and paste join the decision-point list with that fallback.
3. Blazor's event directives (`@on…:preventDefault`, `@on…:stopPropagation`) are never used by library parts and are rejected on them: they are applied by Blazor's document-level dispatcher after the modules' element-level listeners have run, so a module cannot see them, and they are unconditional. In development the pipeline throws (a new RND-EDGE diagnostic, message adapted to Blazor terms) when a directive-generated key (`__internal_preventDefault_*`, `__internal_stopPropagation_*`) appears in a part's unmatched bag or in a substitute element's attribute frames (ADR 02 frame rewriting); the message names the two sanctioned channels.
4. Library-internal suppressions (Radio's Enter suppression through UBT-KEY-03) are options of the owning module, not vetoes.

Verified 2026-10-18 (SDK 10.0.301, throwaway Razor class library): `<Part @onclick:preventDefault @onkeydown:stopPropagation />` compiles to `AddEventPreventDefaultAttribute(1, "onclick", true)` and `AddEventStopPropagationAttribute(2, "onkeydown", true)` on the component frame, i.e. attribute frames that reach `CaptureUnmatchedValues` (the key is the `__internal_preventDefault_<event>` / `__internal_stopPropagation_<event>` name `RenderTreeBuilder` uses; confirm the literal in the first L2 test). Combining `@onclick="H"` with `@onclick:preventDefault` on a component is already a compile error (RZ10010, duplicate parameter `onclick`, matched case-insensitively). The development-time diagnostic of item 3 is therefore implementable as a key check on the unmatched bag and on substitute-element attribute frames.

**2026-10-18, convergent write for Avatar; hovered-line coordinates are JavaScript's (settles avatar §16 item 8, preview-card §14 PRV-STA-13).**

The optimistic convergent write of Decision 2 (JavaScript may set a value C#'s next render is already determined to render) is extended from exit-animation `hidden` to the Avatar load status: the `dom` module's image-load primitive toggles the Image's and the Fallback's visibility at load time, and C# renders the same values when `loadingStatus` arrives; AVA-EDGE-02/03 therefore hold on both render modes. PreviewCard's hovered-line coordinates (PRV-STA-13) are owned by JavaScript end to end: the `interactions` module records them from the Trigger's hover handlers and the `positioning` module's inline-rect middleware reads them; for an open with no recorded pointer position the positioning module derives the point from the trigger rect. C# never writes them. The microtask mapping for PSH-STA-23 and FLD-FRM-17 is in ADR 01 (constraint C2, amended 2026-10-18).

**2026-10-18, untested Base UI quirks (settles context-menu §16 Q13 / menu §16 Q9, menu §16 Q10 / menubar §16 Q3, tooltip §16 Q16).**

Not replicated: CTX-KEY-07 — the port's `context-menu` module does not pass the nested close key to a root context menu, so a root context menu never closes with reason `list-navigation`; the doc keeps the Base UI description at SHOULD. Replicated as the source does: a plain menu opened on hover resolves `initialFocus` like any other open and moves focus into the popup (menu §16 Q10, menubar §16 Q3); the Tooltip Popup-leave close delay uses the Trigger's `closeDelay ?? 0` rather than the Provider's value (tooltip §16 Q16, to be reported upstream).

**2026-10-20, from the implementation revision (settles the ADR 04 gaps listed in `_qa-notes.md` "Implementation revision"; decided in the grilling of ADRs 07–16).**

*Ownership list (Decision 2), new rows:*

| Part(s) | JS-owned | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `Checkbox.Root` hidden input | the `indeterminate` DOM property | a `dom` primitive sets it on `attach`/`update` and re-asserts it after a native click on the hidden input inside the dispatch (CHK-DOM-13) |
| every Drawer part (`Root`, `Popup`, `Backdrop`, `Indent*`, `SwipeArea`) | `data-swiping` | uniform: `swiping` is a mirrored field; the Root's row in `drawer.md` is corrected from `C#` to `JS` (corrected 2026-11-18: Popup and SwipeArea only mirrored, Backdrop imperative, Root and Indents none; Amendments 2026-11-18) |
| `Drawer.Viewport` | `--drawer-keyboard-inset` | the `drawer` module follows `visualViewport`; C# holds no field for it |
| `NavigationMenu.Positioner` | `--positioner-width`, `--positioner-height` | written by the `navigation-menu` module's size pass beside `--popup-width/height` (NAV-DOM-29) |

*Module owners (Decision 1, under the placement rule):* the live-region marker of UTL-A11Y-01..04 (text-node mutation after paint, the 200 ms restore, the iOS exclusion) is one `dom` primitive with its own timer; the Radio focus-driven selection of RAD-FOC-05 is an option of the `composite` module (RadioGroup passes it): the pending-navigation flag is set by composite's arrow handling and consumed in its focus handler, which clicks the hidden input synchronously, so no Radio module exists; the `slider` module classifies each thumb blur by `relatedTarget` (to a sibling thumb or outside) and reports one message for SLD-FOC-04 and SLD-FRM-06, C# applying field focus, touched and blur-mode validation.

*Decision points (Decision 3), added:* ChipRemove activation (CMB-KEY-10, CMB-PTR-10, CMB-EVT-09) with fallback **stop**; the closed-popup Escape clear (CMB-KEY-05) with fallback **stop when there is something to clear and the root is not inline**, decided in JavaScript from the `update` options `hasValue` and `inline`; the Radio focus-driven selection with fallback **run** (a consumer JavaScript `preventDefault()` on the non-cancelable focus event is honoured as the amendment of 2026-10-18 states). The `context-menu` module prevents the native menu and records the anchor synchronously on both modes; the open request of CTX-EVT-02/CTX-PTR-01 runs inside the dispatch on WebAssembly only (ADR 01 row).

**2026-10-20, the directive-key diagnostic is always on (M0 Track 1, issue 31; chosen by the user the same day).** Item 3 of the 2026-10-18 amendment ("in development the pipeline throws") reads: the pipeline throws in every build when a `__internal_preventDefault_*` or `__internal_stopPropagation_*` key appears in a part's unmatched bag or in a substitute element's attribute frames. See ADR 02 Amendments 2026-10-20.

**2026-10-21, the event snapshot of a request a C# handler starts (Decision 3; M1 track 4, issue 61 Q3; option (b), chosen by the user the same day).** Decision 3's `details.event` is the snapshot JavaScript takes inside the dispatch. A request that a part's C# handler starts has no such snapshot: CollapsibleTrigger's and Toggle's `onclick`, which runs after the `button` module, and any later C#-dispatched key. Such a request builds its snapshot from Blazor's event arguments with the internal `EventSnapshot.FromEventArgs(MouseEventArgs)` / `FromEventArgs(KeyboardEventArgs)`.
- `Kind` follows the argument type: `PointerEventArgs` → `PointerEvent`, `WheelEventArgs` → `WheelEvent`, other mouse arguments → `MouseEvent`, `KeyboardEventArgs` → `KeyboardEvent`.
- `Type`, `Detail`, `Buttons`, `AltKey`/`CtrlKey`/`MetaKey`/`ShiftKey`, `PointerType`, `Key` and `Code` are copied.
- What the C#-built snapshot lacks: `Target` is `null`, `DefaultPrevented` is `false` and `TimeStamp` is `0`. `Kind` reads `MouseEvent` for a click Chromium delivers as a `PointerEvent`; `IsInstanceOf(EventKind.MouseEvent)` holds for both.
- The keyboard-versus-pointer `detail` and the modifiers survive, which is what the EVD tests read.

The rejected alternatives:
- (a) The twin reports the click's snapshot. That costs a second message per click on Server (ADR 16 rule 6) and needs fragile pairing with Blazor's dispatch.
- (c) The `base-ui` placeholder. It loses every field.

The inverse, `snapshot.ToKeyboardEventArgs()`, is what the `activate` decision point hands a consumer's C# key handler. Tested by `tests/Blazix.Primitives.Tests/Events/EventSnapshotFromEventArgsTests.cs`.

**2026-10-21, the open input of an attached animated part and the decision point `reveal` (Decision 3; M1 track 5, issue 62; option B and the `reveal` proposal, chosen by the user the same day).**

*Contract, the open input.*
- Besides `update(options)`, the `transitions` module reads a part's open state from the attributes C# renders on the part's element.
- The part names them with a selector in its options, `openSelector`: for example `[data-open]` on the Collapsible Root and Panel, `[data-panel-open]` on the Trigger, and `:not([aria-hidden])` on a kept-mounted `Avatar.Image`.
- The module reads the selector in a `MutationObserver` callback, which runs after the render batch is applied and before paint. With a selector set, the applied DOM is the input and `update`'s `open` is not read after `attach`, so a late `update` cannot reopen a part that a newer batch closed.
- Decision 2's single writer stands: JavaScript writes `data-starting-style` and `data-ending-style` from `attach`, and they land with the batch that opens or closes an already-attached element on both modes.
- C# renders the initial status under the handover and keeps the mounted flag's rules for a newly rendered element. ADR 01 Amendments 2026-10-21 record the evaluation step in both languages.

*Decision points, added.*
- `reveal`: a `beforematch` on a hidden-until-found panel (TRN-ALG-07, TRN-ANI-32..34, TRN-EVT-09), with fallback **run**.
- Inside the dispatch the module removes `data-starting-style` and arms the one-shot motion skip before it decides, and restores both on `stop`. On WebAssembly the C# request commits the open inside the decision, and the open render's `update` re-enters the module before `decide` returns, so it finds them already applied.
- On Server, `run` is returned without a call. The request is then an invocation of the twin's `OnRevealRequest`, whose `Task<bool>` result says whether the part is open once the request ran. A `false` answer, with no open arrived, reverts the panel to `hidden="until-found"` on the next frame. The answer is asynchronous, so ADR 01 decision 1 holds: no synchronous invoke moves state.
- ADR 01 relaxation table, row added 2026-10-21.

**2026-10-21, a twin's host element may be its Root's element (Decision 3; M1 track 7, [issue 64](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/64); chosen by the user the same day).** A part owns its twin; the twin's host element may be its Root's element. `Avatar.Image` in preload mode has no element until its image has loaded, so its own `DomTwin` attaches to the `Avatar.Root` element, found through the Root's context, and runs the detached probe there; in kept-mounted mode it attaches to the `<img>`, and a runtime switch of `KeepMounted` disposes the handle and attaches again. The Root draws a generated id under a substitute (`Render` or `RenderFunction`), as Button does (ADR 02 decision 1), so the twin can find it without an element reference. The rule that one twin is never attached to several parts' elements at once is unchanged: the twin has one live handle.

**2026-10-21, the `collapsible` component module (M1 track 6, issue 63; placed by the orchestrator under the placement rule, issue 62 point 3).** Decision 1's table gains a row:

| Module | Owns | Doc |
|---|---|---|
| `collapsible` (component) | the panel's measurement passes interleaved with the transition flips (useCollapsiblePanel: animation type detection, the opening and closing passes with the held and cached size, the alignment reset, the zero-size and no-motion exits, the one-shot suppression of the opening keyframe animation), the until-found write and the `beforematch` reveal of the panel, composed from the `transitions` module's exported factories and `dom`'s reads. `Collapsible.Panel` attaches it; `Accordion.Panel` (M3) attaches the same module | collapsible (COL-ANI-01..30, COL-DOM-12..18) |

The panel's twin is `CollapsibleTwin`; the Collapsible Root and Trigger keep `TransitionsTwin`, with the new `TransitionsOptions.ExternalExit`: their exit ends only when the panel's ends (`UnmountRequests`), as Base UI's root status does (COL-STA-05, COL-DOM-03/06).
- The module reports the forced idle, the end of the exit, the detected type and the reveal, each once per change.
- It re-resolves a withheld or replaced element by the panel's id on `update` (COL-ANI-15/18/19).
- After the closing measurement it computes the element's style, so the closing transition starts from the held pixel size. This matters because the ending flip lands in the next frame's callback, before that frame computes styles.
- `moduleKinds` has the row, and the module-graph lint holds.

**2026-10-22, JavaScript owns the roving `tabindex` and the hidden input's `checked` after attach (M2 planning; chosen by the user the same day).** Decision 2's ownership list gains two rows, written in full by the implementing pull requests. (1) `tabindex` on a composite item (CMP-DOM-01; RAD-FOC-01 and the M3 composite families) is written by the `composite` module from `attach`, in the same task as the focus move, so CMP-FOC-02's "focus and tab stop stay together" is met on both modes; C# renders the initial value under the handover rule, and a consumer `tabindex` carried in `update` wins (RAD-DOM-16). (2) The `checked` property of the hidden `<input type="checkbox">` or `<input type="radio">` of Switch, Checkbox and Radio is re-asserted by the `dom` primitive that owns `indeterminate` (Amendments 2026-10-20) on `attach` and on `update`, and restored on `update` when C# raises `DomOptions.CheckedRestores` after a request that left its value different from the input's new `checked` (chosen by the user 2026-10-22, mechanism refined by track 4: no re-assert inside a native click, which would undo an accepted toggle on Server before C# commits it); a cancelled toggle is decided inside the input's click on WebAssembly through the `button` module's decision point `toggle` (chosen by the user 2026-10-22; first written as "before the synthetic click through the `activate` decision point") and restored about two round trips later on Server (the report, then the `update` sent from `OnAfterRender`; measured by track 4, see ADR 01's row), a new ADR 01 row of the "C#-origin consumer vetoes" class that the Switch track writes. The `Writer` cells of SWT, CHK, RAD and RDG change to JS for these attributes in the implementing pull requests (ADR 13 §3), and the ownership registry gains the rows. The alternative, C# rendering both with two relaxation rows, left the tab stop a round trip behind the focused item on Server and a cancelled box flipped for a round trip.

**2026-10-22, the ownership row for the hidden input's `checked` (M2 track 4, [issue 80](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/80); item 2 of the note above, chosen by the user 2026-10-22, mechanism refined by track 4).** Decision 2's ownership list, in full:

| Part(s) | JS-owned | Initial value rendered by C# | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Switch.Root`, `Checkbox.Root`, `Radio.Root` hidden `<input type="checkbox">` / `<input type="radio">` | the `checked` DOM property | the `checked` attribute, in prerender and the first interactive render, then frozen (the handover at attach) | the `dom` primitive (`DomOptions.Checked`) sets it on `attach` and on every `update`. It does not re-assert it inside a native click, as it does `indeterminate`, because that would undo a toggle C# accepts before its commit arrives (a round trip on Server). A click C# refuses is reverted natively on WebAssembly by the `button` module's `toggle` decision (`preventDefault()` on the input's click; chosen by the user 2026-10-22), and a read-only or pre-prevented click never flips the box. After the owning form's `reset` the primitive re-asserts the property one task later (switch §16 Q1 option (b), chosen by the user 2026-10-22; FFI-FRM-30's dated note). On Server the part raises `DomOptions.CheckedRestores` after a request that leaves its value different from the one the click set (a `Cancel()`, or a controlled consumer that does not pass the value back), so the `update` that restores the property is sent although `Checked` is unchanged (the `TransitionsOptions.UnmountRequests` precedent). The new ADR 01 row of the "C#-origin consumer vetoes" class states the Server substitute |

The `Writer` cells of SWT-DOM-08 (and of CHK, RAD and RDG in their pull requests) read `C# → JS` under conventions §3.4: C# renders the initial value for prerender. Tested by `js/dom.test.ts` ("SwitchRoot hidden input checked") and `DomTwinContractTests`.

**2026-10-22, the hidden-input bridge on the `button` row (M2 track 4, [issue 80](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/80); ADR 12 Amendments 2026-10-22 item 4, chosen by the user 2026-10-22; the `toggle` point chosen by the user 2026-10-22; the finding of the input by position settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-22 under ADR 04 Amendments 2026-10-22 item 4 and this row's one-module rule).** Decision 1's `button` row also owns UBT-EVT-09's bridge and the library-internal Enter suppression of the amendment of 2026-10-18 item 4, as options a part turns on (`ButtonOptions`, sent only when set):
- `HiddenInput`: a root click (enabled, not read-only, not vetoed by the consumer through `activate`) is default-prevented and dispatched with its modifiers on the checkbox or radio input the part renders as its next sibling (SWT-PTR-01/02, CHK-PTR-01/02, RAD-PTR-01/02), only while that input is connected (SWT-EDGE-05). The module finds the input by position at click time, past an `<input type="hidden">`, because it has no `id` under `nativeButton`; under a `RenderFunction` callback that assumes the callback renders one top-level element. *Amended 2026-10-23 (design point R9 of M2 tracks 7–8, option (a), settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-23; RAD-EDGE-09):* the input follows the part's element or the callback's top-level element that contains it. When no input follows the element, the module takes the first ancestor whose following siblings, past hidden inputs, begin with a checkbox or radio input; a sibling that is not an input stops the search at its level, so another control's input is never taken. The `composite` module's focus-driven selection finds an item's input the same way (`findHiddenInput`). The input's clicks do not propagate (SWT-PTR-03); a click prevented before the module saw it is ignored (SWT-PTR-11).
- `ReadOnly`: root clicks are ignored and input clicks reverted with `preventDefault()` (SWT-PTR-07).
- `SuppressEnter`: an Enter keydown is prevented and never activates, in either mode and from any target (CHK-KEY-03, RAD-KEY-02). Switch does not set it.
- The toggle reaches C# once per click. Decision 3 gains the decision point `toggle`, fallback **run**, asked inside the input's click: on WebAssembly C# runs the request there and a `stop` (a cancel, or a controlled consumer that did not pass the value back) is the click's `preventDefault()`, so the browser reverts the box in the same dispatch; otherwise the module reports `OnToggle` with the input's new `checked` and the click's snapshot (`ButtonTwin.Toggled`, payload `ButtonToggle`), and C# restores the box through `DomOptions.CheckedRestores`. The `activate` point keeps its role as the consumer's C# veto: on a root click it runs the consumer's declined `onclick`. The `toggle` point replaces the amendment's "decided before the synthetic click … through `activate`" (chosen by the user 2026-10-22), which could not give `details.event` the input's click (SWT-EVT-03) nor cover a label's click.
- The C# half is opt-in members of `ButtonBehavior` (`HiddenInput`, `ReadOnly`, `SuppressEnter`, `ToggleRequest`, `RestoreRequested`), so Switch, Checkbox and Radio share one copy (settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-22 under the M1 track 6 precedent, one behavior for every button-like part).
- Tested by `js/button.test.ts` ("button module: hidden-input bridge", "button module: Enter suppression") and `ButtonTwinContractTests`.

**2026-10-22, the field half of the `form` module (M2 track 1, PR 1a, [issue 77](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/77); the listener ownership settled in M2 planning; the value ownership and the decision point `label` chosen by the user 2026-10-22; the rest settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-22).** Decision 1's `form` row is written as follows for `Field.Control`, `Input` and `Field.Label`. Track 2 adds the submit half.
- **Settled (M2 planning).** The module owns a control's native `input`, `change`, `blur` and `keydown` listeners and reports the value and the validity read in one message per event (ADR 16 rule 6). The focus redirect of a form-participation input (FFI-FOC-05) and the native label lookup (FFI-ALG-08) are the module's. The placement rule puts the browser steps the row does not name here: the label's pointer handling, the active-element read at attach (FFI-STA-09) and the Enter owner-form comparison (FFI-ALG-16). The DOM value after blur (FFI-FRM-32) arrives with PR 1c.
- **Decision 2's ownership list gains a row (chosen by the user 2026-10-22).** `value` and `defaultValue` of `Field.Control` and `Input` are written by the `form` module from attach. C# renders the value once, under the handover rule. The module then sets `defaultValue` from `DefaultValue`, and the live value too while nothing has changed it since the module or the renderer last set it (FFI-EDGE-06/07). For a controlled control the module re-asserts `Value` on `update`, so an edit the consumer does not apply is reverted (FLD-API-15). Evidence: Blazor's renderer (`blazor.webassembly.js` 11.0.0-rc.2) writes `element.value` as a property, never the content attribute, and only when the rendered frame changes. Without this row it could neither revert an unchanged controlled value nor keep a user edit under a changed default nor give a native reset its default. Both facts are confirmed at L4 on both modes on a plain `<input>` (`field.spec.ts`, "platform: the value Blazor renders"): an unchanged frame leaves the user's text, a changed one overwrites it, and `defaultValue` stays empty. An edit made before attach is the user's: the handover hands over the element's state, so when the live value differs from the one C# rendered the module keeps it and reports it as an `input` instead of overwriting it (settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-22; L3, and L4 "keeps and reports an edit made before the control attached" on both modes). FLD-DOM-11's `Writer` becomes `C# → JS` (ADR 13 §3).
- **The Enter commit's 0 ms timer is JavaScript's (settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-22 under ADR 01 decision 3's ordering clause).** On Server a C# timer would race the implicit `submit` report, and C# cannot read the value a later listener wrote. The module therefore schedules the task after an Enter keydown on an `<input>` whose form owner is the enclosing form, and reports the value, the validity and the owner comparison when the task runs. C# commits unless the form's submission counter moved since the keydown report. This reads ADR 01 decision 3's amendment ("ordering against a browser event inside the same task stays JavaScript") as covering this macrotask too.
- **Consumer handlers on the events the module owns (the decline-and-run settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-22 under ADR 02 Amendments 2026-10-21; the decision point `label`, fallback `run`, chosen by the user 2026-10-22).** `Field.Control` and `Input` decline the consumer's `oninput`, `onchange`, `onblur` and `onkeydown` (`ForwardsConsumerHandler`) and run them from the module's report, before their own logic, with arguments built from the report. `PreventPartHandler()` therefore vetoes the part's reaction on both modes with no decision point. The module reports every keydown only while the consumer has an `onkeydown`. `Field.Label`'s pointer handling acts inside the dispatch through the decision point `label`, fallback `run` (the `ButtonBehavior` pattern): a consumer's C# veto is met on WebAssembly and is the existing "C#-origin consumer vetoes" row on Server.
- **Autofocus at attach (settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-22, ADR 02 verification item 1 and ADR 15).** Blazor replaces a prerendered element when the page becomes interactive, which loses the focus the browser's `autofocus` gave it. A probe on both modes found the prerendered input disconnected and `<body>` focused. The module therefore focuses a control rendered with `AutoFocus` once, at attach, unless another element has focus, then reports the active element as before (FLD-FOC-04, INP-API-07). This is React's own `autoFocus` on mount, and it never takes focus the user gave elsewhere.
- **One twin (settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-22, the `DomOptions.Image` precedent).** `FormTwin` has one `FormOptions` with a nullable record per attaching role: `Control` and `Label` here, `Submit` with track 2. A control's sibling hidden input (tracks 4, 5, 7) attaches by id.

**2026-10-22, the composite row of the ownership list and the composite decision points (M2 track 3, [issue 79](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/79); the row and the two decision-point names chosen by the user the same day; the handled-key question answered with option (i), chosen by the user 2026-10-23).**

*Ownership list (Decision 2): the composite row the amendment above announced, in full, in the table form of 2026-10-20 with the "initial value rendered by C#" column of 2026-10-18.*

| Part(s) | JS-owned | Initial value rendered by C# | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| every composite item (`Radio.Root` in a group in M2; the items of ToggleGroup, Toolbar, Tabs, Menubar and NavigationMenu later) | `tabindex` | `"-1"` on every render (CMP-STA-15), so Blazor never writes it again | written by `composite` from `attach`, in the same task as the highlight change: `"0"` on the highlighted item, `"-1"` elsewhere (CMP-DOM-01, CMP-FOC-02); an item whose registration carries a consumer `tabindex` is not written (RAD-DOM-16) |

- C# registers each item by the id its part renders, with the consumer's `tabindex`, in `CompositeOptions.Items`; the module resolves the ids from the root's document or shadow root on `attach` and on every `update`.
- The ownership registry gains `radio` / `Root` / `tabindex` as `C# → JS`, inert until the Radio family exists; RAD §5.1's `Writer` cell changes with track 8.

*Decision points (Decision 3), added. Both fall back to **run**; the names were chosen by the user 2026-10-22:*
- `navigate`: the consumer's `onKeyDown` veto against composite key handling (EVD-EVT-26, CMP-EVT-12, RDG-API-12). The module asks it only where it is about to act, when the candidate index differs from the highlighted one and is in bounds, before it prevents, stops, highlights or focuses.
  - The C# chain is the part's own `NavigationDecider`, then the root consumer's `onkeydown`, which the root part declines (ADR 02 Amendments 2026-10-21) and otherwise runs from its own handler.
  - The one-shot marker that keeps Blazor's later dispatch from running the handler twice is left only when Blazor will dispatch the key: after a veto, or with `stopEventPropagation` off.
- `select`: the focus-driven selection of 2026-10-20 (RAD-FOC-05). The module asks it only where it is about to click the focused item's input. The snapshot's `Target.Id` names the item, so a part can run that item's consumer handler.
- `CompositeTwin.NavigatePoint` and `SelectPoint` hold the names; `NavigationDecider` and `SelectionDecider` are the twin's chains.

*A key the module handles never reaches Blazor's dispatcher (option (i) chosen by the user 2026-10-23).* Blazor dispatches C# handlers from its document-level listener, after the module's listener on the root element (Amendments 2026-10-18, item 3).
- With `stopEventPropagation` (CMP-KEY-15, `true` for a root part, RadioGroup's value), a key the module handles is stopped at the root.
- So no C# `onkeydown` on the root, on an item or on an ancestor runs for that key: on Server not at all, and on WebAssembly only the root's, inside `navigate`.
- Base UI runs the consumer's handlers of the item and the root before the root's handling, and stops only the ancestors'.
- The probe ran on the `composite-list` fixture, both modes, before (i) was built. Its titles now assert (i): `tests/browser/composite.spec.ts` "with stopEventPropagation a handled key runs the item's and the root's C# onkeydown and no ancestor's; an unhandled key runs all three".
- The options went to the user: (i) an opt-in handled-key report with the item's handler routed through the root; (ii) a relaxation row; (iii) a deferred stop. The user chose (i), the recommendation, on 2026-10-23. The module still stops the key at the root, as CMP-KEY-10 states.
  - The root and the items decline the consumer's `onkeydown` and run it themselves.
  - `navigate` runs the focused item's handler, then the root's, on one shared event, so a `PreventPartHandler()` in either stops the key: Base UI's flag lives on the shared synthetic event (`merge-props/mergeProps.ts:210-231`).
  - On Server, `CompositeOptions.ReportHandledKeys`, sent only while the root or an item has such a handler, makes the module report a key it handled and stopped without running the C# chain (`CompositeTwin.KeyHandled`). The two handlers then run a round trip late and cannot veto. ADR 01's row "… EVD-EVT-26 against composite/button key handling …" says so for Server (ADR 01 Amendments 2026-10-23).
  - An ancestor's handler never sees the key, as in Base UI.

**2026-10-23, the `form` module's Enter path and native-label fallback for a box, and the starting-style read of the `transitions` module (M2 track 5, [issue 81](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/81); design points C2, C4 and C6, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-23).**
- **Enter submission on a box's root (C2; CHK-KEY-03..07, FFI-KEY-02, FFI-ALG-10).** Decision 1's `form` row names the Enter-to-submit path. `FormControlOptions.EnterSubmit` turns it on for the handle a box attaches to its root.
  - The module listens in the capture phase at the root, so it runs before the `button` module's Enter suppression (Amendments 2026-10-22) and before any bubbling listener. A disabled root does nothing.
  - It cancels the native default at once, so a native-button root never clicks itself.
  - It records a later `preventDefault()` by shadowing the method on that event.
  - It decides in a window listener added during the dispatch, which runs after every ancestor. A 0 ms task decides instead when an ancestor stopped the propagation.
  - Unless the keydown was prevented before or after it, a microtask clicks the default submitter of the form the hidden input belongs to, when that submitter is not disabled.
  - A JavaScript ancestor therefore observes `defaultPrevented === true` (dated note on CHK-KEY-03). React's synthetic and native pair, which kept it `false`, has no equivalent with one event. A C# ancestor has no `defaultPrevented` (decision 3 of ADR 02).
  - Checkbox §16 Q4 (a consumer veto on keydown toggles the box in Base UI) is fixed, not mirrored: it is an untested quirk with no requirement (Amendments 2026-10-18, quirks) and contradicts CHK-KEY-03's MUST NOT toggle.
- **Native-label fallback (C4; CHK-ARIA-07/08, CHK-DOM-22, CHK-EDGE-13, FFI-ALG-08, FFI-ARIA-06).** `FormControlOptions.LabelFallback` turns the native label lookup of the 2026-10-22 note on for a form-participation input. The part sends it while nothing earlier in CHK-ARIA-06 names the control and the control is not a native button.
  - The module runs `discoverLabel` on `attach`, on every `update` and after every change of the document's labels and ids.
  - One `MutationObserver` per document serves every such input. A Blazor component whose parameters did not change does not render again when a label elsewhere mounts, so "after every render of the root" (Base UI) cannot be the trigger.
  - An id-less label gets `<element id>-label`. The label's id is reported, when it changed, as a `label` report carrying the id in `Value`. Neither payload grew: a new member would have cost every Field options record and every report 8 bytes.
  - C# renders the id as `aria-labelledby`, a round trip after the change on Server (ADR 01's "FFI registration" row).
  - The box's hidden input is now always attached, so its focus goes to the root outside a field too (CHK-FOC-03, FFI-FOC-05).
- **The starting style is styled before it is cleared (C6; CHK-ANI-03, TRN-STA-04).** When the `transitions` module writes `data-starting-style`, or takes over the one C# rendered under the handover rule (Amendments 2026-10-18), it reads the element's computed style once.
  - On a fast Server circuit, the render that creates an element open, its `attach` and the module's clearing frame landed before the frame that first styles the element. No transition ran from the starting style (`checkbox.spec.ts` "triggers enter animation via data-starting-style when mounting", Server).
  - The read gives the element one computed style with the attribute before the clearing frame. TRN-STA-04's "removed on the next frame" is unchanged.
  - Grounds of the settlement: the `collapsible` module already forces a style computation after its closing measurement for the same reason (Amendments 2026-10-22, the `collapsible` row note), and TRN-ALG-01's frame steps stay in JavaScript. The cost is one style read per opening of an animated part.

**2026-10-23, the submit half of the `form` module (M2 track 2, [issue 78](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/78); the interception settled in M2 planning, the rest settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-23).**
- **Settled (M2 planning 2026-10-22, the plan's "Settled by the ADRs" list accepted by the user).** The module prevents every native `submit` of a `Form`'s element. C# runs FRM-EVT-01's steps. When the submission passes and neither `OnFormSubmit` nor the consumer prevents it, the module re-issues it with `requestSubmit(submitter)` behind a one-shot pass flag, which lets that one event through. A submit started during that event is intercepted again.
- **The answer channel (design point F1, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-23; ADR 04 Amendments 2026-10-21, the OnRevealRequest precedent).** A submission reaches C# as the invocation `FormTwin.OnSubmitRequest`, whose result answers it: the `OnRevealRequest` precedent of the 2026-10-21 amendment. The answer is JSON from the source-generated context: `{ verdict: 'submit' | 'blocked' | 'handled', focus }`. `blocked` carries the invalid fields' control ids in registration order. The module focuses the first by document order (registration order across disconnected trees) and calls `select()` on an `<input>`. The post-submission focus (FRM-FOC-02) is an `update` whose `FocusRevision` increases.
- **The controls' state at submit (design point F2, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-23; ADR 16 rule 6, DP6's answer).** Each control handle enrols at attach on its form's record in the module's token registry (next item). The submit report carries the value and native validity the module reads from every enrolled control in the submit task, one message per submission (ADR 16 rule 6). C# applies each state to its field through the registration's `ApplyState` before validating (FFI-FRM-04, FFI-EDGE-07).
- **The form's handle (design point F6, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-23; FRM-DOM-02, ADR 04 §3's one-way contract, ADR 02 decision 1 for the substitute).** The form draws a token it never renders (`IFormScope.FormToken`, drawn with `PartIdGenerator`). Its attach registers its element under the token in the module's one module-level `Map<token, { element, controls }>`, and clears it at dispose. A control carries the same token as `FormControlOptions.FormId`, enrols by it whichever attaches first, and resolves its owner form at each use. The form renders no attribute (FRM-DOM-02 as written). A substituted form with no element reference attaches by a generated id it renders (port note on FRM-DOM-02). Not chosen: Blazor's `_bl_` capture attribute, which nothing pins; an id on every form; the nearest ancestor `<form>`, which is wrong for portaled fields.

**2026-10-24, a native-button checkable control reads its hidden input at attach (M2 track 4 follow-up, [PR 95](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/95), the `form` row; decided on evidence as the orchestrator asked, 2026-10-24).** The state the `form` module reads from each enrolled element when a form submits reaches a checkable control's validation input by the hidden input's id (M2 track 6's submit-state sink, FFI-FRM-04's port note of 2026-10-23). A control rendered as a native button renders no id on its hidden input, so the sink cannot reach it, and the field validates it at submit from the input's last report. `CheckableFieldBehavior` therefore asks such an input for one report when its handle attaches inside a field, beside the reports that follow every change. A required, unchecked native-button switch is then `valueMissing` at its first submit (SWT-FRM-08). An input with an id is left to the sink. The evidence is in `SwitchFieldTests`: "a required switch is invalid at its first submit, before any toggle" is green on the sink alone, and "a required native-button switch is invalid at its first submit, before any toggle" is red without the attach-time read.

**2026-10-26, the M3 composite extensions, the `tabs` module's activation, composite button mode and a precision on option (i) (M3 planning, plan of 2026-10-25; chosen by the user 2026-10-26; the rows and notes are written in full by the implementing pull requests).**
- *Composite extensions (track 1), options and reports of the `composite` module, each a dated note on the composite row when it lands:* a registration-only mode that installs no listeners and writes no `tabindex` and reports the items' indexes to C# (`TabsPanel`, `AccordionItem`; CMP-STR-03); an ordered-ids report on every publication that changed the set or the order, ids only, C# joining them to its own registrations (`TabsList`, `ToolbarRoot`; CMP-EVT-03; the values never cross to JavaScript); per-item `disabled` and `focusableWhenDisabled` flags on the registration, from which JavaScript derives `disabledIndices` (`ToolbarRoot`; CMP-STA-08, CMP-STA-29, CMP-KEY-08, TLB-STA-02; ADR 01 decision 2 keeps the indices in JavaScript); the outer registration wins when two parts register one element, as a C# rule in `CompositeRootBehavior` (the first registration per id; parameters are set parent before child) with the inner part's bag `tabindex` ignored for that item (`Toolbar.Button` substituting a `Toggle`; CMP-STR-04); a highlight that follows the item marked active when focus is outside the root and the item is not disabled, except once after a keydown on that item (`TabsList`; CMP-STA-22, CMP-EDGE-09, TAB-FOC-04..07). The option, payload and report names are track 1's.
- *The `button` row gains an option for `Toolbar.Input`* (UBT-DOM-12): the focusable-when-disabled attribute resolution with the UBT-KEY-20 key prevention and the disabled press suppression, without Enter or Space activation and without `type` or `role`; written by the Toolbar track.
- *Tabs (track 5).* The `tabs` component module owns the Indicator's measurement over the `dom` module's `ResizeObserver` subscription (§1), and the activation: it listens for a Tab's click and focus (tracking the pressing state of TAB-PTR-05 and TAB-PTR-06), measures the two Tab rects, and asks the decision point **`select`** on the `tabs` twin, fallback **run**. The name is the composite's: `select` names the point where a module is about to select the item a user gesture points at, and a point's name is per twin. On WebAssembly C# runs the declined consumer `onclick` or `onfocus` and the request inside the dispatch; on Server the module reports the Tab's id, the `activationDirection` and the event, and C# runs the request a round trip later (the "C#-origin consumer vetoes" row of ADR 01, for TAB-PTR-09). Decision 2's ownership list gains `data-activation-direction` on `Tabs.Root`, `Tabs.List`, `Tabs.Tab`, `Tabs.Panel` and `Tabs.Indicator`: JavaScript's from attach, written when the applied batch changes the active Tab (a `MutationObserver` on `data-active`, the `openSelector` precedent, pre-paint on both modes), C# mirroring `TabActivationDirection` (the RND-STA-01 row). One `TabsTwin` on the List owns the observer and the direction; one per Indicator writes that Indicator's `hidden` and `--active-tab-*` (§2's row), reading the active Tab from `[data-active]` in the applied batch. The alternatives, C# handlers with mirrored Tab positions or an awaited measurement, were rejected for staleness and for a second Server round trip per click.
- *Composite button mode* (UBT-STA-01): `Toggle` infers it from the optional `CompositeRootContext` cascade, so a bare Toggle under `Toolbar.Root` behaves as TGL-EDGE-09 states; `ToolbarButton` is always composite; `AccordionTrigger` and the M1 and M2 button-like parts stay explicit `false`, with a dated note on UBT-STA-01. A follow-up issue tracks the retrofit of Switch, Checkbox, Button and CollapsibleTrigger; its evidence is an L4 title "Space on a Switch substituted into a toolbar button activates on keydown".
  - *2026-11-26 (M6 track 10, issues 238 and 107; ADR 12 Amendments 2026-11-18 item 1, track 10's scope, chosen by the user 2026-11-18):* the retrofit is done. `Button`, `SwitchRoot`, `CheckboxRoot` and `CollapsibleTrigger` infer the mode from the optional `CompositeRootContext` cascade; `RadioRoot` and `AccordionTrigger` stay explicit `false`. The evidence is at L2, one red title per part (UBT-STA-01's note of this date), and at L4 over a fixture case chosen by the user 2026-11-26: substituted into a `ToolbarButton`, the Switch's Space already activated on keydown through the toolbar button's own composite handle, so the title named above would pass on the base; the user chose to add a case with the Switch directly under the toolbar root, where its own handle is the only one. `toolbar.spec.ts` "port: Space on a Switch directly under a toolbar root activates on keydown (issue 107)" is red on the base on both modes and green with the retrofit. The Toolbar `Switch` block stays one click per press: the first handle marks the keydown activated and the other skips it.
- *Option (i), a precision.* The 2026-10-22 note's "an ancestor's handler never sees the key, as in Base UI" holds for ancestors of the root. Base UI also runs the handlers of elements between the item and the root (a `Toolbar.Group`, a ToggleGroup inside a toolbar, a consumer wrapper) before the root stops propagation; in the port a C# `onkeydown` on those never runs for a handled key, while a JavaScript listener does. The implementing pull requests record it on TGG-DOM-06 and TLB-API-16; ADR 01 Amendments 2026-10-26 carries the row text.
- *`Accordion.Panel`* in §2's row "`Collapsible.Panel`, `Accordion.Panel` | `hidden="until-found"`, measured `--collapsible-panel-height/width`": for `Accordion.Panel` the variables are `--accordion-panel-height/width` (ACC-DOM-17); the `collapsible` module takes the names as an option (track 6).

**2026-10-26, the `collapsible` row takes Accordion's names and rules (M3 track 6, [issue 105](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/105); the `Accordion.Panel` item of the note above).** The module and `CollapsiblePanelBehavior` take three inputs whose defaults are Collapsible's, so a `Collapsible.Panel` sends and renders what it did before:
- *The size variables* (ACC-DOM-17; the names chosen by the user 2026-10-26, the shape mine): `CollapsibleOptions.HeightVariable` and `WidthVariable` (`heightVariable`, `widthVariable`), read when the module starts on an element; `null`, the default, is omitted from the JSON and means `--collapsible-panel-height/width`. The behaviour takes an internal `CollapsiblePanelVariables` (`Collapsible` or `Accordion`) and composes its `auto` style from it. The Collapsible names are never set on an Accordion panel.
- *The empty id* (ACC-API-18, accordion §16 Q2 following the source; settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-25): `CollapsiblePanelBehavior.RendersEmptyId`. Under it `id=""` is rendered as written and registers nothing, so the triggers' `aria-controls` names the generated panel id; a `Collapsible.Panel` keeps COL-API-10.
- *The messages* (ACC-EDGE-04/05, TRN-EDGE-04; settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-25): the caller chooses the KeepMounted message (`CollapsiblePanelBehavior.KeepMountedIgnoredMessage`); `CollapsibleDevelopmentLog` gains `AccordionRootKeepMountedIgnoredMessage` and `AccordionPanelKeepMountedIgnoredMessage`, worded as collapsible §16 item 17 worded Collapsible's. The mixed-motion message already names accordion panels (ACC-EDGE-06).
- *The status report* (ACC-STA-03/09, ACC-EDGE-14; the report settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-25; its shape, design point D1 of track 6, and where the opt-in lives, design point D5, both option (a), settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-26): an Accordion Item has no root twin, so nothing moves its shared status off `Starting` after an open, or to the unset status after a close. `CollapsibleOptions.ReportStatus` (`reportStatus`; `null`, the default, omitted from the JSON) asks the module for `OnPanelStatusChange` (`PanelStatusChange(Status)`, `BlazixJsonContext`), the machine's status before the forced idle, sent once per change. It is never sent for the unset status, which `OnTransitionComplete` implies, nor for the `starting` the evaluation step gives a panel that was not mounted, which C# computes in the same render; the `starting` of a reopen during an exit is sent. `CollapsibleTwin.StatusChanged` reaches `CollapsibleRootContext.MirrorStatus`. The opt-in is the shared state's `CollapsibleRootContext.PanelReportsStatus`, which an Accordion Item sets and every panel behaviour reads, so a `Collapsible.Panel` inside an Item reports too; under a `Collapsible.Root` it is never set and Collapsible's JSON is unchanged. Cost on Server: about two messages per open and close of a panel (`idle`, `ending`); the lag is the RND-STA-01 row (ACC §9 lists it for ACC-STA-03).
Tested by `js/collapsible.test.ts` ("writes the size variables the options name and never the collapsible ones"; "the status report" in both fake-twin settings) and `tests/Blazix.Primitives.Tests.Components/Accordion/AccordionPanelBehaviorTests.cs`; Collapsible's suites are unchanged and green.

**2026-10-26, the composite row: the M3 extensions as they land (M3 track 1, [issue 100](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/100); the five extensions chosen by the user 2026-10-26, the first bullet of the note above).** The composite row of Amendments 2026-10-22 gains one note per extension, dated when it lands:
- *E3, the items' flags (2026-10-26; chosen by the user 2026-10-26).* `CompositeItemRegistration(Id, TabIndex, Disabled = false, FocusableWhenDisabled = false)`: the item's part sets both flags on every parameter set, `CompositeItemBehavior.SetRegistration(id, attributes, disabled, focusableWhenDisabled)` compares them, so a change sends one `update`, and each flag is sent only when set. The root option `CompositeOptions.DeriveDisabledIndices` (`CompositeRootBehavior.DeriveDisabledIndices`, sent only when set) makes the module derive the indices of the items that are disabled and not focusable when disabled, in its own order, in the same pass as each publication and on each `update` that changes a flag (`ToolbarRoot.tsx:32-46`, one pass). The derived set replaces `disabledIndices` for the first publication's adjustment (CMP-STA-24), the key resolution (CMP-KEY-08) and the re-validation (CMP-STA-29), which also runs after each publication, as Base UI's effect does on its new derived array. Without the option the flags travel and nothing is derived: TabsList keeps `DisabledIndices = []` (TAB-KEY-07), RadioGroup and ToggleGroup keep `null`. No new report and no new decision point. On Server the flags are `update`-carried (ADR 01's row "JS-owned values that originate in C# state").
- *E4, the outer registration wins (2026-10-26; chosen by the user 2026-10-26).* A C# rule in `CompositeRootBehavior`: it sends the first registration per id, and Blazor sets parameters parent before child, so the outer item's (Base UI's outer item attaches its ref last, and the last registration wins: `CompositeItem.tsx:28-31`, CMP-STR-04). The inner item's registration, its flags and the `tabindex="-1"` the outer item put in its bag are never sent for that id; the items version moves only for a registration that is or was the first for its id, so a change to the inner item alone sends nothing, and the inner item's registration takes over when the outer one is removed. The module is unchanged (it resolves one registration per id). Option (i)'s key path is unchanged: `FindItem` returns the first item, so the outer item's consumer `onkeydown` runs once, inside `navigate` on WebAssembly and from the handled-key report on Server.
- *E2, the items report (2026-10-27; chosen by the user 2026-10-26; its shape and ordering settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-26: design point 1 (a), one ordered-ids report for E1 and E2, by ADR 16 rule 6, about 30 bytes per item per report; design point 3 (a), each report a self-contained sequence joined when it arrives, by ADR 01 decision 4 and CMP-STA-10's row).* The root option `CompositeOptions.ReportItems` (`CompositeRootBehavior.ReportItems`, sent only when set; TabsList, and ToolbarRoot if track 4 needs it) makes the module report `OnItemsChange` with the payload `CompositeItemsChange(IReadOnlyList<string> Ids)`, the registered items' ids in index order, on each publication whose id sequence changed: the first in `attach`, one in `update`, or an observed move (CMP-STA-11). Its listener subscribes before the root's adjustment, so the report precedes the same publication's highlight report (CMP-EVT-03, `CompositeRoot.tsx:88-91`). Ids only: values stay in C#. `CompositeTwin.ItemsChanged` carries the ids; `CompositeRootBehavior` joins them to its registrations (the first per id; an unknown id is ignored; an item removed since is dropped) as `OrderedItems` and raises `ItemsChanged` once per report, before or after the handle exists. A report during an `update` re-renders the part and sends nothing more. RadioGroup and ToggleGroup do not ask and pay nothing (ADR 16 rule 6).
- *E1, the registration-only list (2026-10-27; chosen by the user 2026-10-26; settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-26: design points 1 and 3 as for E2; design point 2 (a), every list item renders the consumer's id or a generated one, by this ADR's notes of 2026-10-22 and 2026-10-26 (items resolved by id); design point 6 (a), a separate `CompositeListContext`, so a Toggle inside a TabsPanel does not infer composite mode and a bare list item does not pass `Require`; the module-mode and class choices accepted by the orchestrator; design point 6b, the two untested nearest-registry quirks not replicated, chosen by the user 2026-10-27 under the note of 2026-10-18).* `CompositeOptions.RegistrationOnly` (sent only when set), read once at `attach`, on the one `CompositeTwin`: the module keeps the registry (move detection, shadow-root items) and always sends the E2 report, and installs no `keydown`, `focusin`, `focusout` or `mousemove` listener, writes no `tabindex`, keeps no highlight, reads no marker and asks no decision point. C#: `CompositeListBehavior` on the list root (TabsRoot, AccordionRoot), attached through `Element`, the generated id or a late id; `CompositeListItemBehavior` on each item (TabsPanel, AccordionItem), whose `Index` is -1 until the first report and in prerender, then the item's position in the report, and whose part re-renders only when it changes; `CompositeListContext`, a fixed cascade separate from `CompositeRootContext`. An item without a list is inert (CMP-STR-03). Every list item renders an id. The composite row's note gains: an item of a registration-only list is not written.
- *E5, the active-item follow (2026-10-27; chosen by the user 2026-10-26; settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-26: design point 4 (a), a `MutationObserver`, the mechanism the user chose for Tabs' `data-activation-direction` the same day; design point 5 (a), `aria-disabled="true"` read from the applied batch, by ADR 01 decision 1 (Server is the floor) and this ADR's handover precedent).* `CompositeOptions.FollowActiveItem` (`CompositeRootBehavior.FollowActiveItem`, sent only when set; TabsList only): each item is evaluated as `TabsTab.tsx:83-109`'s effect is, when its marker, its index, the highlight or its `disabled` flag changed; a keydown that reached the item since (the capture-phase mark of `isNavigatingRef`) only clears the mark; otherwise a marked item whose index differs from the highlight takes it, without scrolling, in the same task as the tab stop, when focus is outside the root (across shadow roots) and it is not `aria-disabled="true"` in the applied batch (a disabled Tab renders that, TAB-DOM-10; a consumer's `aria-disabled="true"` on an enabled Tab also blocks the follow, a dated divergence on CMP-STA-22). A change of the marker or of an item's `aria-disabled` is observed by a `MutationObserver` on the root (`attributeFilter` both, `subtree`), installed only with the option: pre-paint on both modes (ADR 01 decision 4), the `openSelector` precedent. E5 does not read E3's flags. The re-validation against `disabledIndices` does not run with the option (CMP-EDGE-09). No new decision point; `navigate` and `select` stay the module's two.

**2026-10-27, the `tabs` module as built (M3 track 5, [issue 104](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/104); the 2026-10-26 Tabs bullet written in full; the design points of the Tabs track it names were settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-26, each as the track recommended).**
- *§1's component row: the `tabs` module.* `js/tabs.ts` (entry `js/tabs`) imports `dom`, `twin`, `decide` and `event-info`, and no other component module. It has two handles, each marked on its element with a symbol as `collapsible.ts` does, so `attach` on an attached element returns the live handle and `dispose` is idempotent: the List's (`TabsOptions.Part` `"list"`) and one per Indicator (`"indicator"`). The List's options are `Orientation`, `ActivateOnFocus`, `Controlled` and the two report flags; the Indicator's are a consumer's `Hidden` and `TwinOptions.Style`. A handle reads the parts it owns as those whose nearest List handle is its own, so a Tabs nested inside another List's Tab stays out (design point 4).
- *The activation and `select`.* The List handle listens for `click`, `focusin` and `pointerdown` on its Tabs and does nothing for a click whose `button` is not 0, for a disabled or active Tab, or for a focus without `ActivateOnFocus` or under a held non-primary press (TAB-PTR-02..07). Otherwise it measures the two Tabs and asks **`select`**, fallback **run**. On WebAssembly it first hands the Tab's id and the measured direction over through the synchronous invocation `TabsTwin.OnSelecting`, which the decider reads. The module reads the channel's mode from the `twin.ts` channel's `readonly synchronous`, which uses the probe `decide` uses (design point 6, option (b)); `decide` and `Decide` are unchanged, so decision 3's contract stands, and the stash moves no state (ADR 01 decision 1). On Server it reports `OnSelect` with the Tab's id, the direction and the event. A click or focus that activates nothing is reported (`OnTabEvent`) only while a consumer handler for it exists, so a Tab renders no `onclick` or `onfocus` and C# runs the consumer's handler before the request (design point 5, option (b), on ADR 16 rule 6 and the `ReportHandledKeys` precedent of option (i); it also sidesteps issue 87).
- *Decision 2's ownership list: `data-activation-direction`.* A `MutationObserver` on the Root (on the List without one) reads each applied batch: `data-active`, `role` and `data-disabled` changes and child lists. It finds the active Tab and then the direction: the measured one when the change matches the pending activation, `none` in an uncontrolled Root or when the old Tab left the document, and positional otherwise (design point 7, option (a′)). It writes the attribute on the Root, the List, its Tabs, the Root's own Panels and the Indicators, and reports `OnDirectionChange` once per change for C#'s mirror. Because the Panel the batch creates is already in the DOM the callback reads, its first open render carries the new direction (TAB-STA-14).
- *§2's `Tabs.Indicator` row.* The Indicator handle measures per TAB-DOM-25..29 with `getCssDimensions` and `getElementTransform` on attach, on each batch the List handle reads, and on each resize notification of the List and its Tabs. The `dom` module's `observeResize` subscriptions are re-synchronised per batch, so Tabs inserted or swapped later are observed. It writes `style.cssText` as the six variables followed by the consumer's string, whose declarations win (TAB-DOM-32), and `hidden` unless both sizes are positive or a consumer `hidden` was given. It reports `OnMeasure` once per change (design point 2, option (a)). C# renders the initial `hidden` and style under the handover and freezes both from attach.
- *The Panel* runs over `TransitionsTwin` with `OpenSelector = ":not([inert])"` (design point 1, option (b)).
- Tested by `js/tabs.test.ts` (L3), `TabsTwinContractTests` (L2) and `tests/browser/tabs.spec.ts` (L4, both modes).
