**2026-10-27, the input item on the `button` row (M3 track 4, [issue 103](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/103); the option of the 2026-10-26 note above, chosen by the user 2026-10-26; its name, the writer variant and the `ButtonBehavior` mode are the Toolbar track's, the mode settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27 as design point 14.5 (a)).** Decision 1's `button` row also owns UBT-DOM-12's resolution for `Toolbar.Input`, as an option a part turns on (`ButtonOptions.InputItem`, `inputItem`, sent only when set):
- `InputItem`: no key activates the element and a Space keydown is never prevented, so a space is typed (the Enter and Space activation and the composite Space prevention are skipped, on keydown and keyup). A disabled, focusable input keeps UBT-KEY-20's prevention of every key but Tab, without stopping propagation, and the disabled `click` and `pointerdown` prevention (TLB-KEY-13, TLB-PTR-02). The part sends `native: false` and `composite: true`, so the native-mode check reports nothing for an `<input>`.
- The attributes are `ButtonAttributeWriter.WriteInputItem`: `aria-disabled="true"` while disabled and nothing else (TLB-DOM-07, TLB-ARIA-04). `ToolbarInput` calls it on the merged attributes, so it wins over the consumer's while disabled (TLB-DOM-11).
- The C# half is `ButtonBehavior.InputItem`, which also declines none of the consumer's handlers: Base UI merges a toolbar input's handlers after its own and runs them while disabled (TLB-API-14), and the module's listeners run before Blazor's dispatch.
- Tested by `js/button.test.ts` ("button module: input item"), `ButtonAttributeWriterTests` and `ButtonTwinContractTests`.

**2026-10-27, the M4 popup modules (M4 planning, plan of 2026-10-27; chosen by the user the same day; the rows, options and notes are written in full by the implementing pull requests, which name the option, payload and report fields).**
- *Decision 3: the token form of `attach` and the per-popup session (track 3, `interactions`).* A popup Root renders no element, and Base UI's root store holds the per-popup data the modules share (`dataRef`, the event bus, the tree node, the hover state). The Root draws a session token with `PartIdGenerator`, never rendered, and cascades it to its parts; `attach` gains a token form for an elementless handle (a Root, a `TooltipProvider`, a floating tree), beside the element and id forms of §3. The `interactions` module keeps the one token-keyed session map; the `form` module's `Map<token, …>` of Amendments 2026-10-23 is the precedent, and §3's "no module-level `WeakMap` registries" is read as it was there: one keyed map per module whose entries a handle creates and its dispose clears, with a test reset. `positioning` and `focus-manager` look a session up through the `interactions` export; every handle's options carry the token. The alternatives were one composing `PopupTwin` per Root over exported factories (which adds a composer without removing the Positioner's, Popup's and Trigger's own handles) and linkage discovered in the DOM at event time (which the portal defeats, the reason Base UI has a tree).
- *The open-change relay.* C# relays the last committed open change in `update` (a revision counter, the reason, the event's kind and type, the pointer type, the trigger id); each module emits its internal `openchange` when the revision moves. FLT-EVT-02's "before the consumer's `onOpenChange` callback runs" is the order of Base UI's root store alone; every M4 popup store calls the consumer first (ADR 05 §2), so track 3 records the fidelity correction on FLT-EVT-02 with a line in `_qa-notes.md`. On Server the modules' listeners run a round trip after the commit (ADR 01 Amendments 2026-10-27).
  - *The record as built (M4 track 1, [issue 117](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/117), 2026-10-27; the record is track 1's, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27).* `internal sealed record OpenChangeRelay(int Revision, string Reason, EventKind EventKind, string EventType, string? PointerType, string? TriggerId)` in `Interop/`, built at commit by `OpenChangeRelay.From(revision, reason, snapshot, triggerId)`, the reason as its literal; listed in `BlazixJsonContext`. The open state travels in each module's own options and `nested` stays the module's, from its session tree, as the shared names below settle. JSON: `{"revision":3,"reason":"trigger-press","eventKind":"PointerEvent","eventType":"click","pointerType":"mouse","triggerId":"base-ui-5"}`. Tested by `OpenChangeRelayTests` (L1).
- *Sanctioned page-global JavaScript state.* The scroll-lock count, the outside-marking counters, the focus stack, the queued focus and the delay groups are page-global in Base UI and stay page-global in the modules, each named in `js/CONTEXT.md` with a test reset; nothing else lives outside a handle or a session.
- *Decision 1's table gains the `popup` mechanism module (track 5, Popover):* `moduleKinds` gains `popup` and `BlazixJsEntry` gains `js/popup.ts`.

| Module | Owns | Doc |
|---|---|---|
| `popup` (mechanism) | PSH's JavaScript: the Viewport's snapshots and `[data-previous]` clone, the auto-resize measurement, the internal backdrop's cut-out, the anchored scroll lock's decision, the trigger focus guards' focus handling (settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27: the `popup` module's, importing `focus-manager`'s `tabbable` export for the destination of PSH-KEY-02/03; the focus manager listens on the inside guards, the `portal` module on the outside guards) | PSH |

- *Decision 3: the decision point `press` (track 3; the Tabs `select` precedent of Amendments 2026-10-26).* The `interactions` trigger handle decides the next open value on click (FLT-ALG-16: toggle, stick-if-open, the touch delay) and asks `press` on the `interactions` twin, fallback `run`. On WebAssembly C# runs the consumer's declined `onclick` and the open request inside the dispatch; on Server the module reports the press with its snapshot and interaction type and C# runs the request a round trip later (the "C#-origin consumer vetoes" row of ADR 01). The stick-if-open window is a module timer whose reading C# never needs; the `button` module's `activate` stays the consumer's veto of the click itself. The alternative, a C# `onclick` request on `PopoverTrigger` and `DialogTrigger` (the Collapsible shape), would have put FLT-ALG-16 in C# for M4 and in JavaScript for M5's `mousedown` paths (ADR 01 decision 2).
- *Decision 3: the decision point `guard` (track 5).* The trigger focus guards' close is asked on the guard's `focus` through `guard`, fallback `run`, on the twin that owns the guards' listeners, so PSH-KEY-04 holds on WebAssembly; PSH-KEY-07 keeps its ADR 01 row on Server.
- *Decision 3: the verdict `Prevent` (track 3, in its own commit with the contract tests of `DecisionPolicy`, `decide.ts` and `twin.ts`).* `DecisionPolicy` gains `Prevent` (literal `prevent`). The Escape point's C# chain returns `PreventAndStop` (accepted), `Prevent` (accepted with `AllowPropagation()`), `Stop` (cancelled) or `Run` (cancelled and propagation allowed). The module decides "innermost" from the session tree before asking and applies the fallback on Server only then; it marks the keydown with a symbol so the same session's document listener skips it: one close request per keydown (FLT §10 Q11, POP §16 Q7). The alternatives were point-specific meanings for `Run` and `Stop` (one literal, two meanings) and two synchronous invokes per keydown.
- *Decision 2: the layered-style writer (track 2, in `dom`).* Where `positioning`, `transitions` and the Viewport's auto-resize write one element's `style`, one primitive writes the element's whole `style` from the consumer's string plus named layers in a fixed order (positioning, transitions, auto-resize), so one writer per property holds where three modules write one element. C# renders everything Base UI renders before the first positioning pass (FLT-DOM-02's `position: fixed; top: 0; left: 0; opacity: 0`, the seeded variables, the requested `data-side` and `data-align`, `pointer-events: none` while closed, `transition: none` while starting) and freezes it at attach (`FinishAttributes`, the 2026-10-18 handover).
- *Decision 2's ownership list gains the M4 rows*, written in full by the implementing pull requests with their `Writer` cells: every Positioner's `style`, `data-side`, `data-align` (C# → JS, mirrored as `Side?` and `Align?`) and `data-anchor-hidden`; every Arrow's `style`, `data-side`, `data-align` and `data-uncentered`; `--popup-width`, `--popup-height` and the internal backdrop's `clip-path` (JavaScript's); `data-instant` (C#'s, derived on commit from the applied reason and the group's phase; per-family enums, ADR 08 Amendments 2026-10-27); `--nested-dialogs` (C#'s, written in the commit path, both modes); the page-level writes (the scroll lock's `<html>` and `<body>` styles, the outside marking's `aria-hidden`, `inert` and `data-tabindex` stash) recorded in `js/CONTEXT.md`, the composite's `tabindex` taking precedence over a non-modal portal's stash on its items; the VoiceOver `role="button"` swap on a focus guard as a JavaScript write after attach; Writer tables added for the Arrow, Backdrop and Viewport parts of Popover, Tooltip and PreviewCard and for the portal node of Dialog.
  - *As built for the scroll lock (M4 track 1, [issue 117](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/117), 2026-10-27; placed as a sub-bullet by the orchestrator 2026-10-28).* JavaScript writes, never C#'s, every value restored on unlock: `overflow-x` and `overflow-y` on the viewport scroller, `<html>` when it is an overflow element and `<body>` otherwise; on the gutter path `scrollbar-gutter` on `<html>`; on the full path `scrollbar-gutter`, `overflow-x/y` and `scroll-behavior` on `<html>`, `position`, `height`, `width`, `box-sizing`, `overflow-x/y` and `scroll-behavior` on `<body>`, the scroll offsets moved to `<body>` and back, and the attribute `data-base-ui-scroll-locked` on `<html>`.
- *Decision 4's "floating-ui is a chunk" is corrected ([issue 16](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/16); track 2).* The vendored dist files carry no legal comment (ADR 07 Context) and, with one importer, the engine is bundled into `js/positioning.js`. A port-owned wrapper `js/floating-ui.ts`, excluded from `moduleKinds` as `test-support/` is, carries a `/*! … */` legal comment naming Floating UI's packages, versions, licence and copyright and re-exports the engine functions ADR 03 names together with the `@floating-ui/utils` helpers Base UI's positioning layer imports; only `positioning` imports it, and the module-graph lint gains the exclusion of this top-level wrapper and accepts its import from `positioning` alone (corrected 2026-10-27 by the track 2 brief's probe: the eight functions are not enough). The other modules keep re-implementing the `@floating-ui/utils/dom` predicates in `dom.ts`, so no shared chunk exists. `.github/scripts/assert-package.mjs` checks permanently that the packed `wwwroot/js/positioning.js` carries the banner, the output the module-graph lint pins as the engine's only importer (ADR 07 Amendments 2026-10-27; the locator settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27, because minification renames `computePosition` and the earlier wording matched nothing). Amended 2026-10-28: the locator follows the engine into a shared chunk through the build manifest's inputs once a second entry imports `positioning` (ADR 07 Amendments 2026-10-28). The L4 setup project warms the positioning entry so the first positioned title does not pay its fetch.
- *Decision 5: the container and the portal's shape (track 1; verification item 3, [issue 8](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/8)).* `Container` is a nullable hand-written `[Union] PortalContainerValue` (ADR 08 Amendments 2026-10-27) with the cases `ElementReference`, `string` (a selector), `IJSObjectReference` (any node a consumer's script holds, which covers Dialog's closed-shadow-root titles) and `Pending` (Base UI's `null`: render nothing until a container arrives); `null` means not supplied: the enclosing portal node, else `<body>`. The Portal renders an in-place wrapper element (`display: contents`) whose only child is the portal node; the outside focus guards and the `aria-owns` owner are the wrapper's siblings, rendered through `BuildBeforeElement` and `BuildAfterElement` outside the pipeline's opt-in host wrapper (ADR 02 Amendments 2026-10-27; the host wrapper settled by the orchestrator the same day); the module moves the node out of the wrapper after the first render and back before dispose, so Blazor's `insertBefore` and `removeChild` never meet a foreign parent. The wrapper is one element Base UI's DOM does not have (a dated note on FLT-DOM-11). The permanent L4 test, `@verifies ADR-04/3`, toggles siblings before and after the Portal, re-renders the portaled content and disposes the Portal, on both modes. A `defaultOpen` popup prerenders its portal node in place, `display: none` until the module moves it (§5 as written). The alternative, no wrapper unless the test fails, was rejected because the old port's proven shape is the wrapper.
- *Hover timers and the delay group (track 3; ADR 01 Amendments 2026-10-27).* Every hover, rest, close, intent and group timer runs in JavaScript and reports a request when it fires. `TooltipProvider` renders no element, draws a provider token and cascades it; the delay group is an object in `interactions` keyed by that token; a member's open closes the previous member through that tooltip's twin with reason `none` (FLT-EVT-07). The open request carries the group's instant phase, so C# renders `data-instant="delay"` in the batch that renders `data-open`, met on both modes. The Popup-leave close delay reads the Trigger's `closeDelay ?? 0` (Amendments 2026-10-18).
- *The scroll lock, the focus manager's open input and nested dialogs (tracks 1, 4 and 8).* The `scroll-lock` twin takes `Enabled` and the reference element and keeps the page-global count in JavaScript, the 0 ms deferral and the takeover observer included (UTL §4.8). The focus manager reads the open state from the applied batch through a `MutationObserver` on the attached popup's `[data-open]` (the `openSelector` precedent), so releases, re-marking and the trap's bookkeeping land pre-paint on both modes. For a popup element the opening render creates, the handles attach from `OnAfterRender`, and the Dialog track measures the Server gap (ADR 01 Amendments 2026-10-27). The nested-dialog count is written in the commit path; the topmost gate is the `interactions` session's own stack; a module disarms itself after requesting a close until the next open arrives. The alternatives were module-level observers acting on inserted portal nodes (options read from attributes Base UI does not render) and `update` alone (every lock, marking and focus a round trip late on Server).
- *The anchor, the offsets, the collision options and the twins' options (track 2).* `AnchorValue` (ADR 08 Amendments 2026-10-27) has an element case, an id case and a virtual-rect case re-sent on change, and no function case: PreviewCard's hovered line and Tooltip's cursor are JavaScript's end to end (Amendments 2026-10-18). The offsets are a number or a declarative record evaluated by the module on every pass. The Arrow publishes its id through the positioner's context, and its `uncentered`, `side` and `align` mirror arrives in the Positioner's report. Every twin's options record has value equality, so an `update` is sent only on change (§3's one message per state change).
- *Dialog's `outsidePress` predicates* are data options of the `interactions` dismissal handle (`PrimaryButtonOnly`, `SingleTouch`, `OwnBackdropIds`, `Topmost`), evaluated in JavaScript; built by track 3 under those names and proven at L3, track 8 renaming by a dated note only on evidence (settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27, so the module keeps one owner in wave 1).
- *Focus targets.* `InitialFocus` and `FinalFocus` are resolved in C# when the open or close commits (a value union or a delegate over the interaction type, ADR 08 Amendments 2026-10-27) and sent resolved to the focus manager; the close type is classified in C# from the request's snapshot, with the module's recorded pointer type in its report.
- *Untested Base UI quirks (the 2026-10-18 note extended to Popover, Tooltip and PreviewCard).* The port does not emit the spurious hover-leave close (it cancels the pending open only) and replicates the stale `instantType`; both are reported upstream; one dated note per family in the implementing pull requests. Tooltip, 2026-10-28 (M4 track 6, [issue 121](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/121)): the stale `instantType` is replicated by `TooltipStore` (tooltip.md §16 Q13) and the spurious hover-leave close is not emitted (§16 Q14); a third quirk, provider-less tooltips sharing Base UI's default delay group, is not replicated, since TIP-STA-08 states that without a Provider the instant phase is always false (design point 13.5; TIP-STA-08's dated note); reported upstream.
- *Element-typed option values cross as interop arguments (settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27 under §3's contract and the `attach` precedent; raised by the track 2 brief; the shared-path edit is track 1's, in its own commit with the contract tests of `TwinHandle`, `TwinBase` and `twin.ts`).* §3's options travel as one JSON string written by the source-generated context (ADR 16), so an `ElementReference` or an `IJSObjectReference` inside it never reaches the module as an element, while the element of `attach` does, because Blazor revives interop arguments. `TwinBase.AttachAsync` and `TwinHandle.UpdateAsync` therefore take an optional array of references passed after the options string (`attach(elementOrId, options, self, references)`, `update(options, references)`), which the module receives revived; an element-typed option value serializes as the index of its reference (the worker names the field), `null` when absent. This carries `PortalContainerValue`'s element and `IJSObjectReference` cases (track 1), `AnchorValue`'s and `CollisionBoundaryValue`'s element cases (track 2) and `FocusTargetValue`'s element case (track 4); tracks 2 and 4 list those cases' rows open until track 1's transport is on `main`, their id cases needing nothing. The alternatives, a `dom` module registry handing out tokens for elements (a module-level registry and a round trip per element) and options passed as objects (reflection-based serialization, against ADR 16), were rejected.
  - *As built (M4 track 1, [issue 117](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/117), 2026-10-27).* `TwinBase.AttachAsync(ElementReference | string, options, TwinReferences? references = null)` and `TwinHandle.UpdateAsync(options, TwinReferences? references = null)`; `TwinReferences` (in `Interop/`) collects elements (`Add(ElementReference)`) and script-held objects (`Add(IJSObjectReference)`) and returns each one's index. Each module names its index fields, `int?` and omitted from the JSON while `null`: `PortalOptions.ContainerRef` here, `AnchorRef` and `BoundaryRefs` for `positioning` and the focus manager's element options as settled for tracks 2 and 4 below. The array follows the options string only when it holds something, so a module that names no element still receives `attach(elementOrId, options, self)` and `update(options)`; `js/twin.ts` gains the `TwinReferences` type, the `references` parameter of `TwinHandle.update` and `resolveReference(references, index)`. Its one boxing site, `Add(ElementReference)`, suppresses BLZX2005 with its reason, since the array's entries differ in type and Blazor serializes each by its runtime type. Tested by `TwinContractTests` (L2) and `twin.test.ts` (L3); the element and object cases cross at L4 with the portal's container titles (`portal.spec.ts`: an element reference, a selector, the enclosing portal's node, and a closed shadow root a page script hands C# as an `IJSObjectReference`, both modes). A caller builds its references only for an attach or an update whose inputs changed, so `Add(ElementReference)` boxes once per attach or update (the portal: `PopupPortalBehavior`, whose unchanged render allocates nothing, L2).
- *Design points settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27 before the track issues were filed (raised by the brief writers against each other's briefs; each under an existing decision or precedent).* The Escape point's literal is `escape` (§3; `decide.ts` documents it). Track 3 publishes the `interactions` export's interface (the lookup by token and the session's members) in its first commit and its design-points note; tracks 2 and 4 code against it, run standalone until it is on `main`, and list their session-dependent rows open by ID. The relay record is track 1's, in `Interop/`, with the five fields above, produced by the popup store at commit with the revision in the store's non-reactive context; the `interactions` and `focus-manager` options carry it as a nullable member. The C# half of the interaction behaviours (a session behaviour for a Root, a Provider or a tree; a trigger behaviour with the deciders) lives in `Floating/` (ADR 09 Amendments 2026-10-27), whichever of tracks 2 and 3 lands first creating its `CONTEXT.md`. The `positioning` handle reads its open and mounted inputs from the applied batch through a `MutationObserver` on the attached element's open attribute (the `OpenSelector` precedent), as the focus manager does. The `portal` module imports `focus-manager`'s `tabbable` export and lists FLT-KEY-07, FLT-FOC-17 and FLT-DOM-18 open to track 4. The test-only popup host's trigger requests its toggle from a C# `onclick` (test-only code) until `press` is on `main`. `FocusTargetValue` is written by track 1 in `Popup/`; its resolution against the interaction type is each family's. The `popup` module's keydown-stop option (POP-KEY-12, DLG-KEY-09) is track 5's, DLG-KEY-09 listed open until it lands. `.github/scripts/classify-paths.mjs` runs `alert-dialog.spec.ts` for a change under `src/Blazix.Primitives/Dialog/` (track 9, with a dated ADR 11 note), since the AlertDialog parts are shells over the shared behaviours there.

**2026-10-27, the positioning track's design points (M4 track 2, [issue 118](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/118); settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27, each as the worker recommended, under the user's plan decision 13 of 2026-10-27, ADR 01 decision 2, §3 and the Amendments 2026-10-27 above, and ADR 13 Amendments 2026-10-22; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the implementing pull request).** The layered-style writer in `dom`: one writer per element kept under a symbol, `styleWriter(el)` with `setConsumer(cssText)` and `layer(name)` offering set, remove and clear; fixed bands, under the consumer `positioning` then `transitions`, over the consumer `positioning-measured` then `auto-resize`, which is Base UI's order (positioner styles, `transition: none`, the consumer's style; the `setProperty` variables win); the consumer string parsed into longhands, writes diffed per property, a layer cleared when its handle is disposed; `transitions` and the `popup` module adopt it in track 5. The offset record: `Offset(double Constant = 0, double AnchorWidth = 0, double AnchorHeight = 0, double PositionerWidth = 0, double PositionerHeight = 0)` with `IReadOnlyList<OffsetOverride>? Overrides`, `OffsetOverride(Side? Side, Align? Align, …the same terms)`, the first override in list order whose non-null keys match the evaluated logical side and align winning; the union is `OffsetValue` (a `double` or an `Offset`) on `SideOffset` and `AlignOffset`; the suite's "reads $description inside the function" titles are restated as "applies the override for $description" and witnessed by the pixel position with `data-side` and `data-align`. `isPositioned`, `physicalSide` and the positioning `update()` stay in JavaScript, exported as `readPlacement(el)` and `update(el)` for the `popup` module and PreviewCard; the report carries none of them. One report per change, `{ side, align, anchorHidden, uncentered }`; the session token field is `Session`. The Arrow has no handle: `ArrowId` and `ArrowStyle` travel in the Positioner's options, the Arrow freezes from the first attach or `update` that carries its id and is re-resolved by id, and the FLT-DOM-10 stand-in returns whenever the id is absent or does not resolve. On `FixturePositioner` run the 14 non-Viewport titles, the 2 Viewport titles over an `adaptiveOrigin` toggle standing in for the Viewport, and the 19 transform-origin titles, each with an incidental note; the families run their 16 each. The handle's open and mounted inputs come from `OpenSelector` on the Positioner (`data-open`, `hidden`); the anchor and boundary references are `AnchorRef` and `BoundaryRefs`, indexes into the references array of track 1.

**2026-10-27, the focus-manager track's design points 3, 4, 6 and 8 and the FLT-FOC-11 row (M4 track 4, [issue 120](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/120); settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27, each as the worker recommended, under §1's no-cycle rule, the Amendments 2026-10-27 above, ADR 08 §3 and ADR 01's timing table; the user was told and may overturn; design point 1, where the focus manager's options travel, is put to the user; written in full by the implementing pull request).** The portal and the focus manager share no import: the portal handle exposes its node and outside guards on the portal node under `Symbol.for('blazix.portal')` (published by track 1), the focus manager finds it with `closest('[data-base-ui-portal]')` and publishes `{ modal, closeOnFocusOut, open, domReference, the inside guards, close(event) }` there, and `portal` imports the `tabbable` exports; C# renders the outside guards from the Popup's state cascaded to the Portal; track 4 wires `portal.ts` in one commit after track 1 lands. The `data-tabindex` stash on a composite item: while the item carries `data-tabindex`, the composite writes its roving value into `data-tabindex` and `enableFocusInside` restores it (a `composite.ts` edit with a dated CMP note), which is how the composite's precedence of the amendment above is kept. The C# half lives in `Floating/`: `internal sealed class FocusManagerBehavior`, composed by `PopoverPopup` and `DialogPopup`, and `internal static class CloseTypeClassifier` with `Classify(EventSnapshot?, InteractionType?) : InteractionType?` (a dated FOC-10 note: a C#-started close reads only `Detail` and `PointerType`); the guard rule of FLT-DOM-15 is `!Disabled && (Modal || InsidePortal)` until Combobox (M5). The options take FLT §6.10's names in PascalCase (`Modal`, `Disabled`, `InitialFocus`/`InitialFocusElement`/`InitialFocusId`, `ReturnFocus`/`ReturnFocusElement`/`ReturnFocusId`, `ExplicitReturnFocus`, `RestoreFocus` as a string literal with constants on the twin (`"none"`, `"tabbable"`, `"popup"`; the `TabsOptions.Part` precedent, corrected from an enum by the worker's addendum of the same day), `CloseOnFocusOut`, `OpenInteractionType` (sent even when `null`, which means a programmatic open), `CloseInteractionType` (C#'s classified close type, read for focus-visible), `DomReferenceId` (the active trigger's id until the session supplies the element), `TriggerFocusGuards` (the guard before the anchor counts as inside, the anchor is the previous focusable element, the guard after it the next, all found by position), `OpenSelector`, `TreatPopupAsFloatingElement`, `PreviousFocusableElement`/`NextFocusableElement`/`InsideElements` as indices into the references array and `InsideElementIds` as ids); the reports are `OnFocusOutClose(FocusOutCloseRequest { Event, InteractionType })` and `OnInteractionTypeChange(InteractionTypeChange { Type })`. FLT-FOC-11's focus-out close, decided in JavaScript and committed by C#, gets an ADR 01 relaxation row of the PSH-KEY-07 kind and FLT's §9 entry.

**2026-10-27, the interactions track's session export, design points 1, 2, 5 and 9, and the names shared across the M4 modules (M4 track 3, [issue 119](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/119); settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27, each as the worker recommended, under the Amendments 2026-10-27 above, the `form` one-twin-per-part-with-role-records precedent of Amendments 2026-10-22, the Tabs stash precedent of Amendments 2026-10-26 and the plan's "mirror the pinned source" item the user accepted; the focus-manager track's design point 1 chosen by the user the same day, ADR 01 Amendments 2026-10-27; written in full by the implementing pull requests).** `js/interactions.ts` exports `acquireSession(token)` (ref-counted, created on first use in any attach order; every module's handle calls it at attach), `releaseSession`, `getSession`, `isTopmostOpenSession`, the ported helpers `getNodeChildren`, `getNodeAncestors`, `isVirtualClick`, `isVirtualPointerEvent`, `matchesFocusVisible`, and `resetInteractionsForTest`; an `InteractionSession` carries the token, `open`, `openOrder`, `dataRef.current`, the `events` bus (`openchange` with `{ open, reason, nativeEvent, nested, triggerElement }`), its tree node (whose `context` the session writes itself, FLT-STR-08), `nested`, the tree, the floating and reference elements, the trigger elements, the position reference with a subscription, and `setPlacement`, which `positioning` calls after each pass; the tree is one forest of all live sessions, each parent the cascaded enclosing popup's token (Base UI gives each Root its own tree and relies on portal-crossing propagation the port does not have). The token form of `attach` is `TwinBase.AttachTokenAsync(string token, TOptions options)` keyed in the same handle map, `attach(null, options, self, references)` on the JavaScript side (`AttachTarget = Element | string | null`). One `InteractionsTwin` per part with the role records `Session` (the Root; Escape and outside press; asks `escape`), `Trigger` (press, hover, focus, reference press, cursor, nested-trigger detection, the hovered line; asks `press`), `Floating` (the Positioner, or Dialog's Popup) and `Provider` (the delay group); the Positioner's two handles get the attach-order title. The `press` payload is the Tabs stash (`OnPressing({ open, triggerId, interactionType })`, then `decide('press')`); the interaction-type classifier is exported from `button.ts` in its own shared commit; the stick-if-open window is armed at the relayed commit. `floatingContext` is never cleared (FLT §10 Q13, an untested quirk mirrored). Shared names: the relay record is `OpenChangeRelay`, written by track 1 in `Interop/`, JSON `{ revision, reason, eventKind, eventType, pointerType, triggerId }`, `open` travelling in each module's own options; the session token field is `SessionToken` (JSON `sessionToken`) on every module's options. The focus manager's options are pushed from the commit path before the render (ADR 01 Amendments 2026-10-27, chosen by the user), which §3's one message per state change allows: it is the same `update`, sent earlier.

**2026-10-27, the portal's move as a task the portaled handles await (M4 track 1, [issue 117](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/117); design point 3, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27 as the worker recommended, under the Toolbar first-attach precedent of TLB-STA-04 and the standing rule that the attach order of two handles is pinned by a module-delay title; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the implementing pull request).** Twins are per part and each imports its own module, and the browser caches module URLs, so when `transitions.js` is loaded and `portal.js` is not, a Popup's `transitions` attach reaches JavaScript before the Portal's attach although the Portal's `OnAfterRender` ran first; the node is then still `display: none` in place (§5), an undisplayed element runs no animations, and the first inspection would complete the enter early (the same hazard gives `positioning` zero rects and the focus manager an element that cannot take focus). The portal context therefore exposes the move as a task (track 1 names the member), which every portaled part's handle awaits before its first attach: the Positioner's (`positioning`, `interactions`), the Popup's (`transitions`, `focus-manager`, `popup`) and the Viewport's. One L4 title per mode stalls `js/portal.js` and asserts `animationstart` after the move and `OnOpenChangeComplete(true)` after it. The alternatives, re-arming the first inspection in `transitions` once the element is displayed (a shared edit that `positioning` and `focus-manager` would each repeat) and accepting the lost enter animation with a note, were rejected.

*As built (M4 track 1, [issue 117](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/117), 2026-10-27).* The portal cascades an internal `PopupPortalContext`, fixed, to the parts inside its node; its `Moved` task completes once the `portal` handle has attached and moved the node (or the portal is disposed), and starts over when the portal renders a node again after rendering none. A portaled part awaits `PopupPortalContext.WhenMoved(context)` before its first attach, which is a completed task outside any portal. L2: `PortalTwinContractTests`, `PopupPortalTests`. A render during the portal's pending first attach awaits that attach: `TwinBase.AttachAsync` hands a repeated attach of the same element the pending handle at once, so the move is completed only by the attach that moved the node (found by the stalled-module L4 title "port: a portaled popup's transitions handle attaches only after the portal module moved its node (portal.js stalled)", red first, both modes). The scroll lock as built: `PopupScrollLockBehavior.ApplyAsync(referenceElement, enabled)` attaches `ScrollLockTwin` once enabled and sends `ScrollLockOptions.Enabled` only on change; the lock count and its pending timers are the sanctioned page-global state (`js/CONTEXT.md`, reset by `resetScrollLockForTest()`).

**2026-10-27, the interactions track's naming table (M4 track 3, [issue 119](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/119); design point 6, accepted by the orchestrator 2026-10-27 as the worker proposed, under ADR 08 §3's PascalCase-of-the-Base-UI-name rule and ADR 08 Amendments 2026-10-27's `Duration?` fallbacks; the full table is the implementing pull request's `Interop/CONTEXT.md`).** One `InteractionsOptions : TwinOptions` carries `SessionToken`, the nullable `Relay` (`OpenChangeRelay`) and one role record per handle: `Session` (the Root, token form: `Open`, `ActiveTriggerId`, `ParentToken`, `FloatingId`, `Ending`, and `Dismiss` with `EscapeKey`, `OutsidePress`, `OutsidePressEvent { Mouse, Touch }`, `BubblesEscapeKey`, `BubblesOutsidePress`, and Dialog's `PrimaryButtonOnly`, `SingleTouch`, `OwnBackdropIds`, `Topmost`), `Trigger` (`TriggerId`; `Click` with `Event`, `Toggle`, `IgnoreMouse`, `StickIfOpen`, `PatientClick`, `TouchOpenDelay`, `Reason`; `ReferencePress`; `CancelPendingOpenOnPress`; `Hover` with `DelayOpen`, `DelayClose`, `RestMs`, `Move`, `MouseOnly`, `SafePolygon`, `BlockPointerEvents`, `GuardStaleOpen`, `NestedTooltipTriggers`, `DelayGroupToken`; `Focus { Delay }`; `ClientPoint { Axis }`; `HoveredLine`), `Floating` (`Node`, `Hover { CloseDelay }`) and `Provider` (token form: `Token`, `DelayOpen`, `DelayClose`, `Timeout`). Every option is nullable and sent only when set; a `null` role record turns its hook off (Base UI's `enabled: false`); milliseconds arrive resolved by C#. The reports are `OnRequest { open, reason, event, triggerId, instant? }` (every module request, Escape on Server and the delay group's close included), `OnPress { open, reason, triggerId, interactionType, event }` (Server) and `OnPressing { open, reason, triggerId, interactionType }` (the synchronous WebAssembly hand-over before `decide('press')`).

**2026-10-27, the relay's Server semantics: a cancelled request re-arms the session, and a coalesced close is replayed (M4 track 3, [issue 119](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/119); design points 10 and 11, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27 under ADR 01 Amendments 2026-10-27's commit-path `update` and §3's one message per state change; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the implementing pull requests of tracks 1 and 3).** (1) A module disarms itself after requesting a close until the next open arrives (the Amendments 2026-10-27 above), and a cancelled request moves no revision and renders nothing, so on Server a consumer's cancel of an Escape or outside-press close would leave the session deaf to the next Escape until the popup reopens, where Base UI asks again. The session role's options therefore gain a counter of answered requests (`Answered`, bumped by the session behaviour for every request it routed, cancelled or not) sent in an `update` from the commit path outside a render, so a cancel still renders nothing; the module re-arms when it moves. WebAssembly is unaffected: the verdict says "cancelled" inside the dispatch. The alternatives, re-arming on the next gesture (a second script-dispatched Escape before the commit could request twice) and keeping the disarm (an unsaved-changes prompt that cancels Escape would ignore the next one on Server), were rejected. (2) The relay carries the last committed open change, so a close and a reopen committed in one batch reach the module as `open: true` alone and the session boundary is missed (FLT-DIS-14's press reset, the hover and focus close branches). `OpenChangeRelay` therefore gains two nullable members, written by track 1 and set only when the batch committed a close before the open the relay carries: the close's revision and its reason (the worker names them); the module emits that close's `openchange` first, with its real reason, then the open. The alternative, inferring the close from a revision jump and emitting a synthetic close with reason `none`, was rejected because the inference depends on what else moves the revision and loses the reason. *Findings recorded:* Escape's "innermost" is Base UI's `hasBlockingChild` over the session forest alone; the brief's open-order gate for a document without a tree is dropped, since every KEY-02 title passes without it and the gate would close fewer popups than Base UI. A hover open still waiting for its commit on Server counts as open, so a quick leave still requests the close (the hover quirk the user chose). For Tooltip (track 6): provider-less tooltips do not share Base UI's default delay group, whose membership the port keys by a provider token (an upstream quirk to record), and a trigger whose hover role is `null` leaves its group where Base UI keeps a disabled trigger a member.

*As built (M4 track 1, [issue 117](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/117), 2026-10-27), item (2).* The two members are `OpenChangeRelay.CoalescedCloseRevision` (`int?`) and `CoalescedCloseReason` (`string?`, the kebab-case literal), JSON `coalescedCloseRevision` and `coalescedCloseReason`, omitted while `null`. The store sets them on an open's relay when the change it commits before is a close whose revision is later than the last render-batch boundary (`AtBatchBoundaryAsync` records the revision it saw); a close after an open in one batch is relayed alone. L1: `PopupOpenChangeTests` and `OpenChangeRelayTests`.

**2026-10-27, two settlements from the verification of the focus-manager pull request (M4 track 4, [PR 128](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/128); settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the pull request).** (1) The focus manager also disarms after requesting a focus-out close until the next open, and that request is a report, so a consumer's cancel leaves it deaf on both modes: `FocusManagerOptions` gains the same answered-requests counter as the interactions session (the note above), bumped by the behaviour that routed the request and pushed from the commit path outside a render, and the module re-arms when it moves; the relay member of the focus manager's options is `Relay` (JSON `relay`) with the two nullable members of the coalesced close. (2) `PreviousFocusableElement` and `NextFocusableElement` stay in the focus manager's options as reference indices beside `TriggerFocusGuards`, which is the positional form Popover uses; the two indices are Menu's (M5), proven at L3 until then.

**2026-10-27, the `positioning` row, its twin and the Positioner and Arrow rows of the ownership list, as built (M4 track 2, [issue 118](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/118); ADR 03; the module, the wrapper, the writer and the C# half chosen by the user 2026-10-27 in the Amendments above; design points 1–8 of the track settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27, each as the worker recommended; the shared token name `SessionToken` settled by the orchestrator the same day).**

*Decision 1's `positioning` row (notes):*
- *The wrapper.* `js/floating-ui.ts` is the one importer of Floating UI: it carries the legal comment and re-exports ADR 03's eight functions, the `@floating-ui/utils` helpers and the engine's types. The module-graph lint skips it as a module, accepts its import from `positioning` alone and flags `@floating-ui/*` anywhere else.
- *The options* (`PositioningOptions`):
  - `SessionToken`, inert until `positioning.ts` acquires the session (track 2's follow-up; the `interactions` export is on `main` with M4 track 3's PR 129, 2026-10-28). Dated 2026-10-28 (M4 track 2's follow-up): `positioning.ts` takes the session of the token at attach and on a change, calls `setPlacement` after each committed pass, follows `positionReference` through `subscribePositionReference`, and releases the session at dispose;
  - `OpenSelector` (design point 7: the open and mounted inputs come from the applied batch, `[data-open]` and the absence of `hidden`);
  - `KeepMounted`, `TransitionStatus`, `TriggerId`;
  - `AnchorId`, `VirtualAnchor` (and `AnchorRef`, with track 1's references array, design point 5);
  - `PositionMethod`, `Side`, `Align` as literals;
  - `SideOffset`, `AlignOffset`, or their declarative `SideOffsetFunction`, `AlignOffsetFunction` (design point 2);
  - `CollisionBoundary` or `BoundaryRect` (and `BoundaryRefs`); `CollisionPadding` or `CollisionPaddingSides`;
  - `Sticky`, `ArrowPadding`, `DisableAnchorTracking`, `CollisionAvoidance`;
  - the private `Shift` and `LazyFlip`; `AdaptiveOrigin`; `Direction`;
  - `ArrowId` and `ArrowStyle` (design point 6); `TwinOptions.Style`.
  The record has value equality, the overrides list compared by hand.
- *The report.* One `OnPlacementChange({ side, align, anchorHidden, uncentered })` per change of the four mirrored values, never per pass (design point 4). `isPositioned`, `physicalSide` and `update()` stay in JavaScript, exported as `readPlacement(element)` and `update(element)` for the `popup` module and PreviewCard (design point 3).
- *The writer.* `dom`'s `styleWriter(element)`, one per element under a symbol: `setConsumer(cssText)` and `layer(name)` with `set`, `remove`, `clear`. The bands are `positioning` then `transitions` under the consumer, and `positioning-measured` then `auto-resize` over it. Writes are diffed per property, and a module clears its layers on dispose (design point 1). `positioning` writes the positioner styles, `transition: none` and the measured variables through it, and `transitions` and `popup` adopt it in track 5.
- *The module* asks no decision point; the pipeline is rebuilt only when an input changes; the anchor and the Arrow are re-resolved by id when a render replaced them.
- *Its imports, dated 2026-10-28 (M4 track 2's follow-up, FLT-STA-08).* `positioning` imports `interactions` (the session by token), one way, besides `dom`, `transitions`, `twin` and the Floating UI wrapper. With the bundle's splitting the `interactions` code becomes a shared chunk both entries import, so the page keeps one session map; the Floating UI banner stays in `js/positioning.js`.
- *The element cases and the portal's move, dated 2026-10-28 (once M4 track 1's PR 126 was on `main`; the element-typed option values and the portal's move as a task, both in the Amendments above, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27).* `AnchorRef` (`int?`) and `BoundaryRefs` (`PositioningIndexes(Start, Count)`: the boundary elements' consecutive indexes, compared by value and serialized as a JSON array) name the anchor element and the boundary elements in the references `attach` and `update` send after the options, the anchor first. `PositionerBehavior` keeps the anchor and boundary inputs it last sent, so an element that changed at the same index still sends an update. An anchor reference not captured yet sends nothing, so the trigger applies; a boundary element, or a list whose elements are all uncaptured, is sent as `clipping-ancestors` (an uncaptured reference never narrows the boundary, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-28). The module passes the boundary elements to the engine as a list. Under a Portal the first attach awaits `PopupPortalContext.WhenMoved(context)`.

*Ownership list (Decision 2): the Positioner and Arrow rows, in full, in the table form of 2026-10-20 with the "initial value rendered by C#" column of 2026-10-18.*

| Part(s) | JS-owned | Initial value rendered by C# | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| every `*.Positioner` (Popover, Tooltip, PreviewCard in M4; Menu, Select, Combobox, NavigationMenu later), through `PositionerBehavior` | `style` (`position`, `top`/`left` or `transform`, `will-change`, `opacity`, `pointer-events`, `transition`, `--available-*`, `--anchor-*`, `--transform-origin`), `data-side`, `data-align`, `data-anchor-hidden` | `position: fixed; top: 0px; left: 0px; --available-width: 100vw; --available-height: 100vh; opacity: 0;`, `pointer-events: none;` while closed, `transition: none;` while starting, then the consumer's merged style; the requested `data-side` and `data-align`; no `data-anchor-hidden`. Frozen from the `attach` call | written by `positioning` from attach through `dom`'s writer; C# mirrors `side`, `align` and `anchorHidden` (`PositionerMirror`, RND-STA-01 row); `role="presentation"`, `hidden` (while not mounted) and `data-open` stay C#'s and live |
| every `*.Arrow`, through `PositionerArrowBehavior` | `style` (`position`, `top`, `left`), `data-side`, `data-align`, `data-uncentered` | `position: absolute;` then the consumer's merged style; `data-side`, `data-align` and `data-uncentered` from the mirror (the requested placement, uncentered, before the first report). Frozen from the Positioner's first `attach` or `update` that carries the Arrow's id | no handle of its own: the id and the consumer's style travel in the Positioner's options and the module re-resolves it by id; C# mirrors `uncentered`; `aria-hidden="true"` stays C#'s |

- The ownership registry gains these rows with the families (tracks 5–7), which declare the `[MirroredField]`s; no library part state carries them yet.
- The titles on the test-only `FixturePositioner` (design point 8): the suite's 14 non-Viewport titles, its 2 Viewport titles over an `AdaptiveOrigin` toggle standing in for the Viewport, and the 19 transform-origin titles; the families run their 16 each.

**2026-10-27, the verdict `Prevent` as built (M4 track 3, [issue 119](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/119); decision 3's verdict of the M4 note above, chosen by the user 2026-10-27).** §3's verdicts gain `DecisionPolicy.Prevent` (literal `prevent`): the module calls `preventDefault()` on the event and lets it propagate. `twin.ts` lists the five literals as `decisionPolicies` in the order of the C# members and derives the `DecisionPolicy` type from it; `decide` (`decide.ts`) and `TwinBase.Decide` are unchanged. The contract tests are the `prevent` row of `TwinContractTests`' literal theory and the `decisionPolicies` title of `twin.test.ts`. The `escape` point returns it when a consumer's `OnOpenChange` accepts the close and calls `AllowPropagation()`.

**2026-10-27, the `interactions` module as built (M4 track 3, [issue 119](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/119); the M4 notes above, chosen by the user 2026-10-27; design points 1, 2, 5, 8 and 9 settled and the naming table, design point 6, accepted by the orchestrator 2026-10-27, each as the track recommended).** Decision 1's `interactions` row is written in full by `js/interactions.ts`, `Interop/InteractionsTwin.cs` with `InteractionsOptions`, `InteractionsPayloads`, and `Floating/`'s four behaviours:
- *The token form and the session (chosen by the user 2026-10-27).* `TwinBase.AttachTokenAsync(token, options)` attaches by token, keyed in the twin's handle map; the module receives `attach(null, …)` (`twin.ts`'s `AttachTarget` and `attachTarget`). One `Map<token, session>` is created by the first handle of any module that names a token (`acquireSession`, reference-counted) and cleared with the last (`releaseSession`); `resetInteractionsForTest` clears it and the delay groups. The session holds `dataRef`, the bus, the tree node (parent: the cascaded `ParentToken`; the forest of all sessions stands in for Base UI's trees and React's portal-crossing propagation), the open order, the trigger map, the virtual anchor and the hovered-line record, and notifies the hooks' post-commit effects after it applies a commit or gains its floating element or a trigger. `positioning` and `focus-manager` read it through the export.
- *The relay (chosen by the user 2026-10-27).* The session handle's options carry `OpenChangeRelay` (track 1's record; `InteractionsOptions.Relay`, set from the popup store's `OpenChange`, 2026-10-28) and `Open`; the session emits `openchange` once per moved revision after recording the open event, then applies the committed state. A request a handle sends is not emitted when sent.
- *`press` (chosen by the user 2026-10-27).* As FLT-PTR-03's note; the interaction type is `button.ts`'s `classifyClick` (design point 5); Popover's patient-click window is armed at the relayed commit.
- *`Prevent` at `escape` (chosen by the user 2026-10-27).* Built in its own commit (the note above). Innermost is decided from the session forest before asking; the keydown carries a symbol naming the sessions that asked; a session that requested a close is disarmed until the relay brings the next open.
- *Hover timers and the delay group (chosen by the user 2026-10-27).* Every hover, rest, close, intent and group timer runs in the module and reports `OnRequest` when it fires; one group per provider token; a member's open closes the previous member through that member's Root twin (`none`, no event, `instant: true`); open requests carry the instant phase, focus opens included. The trigger `mouseleave` on a closed popup requests no close.
- *Dialog's `outsidePress` predicates (settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27).* `PrimaryButtonOnly`, `SingleTouch`, `OwnBackdropIds`, `Topmost`, evaluated in JavaScript.
- *Page-global state.* The session map and the delay groups, named in `js/CONTEXT.md`. The safe polygon's pointer-events owner is a symbol on the scope element, not a module-level map.
- *The relay's Server semantics (design points 10 and 11, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27, the note above).* `InteractionsSessionOptions.Answered` moves before the store's sequence for every request the session routes and travels with the commit's render, or alone after a cancel; the module re-arms on it, and disarms only when no answer arrived during the request. The relay's two nullable members are track 1's `OpenChangeRelay.CoalescedCloseRevision` (`int?`) and `CoalescedCloseReason` (`string?`, the reason's literal), JSON `coalescedCloseRevision` and `coalescedCloseReason`, omitted while null and set on an open's relay when a close was committed after the last render-batch boundary (named by the record's owner, PR 126); the session replays that close's `openchange` before the open, with the session's own request event when it was that close.

**2026-10-27, `classifyClick` on the `button` row (M4 track 3, [issue 119](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/119); design point 5, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27, option (b)).** Decision 1's `button` row exports its click classification as `classifyClick(event, lastPointerType)`: a click without a click count is the keyboard's, else its own pointer type, else the last `pointerdown`'s (UBT-ALG-06, `utils/useEnhancedClickHandler.ts:20-34`). The `button` module's own interaction report uses it, and the `interactions` module's `press` reads it for the open interaction type (PSH-ALG-19, iOS's empty type read as touch there). Built in its own commit with its red test (`button.test.ts` "port: a click without a click count is the keyboard's, else its own pointer type, else the last pointerdown's").

**2026-10-28, the `popup` module's design points (M4 track 5, [issue 125](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/125); settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-28, each as the worker recommended, under §1's placement rule and the `popup` row above, the applied-batch observer precedent, the one-twin-with-role-records precedent of Amendments 2026-10-22 and 2026-10-27, track 2's `readPlacement` export and the settled ownership of the keydown-stop option; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the implementing pull request).** The Viewport's snapshots and auto-resize live in `popup.ts`, run from a `MutationObserver` on the Viewport and from `update`, over `dom`'s `getCssDimensions` and `styleWriter` and `transitions`' animations watcher and frame request; the new trigger is the applied batch's `[aria-controls=<popup id>][aria-expanded=true]`; one `cloneNode` per batch that touches the Viewport, as Base UI clones per commit (a dated note on §1's `dom` row). JavaScript inserts the `[data-previous]` clone container before C#'s `[data-current]`, proven by an issue-8-style L4 title on both modes (a remount, a re-render and a dispose mid-switch); the C#-rendered slot is the fallback if the proof fails. `physicalSide` comes from `positioning`'s `readPlacement(positioner)` at frame timing. One `PopupTwin` with the role records `Viewport { Open, PopupId }`, `Positioner { Cutout, ScrollLock, TouchOpen, WatchTriggerChange, ActiveTriggerId }`, `Trigger { Guards, PopupId }` and `Popup { StopCompositeKeys }` beside `SessionToken`; the reports `OnViewportChange(ActivationDirection, Transitioning)`, `OnTriggerChangeSettled()` and, on Server, `OnGuardFocus(Guard, Event)`, the decision point `guard` on WebAssembly; the internal backdrop's cut-out re-measured on attach, `update`, scroll, resize and each positioning pass through a `MutationObserver` on the Positioner's `style`, so `positioning` needs no per-pass event; Tooltip and PreviewCard attach the Viewport handle as is. The anchored scroll lock is the Positioner handle's: it takes `ScrollLock` and `TouchOpen`, runs the 20 px width test after the batch and calls `scroll-lock`'s `acquireScrollLock(activeTrigger)`; Popover has no `ScrollLockTwin`. The trigger-change instant: the Positioner handle watches its finite animations after an active-trigger change while open and reports; C# writes `TriggerChange` into `data-instant` only while still open (a Server row of the RND-STA-01 kind). The keydown stop of POP-KEY-12 is `PopupElementOptions.StopCompositeKeys` on the Popup handle (the arrow keys, Home and End), set while `ToolbarRootContext` cascades; Dialog sets it unconditionally for DLG-KEY-09. *Naming a focus guard to the session (the sub-point of design point 4):* the session resolves a relayed trigger id among registered triggers only, so a focus-out close through a trigger guard never reached the focus manager and FLT-FOC-08's suppression never fired; `interactions` gains the export `nameRelayTrigger(session, element)`: the next relayed focus-out close with a null trigger id emits that element as `triggerElement`, cleared when `Answered` moves; `popup` imports `interactions` beside §1's four. A wave-2 edit to track 3's module by track 5, the only wave 2 session that edits it, in its own commit with its red L3 title. The alternatives, a nullable guard member on `OpenChangeRelay` (edits to tracks 1 and 3) and a listed deviation (an explicit `finalFocus` would pull focus back), were rejected.

**2026-10-28, `ContentVersion` on the `popup` module's Viewport role record (M4 track 5, [issue 125](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/125); settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-28 on the module's first push, under the payload version counter of ADR 05 Amendments 2026-10-27; written by the implementing pull request).** `Viewport { Open, PopupId }` of the paragraph above gains `ContentVersion`, the store's payload version counter: Base UI's `usePopupViewport` identifies the current content by the active trigger and by the payload's identity (`usePopupContentKey`), so a payload that arrives after the trigger switch replaces and re-measures the content; the counter is the port's stand-in for that identity, so the module treats a changed value as new content and an equal one as none. `Direction` is not added: the physical side comes from `positioning`'s `readPlacement` (the paragraph above; ADR 07 Amendments 2026-10-28), and the pull request drops the copied side map.

**2026-10-28, one close request in flight per handle (M4 track 6, [issue 121](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/121); design point 13.9, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-28 as the worker proposed, under the relay's Server semantics of Amendments 2026-10-27; the user was told and may overturn; written by the implementing pull request in its own commit with its red L3 title).** Base UI requests the reference-press close twice, on `pointerdown` and on `click` (the fork's `useDismiss` binds `closeOnReferencePress` to both, `useDismiss.ts:778-779`; a Tooltip trigger's `click` while closed also dispatches a `trigger-press` close to cancel a pending hover open), and the second is a synchronous no-op on a store the first has already closed. The port mirrors both requests; on Server the relay's round trip turns the second into a late close that reaches the store after the next hover has begun and cancels its rest timer. The `interactions` module therefore emits no further close request for a handle while one is in flight, from the request's emission until the relay is answered (the applied batch that reflects the state, or the cancellation answer; the implementing commit states the exact boundary); WebAssembly is unchanged because the first request applies synchronously. A dated note on FLT-PTR-30 records it. The alternatives, a Server relaxation row (the hover after a click-close would not open until the pointer left and re-entered) and a Tooltip-local cancel, were rejected.

**2026-10-28, twins inside a shadow root resolve through the portal's registry (M4 track 8, [issue 123](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/123); design point 9, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-28 as the worker recommended, under this ADR's `portal` paragraph (a shadow root as `Container`) and the composite notes of 2026-10-22 and 2026-10-26 (elements resolved from the root's document or shadow root); the user was told and may overturn; written by a separate small pull request of track 8 in its own commits, with its red L3 title and an L4 title on both modes).** Blazor resolves an `ElementReference` with a document query for its capture attribute (`[_bl_<id>]`, in `blazor.web.js` 10.0.9 and 11.0.0-rc.2 alike), and a document query does not pierce a shadow root, open or closed. Track 1's shadow-root L4 case proved the portal node, which attaches before the move; every part inside a portal moved into a shadow root attaches after the move (`WhenMoved`), so its twins received `null` (the Popup's focus manager, interactions and transitions handles, the Viewport's): a Dialog rendered into a closed shadow root closed on neither Escape nor an outside press. The `portal` module keeps a page-global registry of the shadow roots it has moved nodes into; `TwinBase` sends the `ElementReference`'s `Id` beside the reference; when the reviver yields `null`, `twin.ts`'s attach and reference resolution look the element up by its capture attribute in the registered roots. Cost: one more string per element in the attach payload, and a dependence on the capture attribute's form, which has not changed since Blazor 3. The alternatives, attaching before the move (it reverses track 1's design point 3: an undisplayed popup can neither be focused nor animate) and supporting only the portal node inside a shadow root (a dated deviation on DLG-STR-04, DLG-API-18, DLG-DIS-06 and FLT-API-13, against this ADR's `portal` paragraph), were rejected.

**2026-10-28, the Viewport's new trigger comes from the interactions session (M4 track 6, [issue 121](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/121); design point 13.13, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-28 as the worker recommended, correcting the `popup` module's design-point paragraph above; the user was told and may overturn; written by track 5's pull request, which owns the module, with its red L3 title).** The paragraph above took the new trigger from the applied batch's `[aria-controls=<popup id>][aria-expanded=true]`, which only Popover's and Dialog's triggers render: Tooltip's and PreviewCard's carry neither attribute (TIP-ARIA-02, TIP-ARIA-05, PRV-ARIA-02), while Base UI's `usePopupViewport` reads the store's `activeTriggerElement`. The `popup` module instead acquires the `interactions` session by the `SessionToken` already in `PopupOptions`, as `positioning` does, and reads the session's `domReferenceElement`: since the Tooltip track's design point 13.4 (1) (Amendments 2026-10-28 above) that element is the hand-off's incoming trigger at once and C#'s active trigger otherwise, so the activation direction is computed for every family from the same element, and a round trip earlier on Server than an option would allow. The alternative, `ActiveTriggerId` on `PopupViewportOptions`, was rejected for that latency.

2026-11-19 (M6 track 2, [issue 176](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/176); Amendments 2026-11-19, design points 1 and 2, chosen by the user): refined, not reversed. The trigger still comes from the session. The handle also decides again when the session's reference moves after a remount (`subscribePositionReference`), and seeds its previous trigger at attach from the trigger C# still renders `data-popup-open` on. A trigger change that completes before the handle attaches morphs nothing on Server (ADR 01's relaxation row of 2026-11-19).

**2026-10-28, the capture id travels as the attach's fifth argument (M4 track 8, [issue 123](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/123); design point 9's transport, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-28 as the worker recommended, amending the three-argument rule of Amendments 2026-10-27; the user was told and may overturn; written by track 8's separate shared-gaps pull request with its red L3 titles and the three contract test files updated).** Blazor's `ElementReference` reviver runs before any reviver a library attaches (`DotNet.attachReviver` chains them in order and Blazor's is attached at boot), so when it yields `null` the capture id is already gone and no reviver-side recovery exists. Every attach of an element reference therefore sends the reference's `Id` as a fifth argument, `(elementOrId, json, twin, references or null, captureId)`, and element references inside `TwinReferences` carry a parallel array of ids, on `update` too; `twin.ts`'s attach target and reference resolution take the id and fall back to `[_bl_<id>]` in the `portal` module's registered shadow roots when the reviver gave `null`. The twelve modules' attach signatures forward the argument; the sentence "a module that names none receives the three arguments it always did" now reads five, the fourth still `null` when a module names no references. Cost: one short string per element attach and a changed pinned contract. The alternative, sending the id only from parts inside a portal whose container is a JavaScript object (through the portal context and a `TwinBase` overload), was rejected: every portaled family would thread the context into each twin it attaches, and a non-popup family rendered inside such a portal (a Tabs inside a Dialog) would get no fix.

**2026-10-28, the starting-style `transition: none` moves into the `transitions` module's layer (M4 track 5, [issue 125](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/125); gap 13, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-28 as the worker recommended, under Decision 2 above (the layered-style writer) and TRN-DOM-05; the user was told and may overturn; written by the implementing pull request with its red L3 titles and the twin contract test updated).** The `transitions` module's adoption of `dom`'s `styleWriter` (its consumer band through `setConsumer` in place of `applyConsumerStyle`'s `cssText` overwrite) removes only what the writer set, so a C#-rendered `transition: none` (POP-DOM-29, TRN-DOM-05's handover at attach) would linger after the handover, and once the style is frozen only the module can drop the declaration on the frame that clears `data-starting-style`, while it cannot tell which elements want it: TRN-DOM-05 names the families (the positioners and the popup elements of Tooltip, PreviewCard, Popover, Menu, Combobox, Select and NavigationMenu; not Dialog, Drawer or Toast). `TransitionsOptions` therefore gains `DisableTransitionWhileStarting` (default `false`), set by the TRN-DOM-05 parts; while the status is `starting` the module writes `transition: none` into its own band, under the consumer band, so a consumer inline `transition` still wins as the row requires, and clears it on the frame that clears `data-starting-style`; C# keeps rendering the declaration only until attach. The alternatives, C# sending the style with each mirrored status (a round trip late on Server, a §9 relaxation per family) and no adoption (POP-DOM-29 exact only before attach), were rejected. Tooltip and PreviewCard set the option at their next `main` merge; Dialog does not.

**2026-10-29, C# event handlers inside a shadow-root portal container do not fire (M4 track 8, [issue 123](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/123); design point 10, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-29 as the worker recommended, a documented deviation under the `portal` paragraph above and design point 9; the user was told and may overturn; written by the implementing pull request; the platform is followed in [issue 146](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/146)).** Blazor's event delegation listens at the document and walks up from `event.target`; for an event raised inside a shadow root, open or closed, the target seen at the document is the shadow host, so no C# handler inside the root is found (`blazor.web.js` 11.0.0-rc.2 uses `composedPath` only for link navigation). The modules listen on the element itself, so Escape, the outside press, hover, focus and the trigger's press work inside a shadow-root container, while `Dialog.Close`, `Popover.Close` and every consumer element activated by `@onclick` inside the popup do not. Base UI's six nested-dialog-inside-a-shadow-root titles become port titles asserting the module-driven paths (Escape and the outside press close the nested dialog), with dated deviation notes on DLG-STR-04, DLG-API-18, DLG-DIS-06 and FLT-API-13 and a `_qa-notes.md` line; the documents tell a consumer who portals into a shadow root that C# handlers inside it do not fire. The alternatives, module-driven activation of the library's own Close parts (one more report per click on Server; consumer handlers still dead) and bridging events out of registered roots in the `portal` module (Blazor's delegator has no public API and an event's target cannot be changed), were rejected.

**2026-10-29, the in-flight close rule widens to the session, and a press that starts in a nested floating element starts inside (M4 track 5, [issue 125](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/125); gaps 14 and 15, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-29 as the worker recommended, under the relay's Server semantics of Amendments 2026-10-27, design point 13.9 above and FLT-DIS-08's tree rule; the user was told and may overturn; written by the implementing pull requests with their red L3 titles).** *Gap 14.* On Server, a real press on a focusable element outside a non-modal popup while focus is inside it raises two close requests before the first commit returns, the focus manager's `focus-out` and the dismiss handle's `outside-press`, so the consumer's open-change callback runs twice ("false:focus-out false:outside-press"); on WebAssembly and in Base UI the first applies synchronously and the second finds the store closed and does nothing. Design point 13.9's rule, one close request in flight per handle, therefore widens to the `interactions` session: while a close request from any handle of the session is in flight, a further close request from another handle of the same session is dropped; the window and its cancellation are 13.9's. The alternatives, a C# equality guard (Base UI has none, FLT §10 item 12) and a Server relaxation row (a doubled callback is observable by the consumer), were rejected; written by the Tooltip track at its re-merge, which holds 13.9's code. *Gap 15, withdrawn 2026-10-29, later (a corrected premise, found by the worker before writing it).* A press that starts inside a nested popover's popup and is released outside closes the parent with `outside-press` on both modes; the settlement of the same day had the `interactions` module count such a press as started inside through the floating tree. Base UI does the opposite on purpose: `markPressStartedInsideReactTree` (`useDismiss.ts:239-258`) returns early unless the target is inside the dismissing element's own floating DOM subtree, "to avoid suppressing parent dismissal when interacting with nested portals", and the port mirrors it. Base UI's title passes for another reason: its synthetic `pointerdown` sets a 0 ms inside-tree mark and its `fireEvent.click` lands in the same tick, while a real drag outlasts the window and closes the parent in a browser too. The port keeps Base UI's rule; the title replays Base UI's same-tick sequence as dispatched events on both modes, a commented exception to the real-input rule like the touch-session title's virtual click (real input cannot fit a 0 ms window), and a dated note on POP-DIS-04 records that the nested drag-out holds only within that window, in Base UI as in the port. As built, gap 14 (M4 track 6, [issue 121](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/121), 2026-10-29): the session holds one close mark (`closeInFlight`), cleared by a session update whose relay revision or answered counter moved and by the request's own invocation settling; `requestOpenChange` returns `false` for a dropped close, so the dismissal's outside-press and Escape paths do not disarm for a request they did not send; `claimSessionClose(session)` is exported, and the focus manager claims it before its focus-out report and releases it when its answered counter moves (L3: one title in `interactions.test.ts`, one in `focus-manager.test.ts`).

**2026-10-29, a click-like open is recorded on the press's request, and an attach overtaken by its twin's disposal yields a no-op handle (M4 track 5, [issue 125](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/125); gaps 16 and 17, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-29 as the worker recommended, under the relay's Server semantics of Amendments 2026-10-27, FLT §10 item 12's resolution and ADR 01 decision 2; the user was told and may overturn; written by the implementing pull request with its red L3 and L2 titles).** *Gap 16.* `interactions.ts` upgraded the recorded open event to the click (`syncOpenEvent`, FLT-STA-03) only when C# relayed the commit, so on Server a pointer leave within the press's round trip still found a hover open and closed a popup that the press had just made click-sticky (POP-PTR-10); Base UI's `useClick` writes `dataRef.openEvent` in the click handler itself. The module now records the click-like open event on the press's request, optimistically as a hover open awaiting its commit already counts as open, and drops it if the answer is a cancellation. The alternative, a Server relaxation row for POP-PTR-10 and FLT-STA-03 (a quick click-and-leave would close the popover), was rejected. *Gap 17.* A part unmounted while its first attach still waited on its module import left the attach naming an element that was gone, and the `JSException` ("Blazix: no element for the reference") surfaced as an unhandled renderer exception on WebAssembly, with Blazor's error UI over the page (issue 134's class). `TwinBase` now yields a no-op handle for an attach whose twin was disposed while it was in flight, as it already does on a `JSDisconnectedException`: one rule for every part and every twin, proven by one `TwinBase` L2 title and the WebAssembly L4 title "a popover closed right after it opens raises no renderer error"; `PopupModuleAttachment`'s own guard becomes redundant and is removed by the Popover pull request, its tests kept. *Corrected 2026-10-29, later (the Tooltip track, at its re-merge):* design point 13.11's catches in `PositionerBehavior` and `PopupPortalBehavior` stay, because `TwinBase`'s rule covers only a twin disposed while its attach is in flight, while a kept-mounted part whose node the close removed (a Tooltip Portal) is a live twin with a gone element, issue 134's original scenario, and its attach would still end the Server circuit; the two layers divide the cases, and both GoneElementRuntime titles fail without the catches. The alternatives, a guard in each behaviour (the next one forgotten) and a catch in each family's part, were rejected.

**2026-10-29, a retained close announces itself to the transitions handle ahead of the close's batch (M4 track 6, [issue 147](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/147), gap 18; settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-29 as the worker recommended, under ADR 01's commit-path update chosen by the user 2026-10-27 (the focus manager's options sent before the render) and §2's optimistic convergent `hidden`; the user was told and may overturn; written by a separate small pull request with its red L3 and L4 titles on both modes and the twin contract test).** The opt-out of unmounting (`PreventUnmountOnClose`, `actionsRef.unmount`) is recorded in the same store write as the close, so the popup's `transitions` handle learned of it only in the update after the close's batch; by then the exit watcher may have ended the exit, dropped `data-ending-style` and written `hidden` (§2's stand-in for Base UI's synchronous unmount), so a retained close ended `data-closed hidden` instead of mounted in the ending phase (TRN-ANI-23; POP-ANI-06, TIP-EVT-04, DLG-EVT-05, POS-03), and the positioner collapsed. When the store records the opt-out, the popup part now sends its transitions handle `TransitionsOptions.RetainExit` at once from the store notification, inside the open-change path and before the batch exists: interop calls and render batches share one ordered connection, so on Server the call lands before the batch, and on WebAssembly it is synchronous; that cycle's exit then completes only when the unmount is requested (the external-exit path), and the next open clears it (TRN-ANI-24); ordinary closes keep §2's optimistic `hidden`. A canceled close resets the opt-out (Dialog's call-time opt-out). Proven by an L4 title under circuit latency as ADR 01's commit-path evidence was. The alternatives, the exit end only reporting and C# answering (one round trip of the ending state on Server, where a keyframe exit without `fill-mode: forwards` snaps back to visible; a §2 amendment for every popup family) and restoring the ending state after the fact (the popup vanishes and its positioner collapses for a round trip on Server), are the fallback and rejected respectively. *Chosen by the user 2026-10-29, after the latency evidence:* the announcement through the handle is lost for a retained close within the popup's first attach round trip on Server (no handle to address yet; the in-flight attach carries the open's options and the close's batch ends the exit), and a part-level store listener cannot announce first (the part renders inside the write's notification loop; `PopupStore` now raises the opt-out change before the write is published). The user chose (B1) with the hardening of [issue 148](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/148) folded in: `TransitionsTwin` announces through a module export, `retainExit(element, value, captureId)` on the already-imported `transitions` module, addressed by the element, so it reaches the handle even while its attach is in flight (JavaScript handles the messages in order: the attach, then the announcement, then the close's batch), and the announcement is made at the `PreventUnmountOnClose()` call through a store hook on the open-change details, ahead of any consumer render, with a reset if the close is then canceled (except under Dialog's call-time opt-out). The alternatives, the exit end only reporting and C# answering (one Server round trip of the ending state; a keyframe exit without `fill-mode: forwards` snaps back to visible, a §9 row) and a documented first-round-trip window, were declined; the former remains the fallback if (B1)'s ordering fails under latency. The same pull request carries the start-all disposal of `PopoverPopup`'s and `TooltipPopup`'s handles ([issue 149](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/149)). *As built (2026-10-29, issue 147's pull request; the refinement settled by the orchestrator the same day, inside the user's (B1)):* the message order holds once the attach has been sent; before that, the attach carries the opt-out itself. While the attach still waits for its module's import, the close's batch can reach the browser first (a probe on Server at 250 ms saw it in 5 of 8 runs), so Popover's, Tooltip's and Dialog's popups await their `transitions` twin's import (`TwinBase.PrepareAsync()`) and build the attach's options right before it is sent: a close processed before the send rides in the options, and one after it arrives behind the attach. The module's element flag covers an announcement that arrives before its attach. `TwinBase` calls the export with typed arguments (ADR 16's no-boxing rule), with the element's capture id for a shadow root. Issue 149's remainder ships alongside: `TooltipPopup` has one handle, so its disposal starts together; `PopoverPopup` and `DialogTransitions` create no twin after a portal move that completes once the part is disposed.
