**2026-10-29, the AlertDialog track's design point 2, the Writer rows of a family that adopts another's (M4 track 9, [issue 124](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/124); settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-29 as the worker recommended, under conventions §3.5 and the ownership lint's per-document rule; the user was told and may overturn; written by the implementing pull request).** The ownership lint needs every `[MirroredField]` named by a registry entry and each entry backed by a Writer row under a heading that names its part, and alert-dialog.md had none of its own (§5.1 says "as in dialog.md"). The document gains three small Writer tables under Backdrop, Viewport and Popup sub-headings (`data-starting-style` and `data-ending-style` as JavaScript's, the Req column citing the adopted DLG rows, a dated note citing ADR 04 Amendments 2026-10-27), so the lint keeps checking each document on its own; Drawer adds its tables the same way in M6. The alternatives, an adoption map in the lint (code for one adoption now and Drawer's later, and a looser per-family check) and no `[MirroredField]` on the AlertDialog records (against the brief's records and ADR 08 Amendments 2026-10-27), were rejected. *For the record:* `DialogHandle`'s warning name is always "Dialog" whatever the mode (PSH-EDGE-02, ADL-EDGE-01), the mode still choosing the fallback store; a fidelity correction of the landed Dialog in its own red-first commit of the same pull request.

**2026-10-29, the PreviewCard track's design points (M4 track 7, [issue 122](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/122); design points 11.1–11.4, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-29, each as the worker recommended, under the fidelity rule (the pinned source over an older sentence of this record), ADR 01's commit-path update and the red-first rule for a shared-module edit; the user was told and may overturn; written by the implementing pull request with its red titles).** *11.1, the no-point case.* The 2026-10-18 sentence above, "derives the point from the trigger rect", is read as PRV-PTR-14 and PRV-POS-03 state it: when no pointer line is recorded (a focus or controlled open), the card positions against the trigger's side-aligned rect, not a derived point; the two titles that would otherwise deviate stay as Base UI wrote them. *11.2, clearing the hovered-line record at unmount (PRV-STA-13 (d)).* Nothing cleared the record but a trigger focus; `InteractionsSessionOptions` gains `Mounted`, sent from the root's commit path as the focus manager's options are, and the session clears its record when `Mounted` goes true → false, so the unmount's update leaves before a controlled reopen's on both modes; proven by a red L3 title, the contract test, an L4 title per mode and one under circuit latency. The alternatives, inferring it from `Ending` going true → false while closed (an inference where an option is explicit), the Positioner's dispose (a kept-mounted card never clears) and the transitions exit end (couples two modules), were rejected. *11.3.* The `interactions` module lacks nothing the card needs (the hover-opened-only leave, the `floating.closed` cascade, the tree join through a nested Root's parent token are on `main`); any gap an L4 title reveals is a red-first `interactions` edit raised to the orchestrator before it is written. *11.4.* PRV-DIS-02 and PRV-DIS-04, which Base UI leaves to its hooks' tests, get two L4 port titles on both modes, a nested Escape closing inner then outer and a real touch sequence outside following the 10 px, 5 px and 1 s rules, rather than a pointer to FLT's L3 rows. The Viewport ships in the same pull request over the `popup` module, as Tooltip's does; the card's retained-close clause (PRV-ANI-06) stays open to issue 147. *11.5, a reopen while still mounted (settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-29, later, as the worker recommended).* Base UI's `PreviewCardPositioner` runs `update()` whenever the card is open and mounted (`PreviewCardPositioner.tsx:82-86`), so a card that reopens during its exit re-anchors to the newly entered line; Popover and Tooltip have no such effect and neither did the `positioning` handle. `PositioningOptions` gains `PositionOnOpen` (default `false`), sent only by `PreviewCardPositioner`: the handle runs one pass when its open input turns true while mounted, from the applied batch's observer, before paint on both modes; red L3 first, the contract title, PRV-ANI-05's note. The alternatives, tying the pass to `HoveredLine` (two behaviours under one name), C# calling `update` after render (a round trip after paint on Server, a new relaxation row) and every handle running the pass on reopen (a pass Base UI's Popover and Tooltip do not run), were rejected. *11.6, the nested hover-close cascade (settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-29, later, as the worker recommended; a fidelity correction of the interactions track's behaviour, found by this track, which also corrects 11.3's "nothing found").* "parent popup closes as soon as the child popup closes" failed on both modes: in Base UI `useHoverFloatingInteraction` runs in the Popup inside the Positioner's floating node, so the `parentId` it registers is the popup's own node for Popover, PreviewCard and Menu and the enclosing node only for Tooltip, which has no node; `interactions.ts` read the tree parent as the enclosing session (`record.parentToken`), so the parent never subscribed to `floating.closed`, and a subscribed parent would still have found the child open until C#'s commit. The module now takes the tree parent as the session's own token when `floating.node` is not `false` and the enclosing session's otherwise, and counts a node as open only while open with no close in flight (design point 13.9 and gap 14's mark); red L3 port titles first, a dated wording note on FLT-PTR-24 and FLT-PTR-25, a `_qa-notes.md` line, and the Popover and Tooltip specs re-run on both modes since Popover's hover nesting changes with it. The alternative, a skipped title and an issue (PRV-DIS-08 open, Popover keeping the latent defect), was rejected.

**2026-10-28, the Tooltip track's design points and the hover hand-off's implementation (M4 track 6, [issue 121](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/121); settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-28, each as the worker recommended, under the received-`id` standing rule of [issue 115](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/115), ADR 06 §1, ADR 01 Amendments 2026-10-27's hand-off row (chosen by the user), the drop-never-throw rule of [issue 134](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/134) and TIP-STA-08; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the implementing pull requests).** A substitute's own `id`: the registry id stays the Trigger's; the `interactions` handle attaches by `Element` when captured, else by the id the substitute rendered; a component substitute that moves the id inward fails by design, named in the remarks. The Viewport's C# half is built over track 5's behaviour after merging `main`, its rows open by ID meanwhile. The shadow-root and empty-composed-path titles: the composed-path lookup proven at L3 with dated port notes, the empty path through `page.evaluate` as the one synthetic event at L4. *The hand-off row was not built by tracks 2 and 3* (the session's `domReferenceElement` is C#'s `ActiveTriggerId` alone and `positioning`'s trigger reference is C#'s `triggerId`, so on Server the popup stayed on the old trigger for a round trip and the new trigger was not the session's anchor): two module edits implement it, each in its own commit with its red L3 title: `interactions.ts` records a hover or focus hand-off's trigger as the session's pending anchor until the relay commits or `Answered` moves without one (a cancel reverts); `positioning.ts` resolves the trigger case through `session.domReferenceElement`, the anchor and the virtual anchor keeping precedence (the order of PR 135). Track 6 edits both (track 5's `nameRelayTrigger` export is the other wave-2 edit of `interactions.ts`; the second to land re-merges `main`). A hover timer whose trigger is disconnected reports nothing (TIP-PTR-23). Provider-less tooltips do not share Base UI's default delay group: TIP-STA-08 states that without a Provider the instant value is always false, so the port follows the requirement, with dated notes on TIP-STA-08 and TIP-PTR-04 and Tooltip's entry in the untested-quirks note, reported upstream. A trigger whose hover role is `null` leaves its group where Base UI keeps a disabled trigger a member: a dated deviation note on TIP-STA-08 and TIP-API-18 (observable only through `OpenAsync(id)` or a controlled `TriggerId` on a disabled trigger). As built, the pending anchor (M4 track 6, 2026-10-28): an open request that names another trigger while the session is open sets it, before the report, and `domReferenceElement` returns it; the first of a session update whose relay revision or answered counter moved and the request's own invocation settling clears it (a trigger's twin routes its request to the store directly, so a canceled one moves neither counter; a Root sends a commit's update from its commit path, ahead of the invocation's completion, so a committed hand-off never reverts); `subscribePositionReference` reports every change of `domReferenceElement` as well (L3 "port: a hand-off makes the new trigger the session reference at once, and a canceled one reverts when its request settles"). As built, positioning's trigger case (M4 track 6, 2026-10-28): after the virtual anchor, the anchor element and the session's position reference, the reference is the session's `domReferenceElement` while it has one, else the element of C#'s `triggerId`; the session's position-reference subscription brings each change (L3 "port: the trigger case follows the session's reference element while it has one, so a hand-off re-anchors at once"). As built, the disconnected trigger (M4 track 6, 2026-10-28): the hover region's open request returns before it reports when the trigger it names, else the handle's element, is no longer connected, which covers the open delay, the rest timer and the nested trigger's local reopen (L3 "port: a hover timer whose trigger left the document requests nothing"). As built, gap 19 (M4 track 6, 2026-10-29; settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-29, option (a)): with a Viewport mounted (`adaptiveOrigin` on), the trigger case reads C#'s `triggerId` only, since the `popup` module's Viewport starts its morph on positioning's write. On Server the anchor then moves in the render that brings the new content, as Base UI moves both in one commit; without a Viewport the hand-off keeps re-anchoring at once (asserted at the end of the 13.4 (1) L3 title above, "port: the trigger case follows the session's reference element while it has one, so a hand-off re-anchors at once").

**2026-10-28, the Dialog track's design points (M4 track 8, [issue 123](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/123); settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-28, each as the worker recommended, under ADR 16's commit-path rule for `--nested-dialogs`, the shared-helper rule (nothing built twice), ADR 13 §3 and the fidelity rule; the user was told and may overturn; EDGE-14's withdrawal is put to the user; written in full by the implementing pull request).** The nested count travels as `DialogRootContext.ReportNestedOpen(int dialogs, int drawers)`, called from the child's commit subscription (open ? own + 1 : 0; a closed child never reports; a dispose while open reports 0); the parent drops it while neither open nor mounted, resets once closed and unmounted, and its Popup and Viewport render in the same batch. The internal backdrop's markup is one shared helper, `Popup/PopupInternalBackdrop.Render(RenderTreeBuilder, string id, bool inert, Action<ElementReference>? capture)`, with Base UI's markup and no cut-out parameter (Popover's `clip-path` stays the `popup` module's write), written by track 8 in an early commit with its red L2 title and consumed by Popover after PR 123 lands. Return focus needs no Dialog addition (the focus manager's commit-path options cover stop, removal and dispose). The focus-target parameters are `FocusTargetValue? InitialFocus` with `Func<InteractionType, FocusTargetValue?>? InitialFocusResolver` and the same pair for `FinalFocus` (ADR 08 Amendments 2026-10-28); the resolver wins over the value with a `Blazix: ` development warning; the close type of a close C# starts comes from the focus manager's recorded `OnInteractionTypeChange` through `CloseTypeClassifier`, no new message. *A fidelity correction of the 2026-10-27 settlement that named `Topmost` as Dialog's dismissal predicate:* the `interactions` module's `Topmost` (`isTopmostOpenSession`: no popup on the page opened later) is not Base UI's gate, which is the nested dialog count (`escapeKey: isTopmost` and the outside-press predicate's first test in `useDialogRoot`), and the two diverge where a Popover is open inside a modal Dialog and the Dialog's backdrop is pressed (Base UI closes both), where two non-nested modal Dialogs are open and the first's uncovered backdrop is pressed (the first closes: its own nested count is zero, where `Topmost` would close nothing), and where two non-nested non-modal Dialogs are open and an outside press lands (both close: each has a nested count of zero, where `Topmost` would close only the later one); Dialog therefore gates from C# with the count already written in the commit path (`EscapeKey = false` and `OutsidePress = false` while the count is above zero, and under `DisablePointerDismissal`) and does not send `Topmost`, which stays in the module for M5; dated notes on DLG-STA-07 and the FLT rows; the three cases proven at L4 on both modes (the parentheticals corrected 2026-10-28, later: they had named `Topmost`'s outcomes, not Base UI's; found by the worker when the titles failed with `Topmost` planted).

**2026-10-27, the `focus-manager` module as built (M4 track 4, [issue 120](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/120); the module's inputs chosen by the user 2026-10-27 in the notes above; design point 1 chosen by the user and design points 3, 4, 6 and 8 settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27, in the notes above; design points 2, 5 and 7 settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27 before filing).**
- *§1's `focus-manager` row.* `js/focus-manager.ts` (entry `js/focus-manager`) imports `composite` (`isElementVisible`), `dom`, `event-info`, `transitions` (`requestFrame`, `cancelFrame`, `observeOpenInput`) and `twin`. It exports the tabbable queries under Base UI's names (`isTabbable`, `focusable`, `tabbable`, `getNextTabbable`, `getPreviousTabbable`, `getTabbableNearElement`, `isOutsideEvent`, `disableFocusInside`, `enableFocusInside`), which `portal` and `popup` import, plus `markOthers(avoid, { ariaHidden, mark })`, `enqueueFocus(el, { preventScroll, sync, shouldFocus })` and the test reset `resetFocusManagerState()`. Its one handle is `attach(elementOrId, optionsJson, twinReference, references?)` on the Popup part's element, marked with a symbol so a repeated attach returns the live handle and `dispose` is idempotent.
- *The inputs.* The open state is the applied batch's: `observeOpenInput` on the popup element with `FocusManagerOptions.OpenSelector` (`[data-open]` by default), never `update` after `attach`. The floating element is the popup element's parent (the Positioner) unless `TreatPopupAsFloatingElement` (Dialog); the floating focus element follows FLT-FOC-05. The options (design point 8, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27): `Modal`, `Disabled`, `InitialFocus` with `InitialFocusElement` (a reference index) or `InitialFocusId`, `ReturnFocus` with `ReturnFocusElement` or `ReturnFocusId` (the parts' `FinalFocus`), `ExplicitReturnFocus`, `CloseInteractionType` (the close type C# classified), `RestoreFocus` (`none`, `tabbable`, `popup`), `CloseOnFocusOut`, `OpenInteractionType` (the literal, `null` for a programmatic open, sent even when null), `DomReferenceId`, `TriggerFocusGuards` (Popover's trigger guards by position), `PreviousFocusableElement` and `NextFocusableElement` (reference indices, Menu's), `InsideElements` (reference indices), `InsideElementIds` and `Answered` (the answered-requests counter: the module re-arms its focus-out close when it moves). The relay is read from `relay` (`Relay`), with its coalesced-close members `coalescedCloseRevision`/`coalescedCloseReason`; the C# member waits for the popup core's `OpenChangeRelay`, and `SessionToken` for the interactions session. The relay and the session token join as nullable members when tracks 1 and 3 land; until then the module drives its `openchange` listener from a relay member of its own shape (`revision`, `reason`, `eventKind`, `eventType`, `pointerType`, `triggerId`).
- *The reports*, each once per change: `OnFocusOutClose` (`FocusOutCloseRequest(EventSnapshot Event, string InteractionType)`, reason `EventReason.FocusOut`), after which the handle disarms until the next open; `OnInteractionTypeChange` (`InteractionTypeChange(string Type)`), the recorded type C# classifies the close from. `FocusManagerTwin` raises `FocusOutCloseRequested` and `InteractionTypeChanged`. No decision point: the trap's prevent is unconditional and the closes are requests (`OnDecide => DecisionPolicy.Run`).
- *The guards.* The module listens on the inside guards C# renders as the popup element's siblings and writes their VoiceOver `role="button"` once after attach (the swap of the note above).
- *Design point 1 as built (chosen by the user 2026-10-27).* The test-only host sends changed options from `OnParametersSet`, before the batch that applies them, so the batch finds this change's `Disabled` and targets; measured on Server at a 150 ms one-way delay, options sent only from `OnAfterRender` land the marking 19 frames after the opening batch (`tests/browser/focus-manager.spec.ts`, the ordering title per mode). Return focus also runs when the batch removes the popup element or an ancestor (each ancestor's child list observed while armed, narrowed from the document's subtree in the review of PR 128), before the dispose. A `tabindex` written inside the floating focus element while initial focus holds its default and nothing has been interacted with re-picks the default once, so a composite group whose module attaches late still gets initial focus (the attach-order title). Design point 4: while a composite item carries `data-tabindex`, `composite.ts` writes its roving value there. Design point 3's portal wiring and design point 6's `Floating/` half follow track 1.
- Tested by `js/focus-manager.test.ts` (L3), `FocusManagerTwinContractTests` (L2) and `tests/browser/focus-manager.spec.ts` (L4, both modes).
- *Decision 2: the page-level writes.* The outside marking's `aria-hidden` and `data-base-ui-inert`, the `data-tabindex` stash, the managed `tabindex` of a floating focus element rendered without one, and the inside guards' VoiceOver `role` are this module's writes on elements no part owns; they are recorded, with the module's page-global state and its test reset, in `js/CONTEXT.md` (no ownership-registry row).

**2026-10-28, the focus manager's portal wiring and C# half as built (M4 track 4, [issue 120](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/120); after the popup core was on `main`; design points 3, 5 and 6, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-27).**
- *The publication.* While enabled inside a portal, `focus-manager` fills the `focusManager` slot of the state `portal` publishes under `Symbol.for('blazix.portal')` with `{ modal, closeOnFocusOut, open, domReference, insideGuards, close }`, and clears it when it stops; `close` is the focus-out request. Its inside guards fall back to the portal's outside guards and listen on `focusin`, as React's `onFocus` does.
- *The portal's half.* `portal.ts` imports `disableFocusInside`, `enableFocusInside`, `getNextTabbable`, `getPreviousTabbable` and `isOutsideEvent` from `focus-manager`, which imports nothing back. One `focusin` listener on the wrapper's parent routes the outside guards (FLT-KEY-07). Unless the publication is modal, capture `focusin` and `focusout` on the node disable and enable the focus inside, and an open publication enables it (FLT-FOC-17, DOM-18); the slot is an accessor.
- *The classifier's result* is `InteractionType`, not the settled `InteractionType?`: Base UI's empty literal is `InteractionType.Unknown`, so a nullable result would never be null (accepted by the orchestrator 2026-10-27).
- *The C# half.* `Floating/FocusManagerBehavior`, with `FocusManagerInputs` and `CloseTypeClassifier`, does what the test-only host did itself: the inputs from the parameter set (design point 1), element targets through `TwinReferences` (an element the render has not captured yet names nothing), `FocusManagerOptions.Relay`, the inside guards, `PopupPortalContext.Publish(PortalFocusPublication(Modal, Open, CloseOnFocusOut))`, the first attach after `PopupPortalContext.WhenMoved`, `Answered`, and the close type classified when the close commits. An attach whose popup left the document before it arrived is dropped, not thrown (found at L4 on a cold Server host).
- Tested by `focus-manager.test.ts` and `portal.test.ts` (L3), `CloseTypeClassifierTests` (L1), `FocusManagerBehaviorTests` and `FocusManagerTwinContractTests` (L2), and `focus-manager.spec.ts` on `focus-manager-portal` (L4, both modes).

**2026-10-28, the focus manager joins the interactions session (M4 track 4, [issue 120](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/120); after M4 track 3's PR 129 was on `main`; the session export of track 3's design point 3).**
- *§1's `focus-manager` row.* `js/focus-manager.ts` also imports `interactions` (`acquireSession`, `releaseSession`, `getNodeChildren`, `getNodeAncestors`, `isVirtualClick`, `isVirtualPointerEvent` and the session types); `interactions` imports nothing from it.
- *The token.* `FocusManagerOptions.SessionToken` (the popup store's `SessionToken`, PSH-STR-01) joins the session at attach and releases it on dispose. The manager then reads the session's `openchange` (the real event, `nested`, the trigger) in place of `Relay`, its `dataRef` (the inside marker shared with the dismiss region; the recorded open event for the screen-reader press), its anchor before `DomReferenceId`, and its registered triggers and tree in the focus-out check and in return focus. Without a token the relay path is unchanged.
- *The allocation point of the delta check.* `FocusManagerBehavior` builds the options at attach and when an input, the answered count or the close type changed, never for an unchanged render (ADR 16 rule 7, the `PopupPortalBehavior` precedent).
- Tested by `focus-manager.test.ts` "with the interactions session" (L3, over `test-support/interactions-harness.ts`), `FocusManagerTwinContractTests` and `FocusManagerBehaviorTests` (L2).

**2026-10-29, the capture ids as built (M4 track 8, [issue 123](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/123); the transport note above, settled by the orchestrator 2026-10-28).** The fifth argument is one `string?[]` that carries both: the attached element's capture id first (`null` for an id or a token), then the references' parallel ids in order (`null` for a script-held object). Every attach of an element reference is therefore exactly five arguments, the fourth `null` when the module names no references; an attach of an id or a token whose references hold no element keeps three, and an `update` whose references hold an element sends the same array third, its first entry `null`. `TwinReferences.CaptureIds` builds it; `TwinContractTests`, `PortalTwinContractTests` and `PositionerBehaviorTests` pin it.

**2026-10-29, the inline middleware as built (M4 track 7, [issue 122](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/122); under §1's `positioning` row and track 2's reserved first slot of Amendments 2026-10-27, as the orchestrator's brief for the track set it; written by the implementing commit after its red L3 titles).** The `positioning` module exports `createInlineMiddleware(read)`, Base UI's `inline` middleware (`inlineRect.ts:237-292`) with the hovered-line reader injected. A handle puts it first in its pipeline only when `PositioningOptions.HoveredLine` is set: `PreviewCardPositioner` sets it, and the option is not sent while false, so no other Positioner's options change. The handle's reader is `readHoveredLine` on the handle's `interactions` session, read at each pass, so a hover recorded after the attach narrows the next pass. The line grouping and the point lookup, which both halves need, live once in `interactions.ts` and are exported (`getLineRects`, `findLineIndex`). With no recorded point, the middleware reduces a wrapping anchor to the side-aligned line, as the pinned source does (PSH-ALG-16, PRV-PTR-14). Design point 11.1, settled 2026-10-29 (the PreviewCard track's design points above), reads the no-point sentence of Amendments 2026-10-18 this way.

**2026-10-29, design point 11.6 as built (M4 track 7, [issue 122](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/122); the settlement above, refined by the implementing pull request).** The rule that a node with a close in flight counts as closed applies to the hover cascade's open-children check (`useHoverFloatingInteraction`'s `hasParentChildren`), the case the design point is about; the dismissal's blocking-child check (Escape, the outside press) keeps the committed state. Base UI's tree node carries a render-time snapshot of `open` (`useFloating.ts:90-118`), so within one keydown a child that has just closed still blocks its parent; applying the rule there let one Escape close two nested cards on WebAssembly, where the child's close commits inside the keydown. Two nested cards open from the first render still register their Escape listeners in either order, and on WebAssembly a child's synchronous close then releases its parent in the same keydown; that ordering edge, which Base UI's snapshot does not have, is a follow-up of the track (the L4 title opens the child after the parent, as in use).

**2026-11-03, the M5 planning decisions (chosen by the user 2026-11-03, "Do recommended"; ADR 12 Amendments 2026-11-03).** (1) *List navigation stays in `composite`* (§1 as written, no new module): a list handle with role records (reference, floating, item, trigger) on `composite`'s `attach`, one `CompositeTwin` per handle, joined through the interactions session token; typeahead wholly in the module, its reset timer and the typing flag included, the `button` module's Space path reading the flag; the registration by rendered id gains `Label` and `Index`, each sent only when set; values stay in C#, joined to the reported order (the E2 precedent of Amendments 2026-10-26). Track 1 adds an L2 byte-size title at 100 and 500 items and adds delta registrations only if that figure is material, by a dated note here either way. (2) *The highlight's writer.* §2 gains a row for every popup list item part (the items of Menu, ContextMenu, Select, Combobox and Autocomplete and their variants): `tabindex` and `data-highlighted`, `C# → JS` from attach, written by the list handle in the same task as the focus move; and, under virtual focus, the reference's `aria-activedescendant` (and the floating element's unless the reference is typeable), JavaScript's, from the active item's own id. C# renders the initial values under the handover, mirrors `Highlighted` as a mirrored field (ADR 01's RND-STA-01 row), and asks for C#-origin highlights through `update` with a revision so a stale echo is ignored (CMP-STA-32). Items keep stable generated ids; Base UI's `{id}-{index}` item ids are a dated deviation on CMP-ARIA-01's id clause, CMB-DOM-09 and AUT-DOM-03. (3) *Decision points.* The consumer's `onKeyDown` veto on list keys reuses `navigate` on the list handles (option (i)'s Server reading, Amendments 2026-10-22). Five new points, a point's name being per twin (Amendments 2026-10-26): `leave` on the list handle (the Shift+Tab close of CMP-KEY-24 and CMP-EVT-08; the verdict decides the focus return; fallback: close and return focus); `clear` and `remove` on the `combobox` twin (the Escape-while-closed clear and ChipRemove, with the fallbacks of Amendments 2026-10-20); `press` on the `context-menu` twin (the `contextmenu` and long-press open, after the consumer's `PreventBaseUIHandler()`; fallback `run`); `activate` on the `select` twin (an item activation the module classified, the synthetic click after a drag release included; fallback `run`). (4) *Component modules*, each row written by the implementing pull request: `select` as one module with roles (aligned placement over a new `aligned` band of `dom`'s style writer, scroll arrows, the trigger release, the items' press gates and click classification; `data-side="none"` written by `positioning` when told aligned); `context-menu` as named in Amendments 2026-10-18, its anchor set through a position-reference setter `interactions` exports and release activation kept in a per-tree slot that Menu's drag-release reads; `navigation-menu`, its row widened to the activation path, the open key, the List's propagation guard, the guards' routing, the focus-out checks and focus return, the Content move a lite portal handle per Content if a probe shows the lite portal accepts a container that can disappear and reappear, else a `portal` move export written by track 2; a new `combobox` component module under the placement rule (IME composition, the input's `inputType` classification, caret and `scrollLeft` writes, Enter's synthetic click, the chips' keys and focus moves, the `mousedown` defaults of Trigger, Clear, InputGroup and Chips). No `menu` module: drag-release and the floating tree's Menu events (`itemhover`, the sibling-close timers, `hoverEnabled`) go in `interactions`, written by Menu's session in wave 2; the keyboard relay of CMP-KEY-14 in `composite`, written by track 1. (5) *The floating tree across families.* One internal, family-agnostic cascade carries the enclosing popup's session token; every popup Root and NavigationMenu's Content cascade it and every Root reads it for its parent token. Track 2 edits the five M4 Roots, re-runs their specs, and settles issue 156 (the children's open state snapshotted at the keydown's start) before any M5 family nests popups. (6) *Combobox's Input `value`* is `C# → JS` (the `Input`/`Field.Control` precedent of Amendments 2026-10-22): C# renders it once under the handover and sends committed values through `update`; the module writes only when the committed value differs from the live text since its last write and re-asserts a controlled value the consumer did not apply. (7) *What track 2 adds:* to `interactions`, data options for NavigationMenu's `getScope`, `getHandleCloseContext` and the trigger's own lock, the lock's rule against a C#-rendered `style`, a cross-menu outside-press selector, the mixed toggle of PSH-PTR-04 and PSH-ALG-20, a position-reference setter and an outside-press grace for ContextMenu, a dismissal exemption list for Combobox, the consumer `onmousedown` veto on the trigger behaviour; to `focus-manager`, the untrapped typeable combobox; to `popup`, a backdrop cut-out element option; and the platform flags reach C# once per runtime (VoiceOver, macOS) for the C#-rendered attributes that depend on them (MNU-A11Y-01).

**2026-11-04, the NavigationMenu track's design points 1–3 and 5–7 (M5 track 5, [issue 163](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/163); settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04, each as the worker recommended; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the pull request).** (1) The store before track 2's lifecycle is on `main`: ADR 12 Amendments 2026-11-04 item 3 answers it; the track follows its order (a), the rows that need mounting open by ID, and merges no unmerged track-2 commit, the interface commit included. (2) One interactions session per NavigationMenu Root, every Trigger registered with its own role record and `ActiveTriggerId` from the active Item (the per-Root session token of Amendments 2026-10-27); the eight multi-trigger paths of the session are each decided by the NAV title the track names, and the session's lock over inactive triggers is off when NavigationMenu's own lock is configured (track 2's data option). (3) The size pass on Server: the module measures the current size at the activation, holds it, and runs the measured steps when the twin `update` carrying the new active trigger arrives from `OnAfterRender`; on WebAssembly the activation's synchronous invoke renders the batch first, so the steps run in the same task; a second activation aborts the first; NAV-PTR-02, NAV-PTR-18 and NAV-ANI-04 gain dated Server marks for the round trip, measured (the `navigation-menu` row of Amendments 2026-10-18 and 2026-11-03 (4); ADR 01 decision 1). (5) C# renders NavigationMenu's four focus guards and the `aria-owns` owner; the `navigation-menu` module listens on them, reads the tree through `getSession`/`getNodeChildren` and the tabbables through `focus-manager`'s exports, and makes the VoiceOver `role` swap (the rule of Amendments 2026-10-27 that C# renders every guard and the owning module listens). (6) `data-instant` stays C#'s on NavigationMenu's Positioner (Amendments 2026-10-27): the module reports a window `resize` at most once per animation frame, C# restarts its 100 ms timer per report and keeps the 0 ms initial reset; a measured ADR 01 row covers NAV-ANI-14 and NAV-DOM-28; the Writer cells for the Popup's anchoring styles, `transition`, `hidden` and `--anchor-*`/`--transform-origin` are corrected by dated notes. (7) A fidelity correction, not a decision (conventions §3.5): Base UI's `nested` is `useFloatingParentNodeId() != null`, so a NavigationMenu inside any enclosing floating node, a Popover's popup included, is nested; the port reads nested from the family-agnostic cascade of Amendments 2026-11-03 (5) (any enclosing token), with a dated note restating NAV-STA-04. Design point 4 (a consumer veto of the open key) is the user's: Base UI merges the consumer's `onKeyDown` to the right of the Trigger's own props (`NavigationMenuTrigger.tsx:729-735`), so the consumer runs first and its `preventBaseUIHandler()` stops the open key.

**2026-11-04, the popup-layer track's design points 2–4 (M5 track 2, [issue 160](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/160); settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04 as the worker recommended; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the pull request).** (2) Correction of Amendments 2026-11-03 (5)'s "every popup Root": Tooltip neither reads nor cascades the family-agnostic cascade, as Base UI's Tooltip takes no part in the floating tree, so a popup inside a tooltip parents to the enclosing popup; TIP-DIS-08 is unchanged. (3) The pointer-events lock on an element whose `style` C# renders (Menu's and NavigationMenu's triggers, NavigationMenu's List, the lock's scope element): while a lock is applied, `interactions` keeps one `MutationObserver` on the scope, the reference and the floating element (`attributeFilter: ['style']`) and re-asserts only a `pointer-events` value that differs, disconnecting when the lock clears; §2 gains the row "inline `pointer-events` on the lock's scope, reference and floating element | JS while the lock is applied, re-asserted after C#'s writes", the re-assert precedent of the Checkbox `indeterminate` and `Field.Control` `value` rows. (4) The platform flags reach C# through a scoped service `PlatformFlags` (`bool? VoiceOver`, `bool? Mac`, `Task EnsureAsync(IJSRuntime)`), registered by `AddBlazixPrimitives()` and filled once per circuit or runtime by an `interactions` export at the first popup Root's first `OnAfterRenderAsync`; until then it reads as neither VoiceOver nor macOS, by a measured ADR 01 row; a static `[JSInvokable]` target is rejected because Server shares it across circuits.

**2026-11-04, the list-navigation track's design points 1–5 (M5 track 1, [issue 159](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/159); settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04, under Amendments 2026-11-03 (1); the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the pull request).** (1) One list handle per element role, items attaching nothing: a reference handle on the trigger or input (closed-trigger keys, typeahead, `aria-activedescendant` under virtual focus), a trigger handle on each other trigger, a floating handle on the popup's list (the registry, delegated item listeners, the highlight writes, `leave`); the shared state (cursor, recorded key, modality, typeahead buffer, typing flag) under a key the `composite` module owns in the session's `dataRef.current`; every handle calls `acquireSession`, and the attach order is pinned by titles that stall `js/composite.js` and `js/interactions.js`. (2) The typing flag reaches the `button` module through `defaultPrevented`: the list handle prevents a Space typed during typeahead in a capture listener on its floating and reference elements, before the item's own `button` listener, whose existing branch skips a prevented Space (Base UI's split, `useMenuItemCommonProps.ts:85-88` and `useButton`); Amendments 2026-11-03 (1)'s "the `button` module's Space path reading the flag" holds through `defaultPrevented`, pinned by a listener-order title, with no `button.ts` change. (3) The keyboard relay of CMP-KEY-14 finds the bar's root through an element-typed list option (an index into the references array, the `FocusManagerOptions.PreviousFocusableElement` precedent); the root handle publishes its relay under a symbol on its element, and `relayKeyboardEvent(rootElement, event)` runs the root's key resolution for a keydown the floating handle did not stop (no module-level registry, §3). (4) CMP-STA-16 (`guess`, MAY) is restated as L3 port titles (the first publication indexes automatic items in DOM order with no second publication; an explicit index takes no automatic slot), re-tagged [L2] → [L3] with a dated note (ADR 13 §3). (5) Registration size: no delta protocol until the track's measured L2 figures are in (Amendments 2026-11-03 (1)); the estimates (about 25 B per item per `update`, 48 B with labels; about 16 B per id in the order report) put the order report of one list at about 2,000 items over Server's default receive limit of 32 KB, which closes the circuit. A circuit-closing limit is not settled here: the track measures, and the user chooses between a documented limit and the cheapest change that removes it (chunked or delta order reports).

**2026-11-04, two M5 design points chosen by the user (chosen by the user 2026-11-04, "File it for me", the answer whose stated meaning was option (b) for both, as recommended; ADR 12 Amendments 2026-11-03).** (1) NavigationMenu's open key keeps Base UI's consumer veto (M5 track 5, [issue 163](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/163), design point 4): the `navigation-menu` twin asks a decision point named `navigate` before the module prevents and stops the open key (the composite meaning: the consumer's `onKeyDown` veto of a handled key; fallback `run`); on WebAssembly the consumer's declined C# `onkeydown` runs inside the point and its `PreventBaseUIHandler()` stops the open, as Base UI's `mergeProps` order does (`NavigationMenuTrigger.tsx:729-735`); on Server the point falls back to `run` under ADR 01's "C#-origin consumer vetoes" row. (2) No list closes a Server circuit (M5 track 1, [issue 159](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/159), design point 5): the list registry's order report is chunked above a fixed size, so no report from JavaScript reaches Server's default 32 KB receive limit, and C# joins the chunks of one publication before acting on it; track 1 measured 7.9 KB for 500 items on the wire, about 2,000 items at the limit unchunked; the `update` direction (about 40 KB for a 500-item labelled list) stays whole, and no delta protocol is added; the chunk size and the joining rule are track 1's, recorded in its pull request with the figures.

**2026-11-04, the list-values track's design points 1–5 (M5 track 4, [issue 162](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/162); settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04, each as the worker recommended; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the pull request, with the dated ADR 08 and 09 lines it names).** (1) The shared list behaviours (`ItemIndicatorBehavior`, `ListboxSeparatorAttributes`) and helpers (`ItemEquality`, `ValueLabels`, `CollatorFilter`, `ListValueParameters`, the null test) live in `Utilities/` (ADR 09 Amendments 2026-10-21; the standing rule that helpers more than one family needs live there); `FormValue.Serialize<TValue>` lives in `Field/`. (2) The label input is an internal view (`LabeledItems<TValue>`, flat, grouped or keyed), the public item shapes being the families' (ADR 08 Amendments 2026-11-03 (4)); a value's default label is its invariant text, as the form value's (ADR 08 Amendments 2026-10-22), which matches Base UI's locale-independent `String(value)`. (3) The collator filter takes one `CompareOptions?` that replaces the defaults (the formatting-parameter rule of ADR 08 Amendments 2026-10-21: .NET types, no options record) and is a value type over the cached `CompareInfo`; `IgnoreKanaType` and `IgnoreWidth` join the defaults only on the L1 collation table's evidence. (4) The `form` module's list additions ride on the Control role with a new `ReportAutofill` and the report `FormAutofillReport(string Text, bool DefaultPrevented, EventSnapshot Event)`; the module restores the C#-owned hidden-input value inside the `change` dispatch and C# applies a match through `update` (the `C# → JS` re-assert of Amendments 2026-11-03 (6) and decision 10 of the M5 plan). (5) The scrollbar-hiding `<style>` is emitted inline by each emitter, honouring `CspProvider`'s nonce and `DisableStyleElements`; Base UI's one-per-document de-duplication (UTL-DOM-32) is a dated deviation (identical duplicate `<style>` elements, no visible effect).

**2026-11-04, the popup-layer track's design points 5 and 6 (M5 track 2, [issue 160](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/160); settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the pull request).** (5) The family-agnostic cascade of Amendments 2026-11-03 (5) is supplied by the family stores: `PopoverStore`, `PreviewCardStore` and `DialogStore` implement `IFloatingNodeContext`, so the fixed store cascade each Root already renders carries the node (one class declaration each; the three store files join track 2's touch list). A `CascadingValue<IFloatingNodeContext>` in each Root is rejected because it raises Popover's measured re-render from 1,544 to 1,624 B, over its budget, and ADR 16 rule 7 tightens budgets only. (6) Issue 156 closes on its L3 title, red in both registration orders and both synchronous settings, then green; the L4 title "nested cards open from the first render" is not added, because it is red on `main` only intermittently on WebAssembly and cannot be red on Server, so it would fail the red check that ADR 11 §3 requires of every added test. Keeping it as a regression guard would need an ADR 11 exemption, which is the user's to grant.

**2026-11-04, the NavigationMenu track's design point 8 and the Content's cascade (M5 track 5, [issue 163](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/163); settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the pull request).** (8) With a real pointer, moving onto another trigger switches the menu by hover and resets the recorded open event, so a click on that trigger then closes the menu (NAV-PTR-05, NAV-PTR-07), as Base UI does in a browser, its titles dispatching a click that never hovers; on WebAssembly the port matches; on Server the click role's toggle arrives a round trip after the hover's commit, so a click inside that window keeps the menu open: a dated Server mark on NAV-PTR-05 and NAV-PTR-07 with one measured ADR 01 row, the latency class of design point 3, and no code; the titles that click a different trigger while open dispatch the click as Base UI's do. The cascade: NavigationMenu's Content cascades its own `FloatingNodeContext`, scoped to the Content, as track 2's interface designs it for a part with no fixed context of its own; the store-supplied form of design point 5 of the same day applies to the M4 Roots only.

**2026-11-04, the `form` module's list additions as built (M5 track 4, [issue 162](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/162); design point 4 (a)+(i), settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04).** §1's `form` row widens to a list root's form-participation input: the `Control` role with `ReportEvents = false` and `ReportAutofill = true`.
- The module reports each `change` once as `OnAutofill`, carrying `FormAutofillReport(string Text, bool DefaultPrevented, EventSnapshot Event)`, and puts the committed value back inside the dispatch. A value the root matches arrives through `update` with a new revision. There is no decision point.
- §2's ownership list gains the row as built: the input's `value` is `C# → JS`, rendered once under the handover, then written by the module from `update` (the `Field.Control` rule of the Amendments 2026-10-22).
- The families change their Writer cells (SEL §5.17's `value`, Combobox's hidden input) and add their `OwnershipRegistry` rows.

**2026-11-04, the popup-layer track as built (M5 track 2, [issue 160](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/160); Amendments 2026-11-03 (5) and (7), and the popup-layer track's design points 2–6 of 2026-11-04, settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04).**
- *(5) The floating tree across families.* `IFloatingNodeContext { string? SessionToken }` (`Floating/`) is supplied by the family stores (design point 5): the fixed store cascade of Popover, PreviewCard and Dialog carries it, AlertDialog's through Dialog's store, and every Root except Tooltip's reads it as a `[CascadingParameter]` for its session's `ParentToken` (design point 2). `FloatingNodeContext(string? sessionToken)` is what a part with no store of its own cascades, fixed (NavigationMenu's Content). Issue 156 is fixed in `interactions`: the Escape keydown snapshots the session forest's nodes at its start, so a child that closes inside the keydown still blocks its parent (L3 "port: nested sessions open from the first render, in either registration order: one Escape closes only the child, and the next closes the parent"; L4 across families, both modes: `popover.spec.ts` "a dialog nested in a popover" and `dialog.spec.ts` "a popover nested in a dialog").
- *(7) What track 2 adds.* The naming table of 2026-10-27 gains these members under its rule (the full table is `Interop/CONTEXT.md`):
  - `Trigger.Hover`: `PointerEventsScopeSelector` (a selector for NavigationMenu's `getScope`), `BlockPointerEventsOnEnter` (the trigger's own lock) and `InlineCloseContext` (`InteractionsInlineCloseContext(FloatingId, NodeToken)`, for `getHandleCloseContext`). With the own lock set, the session's lock over inactive triggers is off (the NavigationMenu track's design point 2).
  - `Trigger.Click`: `MouseDownAction` (`open` or `close`, the mixed toggle).
  - `Session.Dismiss`: `OutsidePressExemptSelector`, `OutsidePressExemptIds` and `OutsidePressGrace` (milliseconds, 0 not sent).
  - The reports `OnPress` and `OnPressing` gain `deferred`, sent only as `true`, for a press deferred to a frame or a touch delay (ADR 01 Amendments 2026-11-04). `InteractionsTriggerBehavior` declines the consumer's `onmousedown` for a press from the `mousedown` and keeps its veto.
  - Three `interactions` exports: `setSessionPositionReference(token, rect | null)` (ContextMenu's anchor, a 0 × 0 rect at a point), `clearSessionOpenEvent(token)` (NAV-PTR-07) and `readPlatformFlags()` (the JSON `{ voiceOver, mac }`). NavigationMenu's patient-click window is the session's and outlives the click role (NAV-PTR-05).
  - `popup`: `PopupPositionerOptions.CutoutElementId`. `focus-manager`: the untrapped typeable combobox, decided from the anchor and `initialFocus === false`, with `FocusManagerInputs.TypeableComboboxAnchor` for the C# half's guards. `positioning`: `PositionerInputs.Shift`, passed as `PositioningOptions.Shift`.
  - Not added: the `portal` move export of (4), since track 5's probe showed that the lite portal accepts a container that disappears and reappears.
- *Design point 3's §2 row, as built.* While a lock is applied, `interactions` keeps one `MutationObserver` (`attributeFilter: ['style']`) on the lock's scope, reference and floating element, re-asserts only a `pointer-events` value that differs, and disconnects when the lock clears (L3 "port: a style rewrite while the lock holds keeps the lock's pointer-events at the next microtask, and one after the release stays as written").
- *Design point 4's "the first popup Root" reads as "the first Root that renders a platform-dependent attribute"* (settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04). Menu's and ContextMenu's Roots inject `PlatformFlags` and call `EnsureAsync` from their first interactive `OnAfterRenderAsync`. The M4 Roots render no such attribute and do not read the service. ADR 01's row of 2026-11-04 has the measured figures.

**2026-11-04, the composite row's registration without a `tabindex` (M5 track 1, [issue 159](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/159), Deliverable 10; the form set by the orchestrator's brief for the track, from fact sheet F Q15 (a); the name `OmitTabIndex` is the track's).** The composite row of Amendments 2026-10-22 ("an item whose registration carries a consumer `tabindex` is not written") gains the no-`tabindex` case: an item registered with `CompositeItemRegistration.OmitTabIndex` (`CompositeItemBehavior.SetRegistration(…, omitTabIndex: true)`) renders no `tabindex`, and the module writes none, highlighted or not, while the item stays in the registry for the arrow keys. A consumer `tabindex` on such an item still wins and travels as the consumer's. NavigationMenu's `Link` is the consumer (NAV-DOM-21, NAV-FOC-03; Base UI's `NavigationMenuLink.tsx:43` overrides the composite `tabIndex` with `undefined`). Proven at L3 ("writes no tabindex on an item registered without one, and the arrow keys still reach it") and L2 (`CompositeItemNoTabIndexTests`).

**2026-11-04, the list handle as built (M5 track 1, [issue 159](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/159); under Amendments 2026-11-03 (1)–(3), chosen by the user; the names are the track's, published in its first commit 24d8c99; design points 1 (a) with its C# refinement (r1), 2 (c), 3 (a), 4 (a) and 6 (a) settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04 as the track recommended, the user told and able to overturn; design point 5's chunked order report chosen by the user 2026-11-04, the note of that name above).** `composite`'s `attach` with `CompositeOptions.List` attaches a list handle in one role (`Reference`, `Trigger` or `Floating`, CMP-STR-06; the item role is the registration). Every handle of a list takes a reference on the popup's interactions session (`acquireSession`) and the list's shared state (Base UI's `indexRef`, `keyRef`, pointer modality, the forced focus and scroll flags, the typeahead buffer and the typing flag) lives in the session's `dataRef` under the key `blazix.composite.list`, which the module owns (FLT-STR-05's rule); so the reference hears the closed trigger's keys and the floating handle owns the registry, the items' listeners by delegation (focus, click, mouse move, and `pointerleave` in the capture phase, as React's per-item handler) and the highlight's writes. The module reads `open` at event time from the session, and during an `openchange` from the value it emits (the session emits before it applies). A list handle attaches after the session's handle (C#: `CompositeRootBehavior` awaits `ListNavigationBehavior.SessionReady`), so a `defaultOpen` session, which emits no `openchange`, is read at attach. On the C# side the list handles are `CompositeRootBehavior` in list mode (`SetList(ListNavigationContext, CompositeListRole)`) and the items `CompositeItemBehavior`, so no second registry exists; the store-agnostic `ListNavigationBehavior(sessionToken, sessionReady, Func<OpenChangeRequest, Task>, dispatchException)` in the family's Root builds the one `CompositeListOptions` record every handle sends (rebuilt only after a field changed, compared by reference) and the Root calls its `FlushAsync()` from `OnAfterRenderAsync`. One `navigate` decision per keydown per handle, asked before the handle's first effect, gates both list navigation and typeahead, as Base UI's `mergeProps` runs the consumer's handler first and its `preventBaseUIHandler()` skips both. `leave`: the module stops the Shift+Tab, then asks; `Run` (the close was not canceled) returns the focus, `Stop` (canceled) leaves it; on Server the fallback `run` requests the close (`OnListOpenRequest`, reason `focus-out`) and returns the focus. Typeahead's typing flag is read by `button` through `defaultPrevented`: the floating handle prevents an item's Space while typing in a capture listener, before the item's own `button` listener, whose branch `button.ts:520-523` skips a prevented Space on a `menuitem*`, `option` or `gridcell` (design point 2 (c)). No listener on the reference: Base UI reads the typing flag only in items (`useMenuItemCommonProps.ts:85-88`, `SelectItem.tsx:161-169`); on a trigger the typeahead is the trigger's own keydown, whose Space branch stops the event (`useTypeahead.ts:132-136`), as the reference handle's does, and a native trigger's Space activation is the browser's on keyup, which a prevented keydown cancels in any listener order (L3 "port: on a closed button trigger a Space while the buffer holds text is consumed by the reference handle and opens nothing"; PR 170's review, fix 5). `composite` imports `enqueueFocus` from `focus-manager`; design point 6 (a) keeps §1's no-cycle rule by moving `isElementVisible` and `isHiddenByStyles` into `dom`, which both import (`composite` re-exports them).

**2026-11-04, the ownership list's row for the popup list items (M5 track 1, issue 159, Deliverable 4; Amendments 2026-11-03 (2), chosen by the user).**

| Part(s) | JS-owned | Initial value rendered by C# | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| every popup list item: Menu's `Item`, `CheckboxItem`, `RadioItem`, `LinkItem`, `SubmenuTrigger` and ContextMenu's shells of them; Select's `Item`; Combobox's and Autocomplete's `Item` | `tabindex`, `data-highlighted` | `tabindex="-1"` on every render (none for an item registered with `OmitTabIndex`, as Combobox's and Autocomplete's, CMB-DOM-09); no `data-highlighted` (the state record's `Highlighted` is a `[MirroredField]`) | written by the floating list handle from `attach`, in the task that moves the focus: `data-highlighted` on the highlighted item; `tabindex` `"0"` on the highlighted item of an open list under DOM focus and `"-1"` elsewhere, into a non-modal portal's `data-tabindex` stash while the item carries one; never over a consumer's `tabindex`. C# mirrors `Highlighted` from the module's report (ADR 01's RND-STA-01 row) |
| the list's reference under virtual focus (Combobox's and Autocomplete's `Input`, Menu's filter input); the floating element under virtual focus unless the reference is a typeable combobox | `aria-activedescendant` | none | written by the list handle from the active item's own element id while the list is open and an item is highlighted, removed otherwise; never written under DOM focus (CMP-ARIA-01, ARIA-02; Base UI's `{id}-{index}` item ids are the dated deviation of Amendments 2026-11-03 (2)) |

- The families' `Writer` cells (MNU, CTX, SEL, CMB, AUT) and the ownership registry's rows are their pull requests'; the track's hand-over lists them per family.

**2026-11-04, correction of the 2026-10-18 sentence on CTX-KEY-07 (M5 track 1, issue 159, Deliverable 6; fact sheet A contradiction 6; a fidelity correction of where the behavior lives, the decision "not replicated" unchanged).** The nested close key of a root context menu is list navigation's, which lives in `composite` (§1), not in the `context-menu` component module: ContextMenu's root menu runs Base UI's `nested: true` (`MenuRoot.tsx:521`) and does not close on the cross-orientation key because it sends the list option `KeepOpenOnCrossOrientationKey` (`CompositeListOptions.KeepOpenOnCrossOrientationKey`, `ListNavigationBehavior.KeepOpenOnCrossOrientationKey`). The 2026-10-18 sentence "the port's `context-menu` module does not pass the nested close key to a root context menu" reads "the root context menu's list handle is configured not to close on the nested close key".

**2026-11-04, the registry's order report chunked, as built (M5 track 1, [issue 159](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/159); design point 5 (b), chosen by the user 2026-11-04, the note "two M5 design points chosen by the user" above; Amendments 2026-11-03 (1)'s byte figures).** Measured at L2 (`CompositeRegistrationFieldsTests`, Release, arm64): the `update` after a registry change is 2265 B at 100 items (4407 B on the wire, where Blazor's interop serializer writes each quote of the options string as `"`), 4055 B (8197 B) with labels, and 10665 B (20807 B) at 500 items, 20055 B (40197 B) with labels; the order report answering it is 1301 B (1505 B on the wire, `JSON.stringify`'s `\"`) at 100 items and 6901 B (7905 B) at 500, about 16 B per id, so one list of about 2,000 items reached Server's default 32 KB `MaximumReceiveMessageSize`, which closes the circuit. Built: every order report goes through one function of the module (`reportItemOrder`), whole while its payload is at most `ORDER_REPORT_CHUNK_CHARS` = 8,192 characters of JSON, else in parts of one publication (`CompositeItemsChange(Ids, Publication, Part, Parts)`, the three fields sent only for a part), each part below 25 KB on the wire even with every quote escaped and non-ASCII ids; `CompositeTwin` joins the parts in order and raises `ItemsChanged` once per publication, a newer publication's first part drops an incomplete older one, and a part out of sequence is ignored. At 5,000 items the whole report would exceed the limit; it arrives in parts, each within it, and the root's `OrderedItems` is the joined order (L2 "at 5,000 items the order report arrives in parts within the receive limit and the root joins them in order"; L3 "reports a long order in parts of one publication, each within the chunk size, whose ids join in order"). The `update` direction stays whole (not bounded by the receive limit; about 40 KB at 500 labeled items), and no delta protocol is added.

**2026-11-04, the highlight report's label (design point 9 (a) of M5 track 6, [issue 172](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/172); settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04, with the `js/composite.ts` and `Composite/` grant for this one field).** `composite`'s highlight report (`OnListHighlight`, `CompositeListHighlight`) gains an optional `label`: the highlighted item's label as the registry resolves it (its registered label, else its text), sent only when there is one, `null` in C# otherwise. Menu's `OnItemHighlighted` reads it for MNU-EVT-10's label in place of the explicit labels C# knew (CMP-EVT-04's dated note).

**2026-11-04, three popup-layer fixes from PR 168 (M5 track 5's review found them in track 2's code; settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04; written by the `interactions` owner of wave 2, M5 track 6, with `js/portal.ts` granted for G2 only).** (G1) The floating hover's layout-effect cleanup clears only the pointer-events mutation its own run applied (`instance.pointerEventsReferenceElement === ref`), as Base UI keeps a hover state per trigger: on Server a hover switch's commit no longer clears the lock the new trigger applied (FLT-PTR-21's dated note). (G2) The `portal` module's focus-inside listener returns at once for a lite node, as Base UI's `FloatingPortalLite` has no tabbable logic (PSH-STR-11's dated note). (G3) A trigger's `mouseleave` uses the session's published floating context only when it has a placement, else the inline close context: an unpositioned floating handle (`node: false`) publishes a context with none, where in Base UI only `useFloating` publishes one (FLT-PTR-14's dated note). The fix is in the leave handler rather than in `attachFloating`, whose published context other readers (the focus manager, positioning) keep relying on.

**2026-11-04, the unpositioned Positioner takes no pointer (G5 of [PR 168](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/168), found by M5 track 5; settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04, with `js/positioning.ts` and `Floating/PositionerBehavior.cs` granted to M5 track 5 for G5 only).** A Positioner carries `pointer-events: none` while the first pass of the current open is pending, not only while closed (usePositioner's `inert: !open`). C# renders it in the pre-attach style it freezes at attach, open or not. The module's rule is "closed, or not yet positioned with a reference in the document". A Positioner with nothing to position against stays as Base UI leaves it, and the module's layer adopts C#'s declaration at attach so that it can drop it. Base UI positions in a layout effect before paint. The port positions after attach, and on Server that is a round trip after the open's render, so the transparent Positioner at the viewport origin (FLT-DOM-02) took a pointer resting there. Both halves are needed. The window is mostly before attach: with the module's half alone, NavigationMenu's deep closeOnClick title passed 89 of 100, and with both, 100 of 100. From attach the module owns the whole style, and its first render precedes the pass (FLT-DOM-02's dated note).

**2026-11-04, the `navigation-menu` component module as built (M5 track 5, [issue 163](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/163); the row widened by Amendments 2026-11-03 (4) and the settlements of 2026-11-04 above, written in full by the pull request; the worker's under those settlements; the user may overturn).** (1) *Scope.* `js/navigation-menu.ts` keeps one menu state per Root element (under a symbol, reference-counted) that a handle per part joins (`part`: `root`, `trigger`, `list`, `link`, `content`, `positioner`, `popup`, `viewport`, `arrow`), and runs its pass in a microtask after its observers' records. It owns:
  - the size pass (NAV-ANI-01..12), written in the `dom` style writer's `auto-resize` band on the Popup and the Positioner;
  - the Trigger's activation with its direction: on the entering `mouseover` (React's `onMouseEnter`), so it runs before the hover interaction's `mouseenter`; on `click`; on the open key, after the `navigate` point;
  - the reset of the session's recorded open event on a hover or key activation of an open menu (NAV-PTR-07), through the `interactions` module's `clearSessionOpenEvent(token)` (track 2's PR 169), as Base UI's Trigger clears it;
  - the List's axis guard (NAV-KEY-06);
  - the guards' routing, over `focus-manager`'s tabbables and the session forest (`getSession`, `getNodeChildren`);
  - the focus-out reports, one in flight until the Root's `answered` moves;
  - the focus return at unmount, gated by the close reason;
  - the inline Viewport's target, its inert report and its exit watcher;
  - the Positioner's tabbables taken out of the tab order while focus is outside, and its resize reports;
  - the Popup's anchoring styles, in the `positioning` band;
  - the exiting Content's `inert`;
  - the Arrow's starting `transition: none`, in the `transitions` band.

(2) *Options and reports.* `NavigationMenuOptions : TwinOptions` carries `Part` and `RootRef` (the Root's element among the references), and on the Root `Orientation`, `Rtl`, `Nested`, `Mounted`, `CloseReason`, `SessionToken` and `Answered`, on a Trigger `HasContent`, on the Viewport `HasPositioner`. The reports are:
  - `OnActivate(NavigationMenuActivation(string? Reason, string? Direction, EventSnapshot? Event))`, invoked synchronously on WebAssembly;
  - `OnFocusOut(NavigationMenuFocusOut(EventSnapshot? Event))`;
  - `OnViewportInertChange(NavigationMenuViewportInertChange(bool Inert))`;
  - `OnResize()` and `OnExitComplete()`.

  The decision point is `navigate` (chosen by the user 2026-11-04). An activation's reason literals are `trigger-hover`, `trigger-press` and `list-navigation`, which `NavigationMenuLiterals` parses (the `interactions` literals have no `list-navigation`).

(3) *The probe's outcome* (Amendments 2026-11-03 (4)). A lite `PortalTwin` per Content, with the Viewport's target as its container, moves the Content in and keeps the element across switches. Six L3 cases held (`spikes/navigation-menu-content-move/`): a container that leaves and comes back, a latch once moved, and the restore before Blazor removes the Content. One hazard: an `update` whose element reference revives as `null` moves the node into the body. The Content's latch renders nothing while its target is away, so no such `update` is sent ("port: a latched kept content follows a portal that unmounts and mounts again, and leaves nothing in the body", L4, both modes).

(4) *Untested quirks.* The untested-quirks note of Amendments 2026-10-18 extends to NavigationMenu: its §16 Q3, Q4, Q6, Q7 and Q11–Q13 are replicated as the source does (sheet F Q17 (a), the brief's instruction).

(5) *§2's ownership list* gains NavigationMenu's rows (`tests/Blazix.Primitives.Tests/Lints/OwnershipRegistry.cs`):
  - Content's and Backdrop's `data-starting-style` and `data-ending-style`: JS;
  - the Positioner's `data-side` and `data-align`: C# → JS; its `data-anchor-hidden`: JS; `--available-width` and `--available-height`: C# → JS; `--anchor-width`, `--anchor-height`, `--transform-origin`, `--positioner-width` and `--positioner-height`: JS;
  - the Popup's transition attributes, `--popup-width` and `--popup-height`: JS;
  - the Arrow's `data-side` and `data-align`: C# → JS; its `data-uncentered`: JS.

(6) *The design points as built.*
  - (2) One session per Root, every Trigger registered, `ActiveTriggerId` from the active Item.
  - (3) The size pass is held across the Server round trip, measured in ADR 01's row (NAV-PTR-02, NAV-PTR-18, NAV-ANI-04). The held steps run in the first applied-batch pass where the Trigger has `data-popup-open` and the Viewport's target directly holds an open Content (the refinement accepted by the orchestrator 2026-11-04).
  - (5) C# renders the four guards and the owner; the owner carries `aria-owns` only while a Viewport is registered, and the module finds the guards past it either way.
  - (6) `data-instant` is C#'s, measured in ADR 01's row (NAV-ANI-14, NAV-DOM-28).
  - (7) `nested` is read from the cascade, and a Content cascades its own `FloatingNodeContext`.
  - (8) The click after a hover switch carries a Server mark, measured in ADR 01's row (NAV-PTR-05, NAV-PTR-07).

**2026-11-04, a press pending when another open commits (G4 of [PR 168](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/168): M5 track 5 found it; settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04; written by the `interactions` owner of wave 2, M5 track 6).** The session's application of a relay no longer drops the optimistic open event of a press whose request is still pending when the relay is not that request's commit and opens a session that did not hold the open yet (the open the press raced). The press is recorded again on its request after the commit's state and the answered counter are applied, so the click upgrades the hover open as Base UI's store does, and a later answer that leaves the press pending still rolls it back (gap 16). On Server a press after a hover open's request and before its commit therefore keeps the popup open when the pointer leaves (FLT-STA-03's dated note). A trigger's press reaches the store through the trigger's twin, which moves no session counter, so the hover commit's own update cannot roll the press back. *Amended 2026-11-11 (Amendments 2026-11-11): a press C# declines, canceled or vetoed, now moves the session's counter after it ran; an accepted one still moves none, so this holds.* A press made while the session held the open is never kept by a later commit: C# answers it with no relay, and NavigationMenu's switch clears the open event as Base UI's trigger does (NAV-PTR-07).

**2026-11-04, the list module's open-focus mark (F1 of [PR 183](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/183): M5 track 6 found it; settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04 as (b), the user told and free to overturn; written by M5 track 6, with `js/focus-manager.ts` and `js/composite.ts` granted for this mark only).** A session data slot shared by two modules: while open, the `composite` module marks each item focus it queues as `{ element, origin }` under `blazix.composite.openFocus` (the key and its type, `ListOpenFocus`, live with the session's data in `interactions.ts`), and the close clears it. At the open's first arm, the focus manager replaces a recorded element that is the mark's `element` with its `origin`, which Base UI's layout effect records before that focus. Nothing else changes: an element the author focused and a re-arm while open keep Base UI's record (CMP-ALG-23's and FLT-FOC-20's dated notes). It replaces an ordering rule between the list's attach and the focus manager's (costed at about +215 ms on a Server keyboard open at 50 ms each way), and serves every list family.

**2026-11-05, a published context with no placement (G6 of [PR 168](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/168): M5 track 5 found it on `main`; settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-05; written by the `interactions` owner of wave 2, M5 track 6).** The trigger's leave no longer falls back to a published floating context that has no placement: with no inline close context either, it closes as Base UI does without a handle-close context. A safe polygon built from a placement-less context could never close, and on Server a leave can come before the Positioner's first pass (FLT-PTR-14's dated note). G3's rule, a placement-less context yields to the inline one, is unchanged.

**2026-11-04, Menu's `interactions` additions as built (M5 track 6, [issue 172](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/172); Amendments 2026-11-03 (4), chosen by the user; the names are the track's, published in its first commit b3071cdb and its design-points note; design point 2 (a) settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04).** Each addition is its own commit with a red L3 title, and this note gains an item per commit. (1) *The hover gates* (MNU-STA-11, MNU-STA-12, MNU-PTR-08, MNU-PTR-09). Each menu session keeps Base UI's `hoverEnabled` and `allowMouseEnter` (`MenuStore.ts:24, 42`) in a `menu` session slot: JavaScript state that no C# decision reads, so neither crosses the boundary. `hoverEnabled` starts on. It goes off on a click in the popup, on a pointer move over a submenu's popup (`floating.menu.disableHoverOnMove`), and when a child menu's session opens (a child whose floating handle carries `floating.menu`, `MenuPositioner.tsx:134-140`). It comes back on at the close; a change while closed is none, as Base UI's layout effect restores it at once (`MenuRoot.tsx:336-339`). A hover handle with `hover.requireHoverEnabled` (the submenu trigger's) or `floating.hover.requireHoverEnabled` (every menu popup's) is off while `hoverEnabled` is, its listeners removed and re-added as on an `update` (the hooks' `enabled`). `allowMouseEnter` goes on at a pointer move over the popup or over the active trigger (`trigger.menu.trackMouseEnter`) and off when the session's `mounted` goes true → false (`MenuRoot.tsx:226-231`). A trigger with `hover.requireParentMouseEnter` (a submenu trigger with a delay) opens on hover only while its parent session's `allowMouseEnter` is on (`MenuSubmenuTrigger.tsx:183`). React's synthetic events bubble through portals, so Base UI's popup handlers also see a child popup's moves and clicks; the port listens on each popup alone, the child's open having already turned these menus' hover off. Proven at L3 (`Menu › hover gates`, five titles) and L2 ("port: Menu's hover gates are written by their module names only when true, and a change is a changed option"). (2) *The tree's item hover and the sibling close* (MNU-PTR-11, MNU-EDGE-12). A menu popup's floating handle with `floating.menu.itemHover` emits `itemhover` (`{ nodeId, target }`, the session token and the item) on the tree's bus, the forest root's, for a pointer move over an item (`menuitem`, `menuitemcheckbox`, `menuitemradio`) whose nearest `[data-rootownerid]` is the menu's own popup, before the popup's own move (`useMenuItemCommonProps.ts:89-101`, React's bubbling order). A submenu's floating handle with `floating.menu.siblingCloseDelay` (the active trigger's `CloseDelay`, absent for a menu with no parent menu) listens: an `itemhover` of its parent session over an item other than its trigger closes it with `sibling-open`, at once for 0, else after the delay on a timer that further hovers do not restart; an `itemhover` over its trigger clears the timer, and the close clears it (`MenuPositioner.tsx:178-216`). The close is a request on the floating twin with no event, as Base UI's `createChangeEventDetails(REASONS.siblingOpen)` carries none. Proven at L3 (`Menu › item hover`, three titles) and L2 ("port: Menu's item hover and sibling close delay are written by their module names, a 0 delay is sent, and a change is a changed option"). (3) *Release activation* (MNU-PTR-02, MNU-PTR-03, MNU-PTR-04, MNU-DIS-07; design point 2 (a)). The tree's release slot is `{ armed, rules, initialPoint }` under `blazix.interactions.menuRelease` in the `dataRef.current` of the tree's outermost menu session (from 2026-11-10, after the interim review of PR 183's F8; the forest root's before, which two top-level menus inside one popup of another family shared), so every menu of a tree, and a plain menu inside a context menu (MNU-EDGE-15), shares Base UI's `allowMouseUpTriggerRef` (`MenuStore.ts:173-181`) and a context menu's rules. A trigger with `trigger.menu.release` (a top-level menu's) arms the slot 200 ms after a `mousedown` on its closed menu and listens once for the document's `mouseup`: the release disarms the slot and, outside the trigger, the floating element, any element of the tree (`data-rootownerid` equal to `trigger.menu.rootId`) and the trigger's bounds, requests a close with `cancel-open` and the `mouseup` as its event; the same listener is added after this trigger's hover open, and both go when the trigger's open ends, which also disarms the slot (`MenuTrigger.tsx:120-171, 240-256`). A popup with `floating.menu.release` clicks an item of its own popup on `mouseup` while the slot is armed, with `detail: 1` and `pointerType: 'mouse'` and the release's modifiers (`button.ts`'s `dispatchClickWithModifiers`); an item with `aria-haspopup="menu"` (a submenu trigger) takes none (`useMenuItemCommonProps.ts:107-145`). The item listener runs before the document's, as React's root listener runs before a `document` listener, so the release that clicks also disarms. A close the release requests closes the tree through the root's cascade to its children, which Base UI's tree `close` does. Track 7's seam: `armMenuRelease(token, armed, rules?)` sets the slot of the token's tree; `rules` (`MenuReleaseRules { button, skipButton, slop, initialPoint }`) replace the set ones and their point, `null` clears them, and an absent `rules` keeps them; with rules, the first release consumes the point and does nothing within `slop` of it, a release with `skipButton` does nothing, and a click needs `button`. The trigger's `Release` is a top-level menu's only; a menubar's triggers are track 8's. Proven at L3 (`Menu › release activation`, five titles) and L2 ("port: Menu's release options are written by their module names, each flag only when true, and a change is a changed option"). (4) *The guard return* (MNU-FOC-08, D3 of [PR 183](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/183), added 2026-11-10): `trigger.menu.closeOnGuardReturn`, sent by a submenu trigger. While the menu is open, a capture `focusout` on the positioner marks a return when focus leaves the inside guard before the popup for this trigger, and the trigger's next `focus` closes the menu with `focus-out` (MenuSubmenuTrigger.tsx:108-125, 246-253). Proven at L3 (`Menu › guard return`) and L2 ("port: Menu's guard-return option is written by its module name, only when true, and a change is a changed option", added 2026-11-10 after the option).

**2026-11-05, an open list waits to show (MNU-FOC-03, found by M5 track 6's L4 session on [PR 183](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/183); the fix was asked for by the orchestrator 2026-11-05; written by M5 track 6).** `composite`'s initial sync waits, up to 120 frames, while the floating element is not visible and the session stays open, before it searches for the first item. A relay reaches a kept-mounted list before the batch that shows it (ADR 01's commit path), and the search skips invisible items (CMP-ALG-24's dated note). Lists whose floating handle attaches after the render are already showing, and do not wait.

**2026-11-10, two focus manager compensations for the port's portal timing (D10 and D7 of [PR 183](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/183): found by M5 track 6's L4 session; D10 accepted by the orchestrator 2026-11-05, and `js/focus-manager.ts` held by M5 track 6 for Menu's rows until PR 183 lands, settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-05; written by M5 track 6).** Both make up for the port's delayed portal, which Base UI does not have because it portals at once. (1) The default initial focus skips focus guards and anything inside a portal node within the popup (FLT-FOC-06's dated note): a nested popup's portal content, guards included, sits inside its parent's popup until the `portal` module moves it. (2) Return focus re-arms when the session's reference changes, and once the manager stops with its popup, an element focused inside that popup is no return target (FLT-FOC-07's dated note): C# disposes the manager before the `portal` module takes the moved popup out of the document.

**2026-11-10, the list's highlight report follows the element at the index (MNU-EVT-11, found by M5 track 6's L4 session on [PR 183](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/183); `js/composite.ts` held by M5 track 6 for Menu's rows until PR 183 lands, settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-05; written by M5 track 6).** `composite`'s highlight report is sent when the index or the element at it changed, and also on each registry publication, with no event (CMP-EVT-04's dated note). C#'s reporters already compare the index and the item.
