**2026-11-10, the hover lock respects the unpositioned Positioner's hold (found by M5 track 6 on [PR 183](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/183), Menu's MNU-PTR-06 title on WebAssembly; the fix from `main` asked for by the orchestrator 2026-11-10; written by M5 track 6, the `interactions` owner).** The safe polygon's pointer-events lock leaves the `positioning` module's hold on the floating element (`opacity: 0` with `pointer-events: none`, G5 of PR 168, Amendments 2026-11-04), lifts the element to `auto` once the hold is gone, and does not clear it; the observer that re-asserts the lock's values follows the same rule. The lock's `auto` had overridden the hold, and G6's plain close (Amendments 2026-11-05) turned the resulting leave into a close (FLT-PTR-21's dated note). G6 is unchanged.

**2026-11-10, an open list waits for the batch that shows it (amends the line of 2026-11-05 above; asked for by the orchestrator 2026-11-05; written by M5 track 6, with `js/composite.ts` held for Menu's rows).** `composite`'s initial sync no longer counts up to 120 frames while the floating element is not visible: it observes the attributes of the floating element and its ancestors, where C# renders what hides a kept-mounted list, and searches at the microtask after the batch that shows it (CMP-ALG-24's dated note). A close or the floating part's teardown ends the wait.

**2026-11-10, a disabled list trigger handles no keys (TLB-STR-05 found it; settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-10).** The `composite` module's reference and trigger list handles return from `keydown` first while their own element is disabled per `isElementDisabled` (a `disabled` attribute or `aria-disabled="true"`): no list navigation, no typeahead, no `navigate` decision. Base UI's `useButton` returns from a disabled trigger's `onKeyDown` before the trigger's merged handlers run (`internals/use-button/useButton.ts:116-119`), and its gate reads the `disabled` parameter, so a trigger focusable when disabled is covered too; the port's list handle listens on the element independently of the trigger's `ButtonBehavior`, so it carries the gate itself (CMP-KEY-22's dated note). It covers every family's list trigger (Menu, Select, Combobox); the floating handle and the pointer handlers are unchanged. TLB-STR-05's Menu clause goes green in Menu's pull request.

**2026-11-10, the button module's disabled-element check (MNU-EDGE-06; settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-10 as option (a), the user told and free to overturn; written by M5 track 6, with `js/button.ts` and `Button/` granted for this check only).** `ButtonOptions.DisabledElementCheck` (`disabledElementCheck`, sent only when true) turns on a development check beside the native-mode one: at attach and on each update, an element rendered disabled while the part is not is reported as `OnDisabledElementDetected` (`ButtonTwin.DisabledElementDetected`), and the part writes its message through `ButtonDevelopmentLog` (UBT-EDGE-01's and MNU-EDGE-06's dated notes).

**2026-11-10, two decision points on the `combobox` twin (chosen by the user 2026-11-10, "Do recommended for decisions"; M5 track 10's design point 6).** The `combobox` twin gains `navigate` and `press`. These are the recorded names, and each name belongs to a twin:
- `navigate` is the consumer's `onKeyDown` veto on the Input.
- `press` is the consumer's `onMouseDown` veto on the Chips and on the InputGroup.

Both fall back to `run`. One keydown reaches two handles, so C# runs the consumer's handler once per event and caches the verdict. The implementing pull request records the verdict paths and ADR 01's "C#-origin consumer vetoes" lines.

**2026-11-10, a list option keeps the highlight through the close (SEL-STA-07, found by M5 track 9; queued by the orchestrator 2026-11-10; written by M5 track 6, with `js/composite.ts` held).** `CompositeListOptions.RetainHighlightWhileClosing` (`retainHighlightWhileClosing`, sent only when true): the close resets the list's index but commits no cleared highlight, as Base UI's Select ignores the reset's `onNavigate()` while closed, so its highlight stays while the popup animates out. Select sets it; Menu does not.

**2026-11-10, NavigationMenu's click activation listens in the capture phase (G4b of [PR 168](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/168), fixed by [PR 198](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/198); settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-10).** `js/navigation-menu.ts` registers the Trigger's `click` listener with `capture: true`. The activation therefore runs before the `interactions` click role's listener on the same element, whichever handle attached first. That is Base UI's order: its `mergeProps` runs the rightmost handler first, and the Trigger passes `defaultProps` (the activation) to the right of `referenceProps` (`useClick`), `NavigationMenuTrigger.tsx:727-734`. The port had matched that order only because the click role was re-added after the module's listener once the Positioner registered. Since the click role stays installed through an open (NAV-PTR-05's note of 2026-11-10), a click that closed the menu would otherwise be followed by the activation's reopen. The precedent is Amendments 2026-10-23's capture listener at the root, which runs before the `button` module's Enter suppression and before any bubbling listener.

**2026-11-11, the `menu-filter` component module (M5 track 12, [issue 197](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/197); design points 1 (a), 5 (a) and (c), and 6 (a), settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-11 under the user's "Do recommended for decisions"; design point 6 follows the user's `combobox` answer of 2026-11-10).** Amendments 2026-11-03 (4)'s "no `menu` module" stands for plain Menu. Menu's filter mode gets its own component module, `menu-filter`, row added to §1.
- *Handles.* One per part that owns a `MenuFilterTwin`, joined by a key C# generates per filterable root. All five are built (the `trigger`, `popup` and `clear` handles on 2026-11-12):
  - `input`: composition, the committed-value writes, the key router over Base UI's `getMenuFilterKeyAction` table, `data-highlighted` and the WebKit `aria-selected`;
  - `trigger`: the relay of the vertical arrows and typed characters while the popup is open, off in a menubar;
  - `list`: the `mousedown` default, the hand-back of focus and the key replay onto the Input, and the item texts;
  - `popup`: the background `mousedown`, the pointer's refocus of the Input, focus on the popup moved to the Input, and the cross-axis close key.
  - `clear` (added 2026-11-12): the Clear's `mousedown` default and the Input's refocus on a click. The Clear re-mounts as the query fills, so its handle follows each element.
- *Imports.* It imports `composite`'s exported orientation predicates and reads the session through `interactions`' exports. Corrected 2026-11-12 (the final review): "it edits no mechanism module" was true when written. The track's mechanism edits are the additive `composite` exports and list options below and the Composite C# members named in the bullets that follow. Each leaves a plain list's behaviour as it was.
- *A filter step renders the popup once (2026-11-12, ADR 16 rule 2; the final review's fix 6, under the orchestrator's `CompositeRootBehavior` grant):* `CompositeRootBehavior.DeferRenders()` and `ResumeRenders()` hold back the render an item's removal requests, and the outermost resume runs one. The filter defers the popup's list root (`MenuFilterRootContext.ListRoot`, set by a filterable `MenuPopupBehavior` while mounted) around its recompute and its text reports. One keystroke that hides ten of twenty items rendered the popup ten times before and renders it once now (`MenuFilterRenderCountTests`).
- *`composite` additions (2026-11-12, the orchestrator's grants):* `skipListNavigation`, `highlightListEdge(element, edge | null, event)`, `highlightListItem(element, item, event)` and `moveListHighlightFrom(element, item, event)` (the second runs the list's own key navigation from the item's index, for MenuFilterSubmenuRoot's moves in the parent), and the list option `itemsChangeHighlight: 'first' | 'clear' | 'auto'` (MNU-STA-20; FilterDropdownRoot.tsx:109-125). With the option, an item change that puts another item under the highlighted index moves the highlight to the first item or clears it before writing or reporting. `auto` does `first` while the list's reference input holds a non-empty trimmed query. A filterable menu's list sends it from `ApplyFilterListOptions`; plain lists send none. The agreed values were `first` and `clear`; `auto` was added because C# learns a typed query a round trip later.
- *`composite` list option `floatingActiveDescendant` (2026-11-12, the orchestrator's grant, option (a) under the user's "Do recommended"):* `true` by default; `false` leaves the floating element out of the virtual-focus `aria-activedescendant` writer, so only the reference carries it. A filterable menu's list sends `false` from `ApplyFilterListOptions`, since its popup is a dialog, which carries none (MNU-ARIA-06, `useMenuFilterPopup.ts:97-101`); C#'s `ListNavigationBehavior.FloatingActiveDescendant` writes the option only when `false`, and plain lists send none.
- *Consumer key handlers in a filterable menu (2026-11-12, the orchestrator's settlement, option (a) under the user's "Do recommended"; MNU-API-59, EVT-14, EVT-15):* `CompositeRootBehavior.DecidedKeysMayPropagate` makes every `navigate` decision of a root mark the consumer's handler as run, for a root whose own module lets decided keys reach Blazor (Menu's filter input lets its editing keys through), so Blazor's dispatch does not run it again. The `menu-filter` Input reports each editing key it keeps (`OnOwnedKey`) before Blazor dispatches it, only while the popup has a consumer key handler (`reportsOwnedKeys`); a filterable `MenuPopup` takes that mark once and skips its consumer, as Base UI's router stops the key.
- *Writers (§2).*
  - The Input's `data-highlighted` is the module's, mirrored in `MenuInputState.Highlighted` (design point 5 (a)).
  - The Input's `value` is C# → JS, on the `Input`/`Field.Control` precedent of Amendments 2026-10-22.
  - The highlighted item's `aria-selected` under WebKit is the module's: the Input handle mirrors `composite`'s `aria-activedescendant` into it while the Input has focus and `engine.webkit` holds, before paint (design point 5 (c)).
- *Decision point (built 2026-11-12).* The `menu-filter` twin declares `navigate`, the consumer's `onKeyDown` veto on the Input's router and on the Trigger's relay. MenuInput and MenuTrigger point its decider at `CompositeRootBehavior.DecideKeyDownOnce`, the orchestrator's 2026-11-12 grant: it runs the composite decision once per keydown and serves that verdict to composite's own handle for the same event, keyed by the event's type, timestamp and key. It falls back to `run`, and C# runs the consumer's handler once per event and caches the verdict, as on the `combobox` twin (Amendments 2026-11-10). The implementing commits record the verdict paths and ADR 01's lines.
- *Reports.* `OnValueChange` (`MenuFilterValueChange`: the input's text, `input-change` or `input-clear`, the event), `OnTexts` (`MenuFilterTexts`: ids and texts, design point 2 (b′)), `OnHighlightedChange` and `OnFocusOut` (Shift+Tab, a `focus-out` close request).

**2026-11-11, the `context-menu` module as built (M5 track 7, [issue 194](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/194); design points 1–3 of M5 track 7, settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-11 under the user's "Do recommended for decisions"; ADR 04 Amendments 2026-10-18 and 2026-11-03 (3), (4)).**
- *Two roles on one `ContextMenuTwin` each.* `root` is attached by token (Amendments 2026-10-27's token form). `trigger` is attached on the Trigger's element. Each part has its own twin.
- *Shared state.* The shared state lives in the menu's interactions session, under `blazix.contextMenu` in its `dataRef.current`: the consumer Backdrop's, the internal backdrop's and the root Positioner's elements, the armed flag as last written, and whether a gesture happened. No page-global state.
- *Attach order.* Both roles take a reference on the session at attach (`acquireSession`), so the order in which they and the session's own handle attach does not matter. An L4 title holding `js/interactions.<hash>.js` pins this (red on WebAssembly before the rule).
- *The root's attach.* Unless a gesture came first, it calls `armMenuRelease(token, true, rules)` with `initialPoint: null`, and `setSessionPositionReference(token, {0, 0, 0, 0})` (CTX-STA-04, STA-06, EDGE-06).
- *The release rules.* `{ button: 2, skipButton: mac ? null : 2, slop: 1, initialPoint }`, with the macOS flag read at dispatch.
- *Options.* `ContextMenuOptions { Part, Token, RootId, Disabled, Handlers, Backdrop, InternalBackdrop, Positioner }`. The last three are reference indexes, sent by `update` whenever an element changes (design point 2 (a)).
- *Reports.*
  - `OnOpen` (`ContextMenuOpenRequest(X, Y, PointerType, Event)`): C# requests the open with `trigger-press`, the event and no trigger, and never writes the open interaction type.
  - `OnCancelOpen` (`ContextMenuCancelOpen(Event)`): C# closes the tree with `cancel-open`.
  - `OnPress` (`ContextMenuPress`): on Server.
- *The decision point `press`* (fallback `run`), for the consumer handlers the Trigger declines (`oncontextmenu`, `ontouchstart`, `ontouchmove`, `ontouchend`, `ontouchcancel`), after the synchronous `OnPressing` hand-over of the coordinates, the button and the touches (design point 3 (a)). It is asked at each `contextmenu` and `touchstart` of a supplied handler, and at a touch move, end or cancel only while a long press is pending; any other dispatch of a supplied handler runs it from an `OnPress` report, as nothing remains to veto.
  - On Server, the same event is reported ahead of the open it may veto.
  - Base UI's handler stops the propagation of `contextmenu` and of a single-touch `touchstart`, which hides them from Blazor's delegated dispatch.
- *Composition.* It composes `interactions` (`acquireSession`, `releaseSession`, `armMenuRelease`, `setSessionPositionReference`), `dom`, `decide`, `event-info`, `platform` and `twin`. No mechanism module changed.
- *§2.* The items' `tabindex` and `data-highlighted` remain the list handle's on ContextMenu's item shells (the 2026-11-03 row).

**2026-11-10, the `select` component module as built and its settlements (M5 track 9, [issue 173](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/173); design points 1–3 settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-04, the user told and free to overturn; the `dom.ts` and `positioning.ts` grants of the same day).** (1) *Scope.* `js/select.ts` attaches one `SelectTwin` handle per part (`part`: `positioner`, `list`, `scroll-arrow`, `trigger`). The positioner's handle places the popup over the trigger (SEL-POS-07's steps), grows it as the list scrolls, closes it on a window resize, runs the scroll arrows' visibility (`data-visible`, reported once per change) and delegates the items' activation (`[role="option"]`) with the 400 ms press-and-release gates; on WebAssembly it hands the item over (`OnActivating`) and asks the `activate` point inside the click, on Server it reports `OnActivate`. The list's handle takes the List's style handover; a scroll arrow's handle auto-scrolls every 40 ms; the trigger's handle reports a release more than 5 px off the trigger and the popup (`OnCancelOpen`) and the trigger's blur with whether focus moved into the popup. (2) *Design point 1 (a).* The style writer's `aligned` band is the last band, over `auto-resize`, written by the `select` module (the positioner's placement, the popup's and list's aligned layout, `--transform-origin`); while told aligned, the `positioning` handle's own layer stands down to `position: fixed`, `opacity: 0` until its first pass, and usePositioner's `pointer-events: none` while closed (SelectPositioner.tsx:115), on the same switch as `data-side="none"`. (3) *Design point 2, channel (i).* The `select` module decides when aligned placement is active (C#'s mounted state, the latched `AlignItemWithTrigger`, the open method, and its own fallback, reported as `OnAlignedChange`) and calls `setAligned(element, aligned, onPositioned?)` on the Positioner element; the `positioning` report keeps the resolved side, which the Trigger's `data-popup-side` renders. (4) *Design point 3 (a).* An autofill report force-mounts the items, then C# matches the value and label strings after that batch; the rendered-label clause of SEL-FRM-18 waits for a read-only `composite` export of the registry's labels, granted once `js/composite.ts` is free (the Server round trip it adds gets its ADR 01 row then). (5) *The open of a kept-mounted popup.* The Root sends a commit's open to the session and the list without a render of the Root (ADR 01 Amendments 2026-10-27), except an open of a popup kept mounted while closed whose list handle is attached: that open goes out from the Positioner's next after-render, once its element shows the open state, because the list reads its items' visibility when it learns of the open (CMP-ALG-03, CMP-STA-37), as Base UI's effects follow the commit's render.

**2026-11-10, the end of the starting phase runs a positioning pass with the adaptive origin on ([issue 207](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/207); settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-10).** The `positioning` module's `update` runs one pass when it ends the popup's starting phase, but only while the Positioner is mounted and positioned with `adaptiveOrigin` on. Its tracking may restart in the same update; that runs a pass already, so the module adds none. During the phase the transitions layer writes `transition: none` (FLT-ANI-05), so each pass of the phase sees no transition and anchors a left or top placement from the near edge (FLT-ANI-03, FLT-ALG-12). In Base UI the last pass of an open follows the end of the phase: `useTransitionStatus.ts:64-68` ends it a frame after the open, and autoUpdate (`@floating-ui/dom` 1.8.0, `floating-ui.dom.mjs:639-660`) re-observes the floating element a frame after its first resize delivery, whose delivery runs a pass. In the port the end of the phase arrives with C#'s `update`. On Server that takes a circuit round trip, so it can land after the tracking's last pass, and the far-edge anchoring was never written. That was issue 207's flake on Tooltip's Viewport title, seen three times on Server. Nothing broader changes: a phase that ends without the adaptive origin runs no pass, since no pass reads the transition then (FLT-ANI-03's dated note).

**2026-11-11, a typeahead-only list handle (CMB-KEY-12; option (a), settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-11 under the user's "Do recommended for decisions"; written by M5 track 10 under the `js/composite.ts` grant).** A list handle whose `CompositeOptions.typeaheadOnly` is set (C#: `CompositeRootBehavior.TypeaheadOnly`) handles none of the list navigation's keys and runs its list's typeahead only while the list is closed; a closed match is the existing `OnListMatch` report. A handle may send its own `list.typeahead` with the list's other options unchanged (C#: `CompositeRootBehavior.TypeaheadSource`). The Combobox trigger joins its list this way, in the trigger role, and runs Base UI's `useTypeahead` (`ComboboxTrigger.tsx:107-122`) over the root's labels (the derived labels with `items`, else the force-mounted items' registered ones), while the input's reference handle runs no typeahead. Proven at L3 (`a typeahead-only reference (port, CMB-KEY-12)`, two titles) and L2 ("port: the trigger's typeahead-only list handle matches the derived labels, and a closed match selects the value with reason none").

**2026-11-11, a Chip's consumer handlers in its own handle (CMB-KEY-10, CMB-PTR-08; option (a), settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-11 under the user's "Do recommended for decisions"; written by M5 track 10; the shape of the 2026-11-10 entry's `navigate` and `press`).** The `combobox` module stops a chip's keys on the Chips container and decides the Chips' press there, before Blazor's document-level dispatch, so a Chip's consumer `onkeydown` and `onmousedown` never ran; Base UI runs them first, and their `preventBaseUIHandler()` skips the chip's own handling and the Chips' press (`ComboboxChip.tsx:100-127` with mergeProps, `handleInputPress.ts:14-16`). A Chip with either handler declines both and attaches its own `combobox` handle, part `chip`. On its element the handle asks `navigate` for a keydown and `press` for a mousedown (fallback `run`), a descendant's keydown that was not stopped included. On WebAssembly C# runs the handler inside the dispatch, and a `PreventPartHandler()` marks the event, which the Chips' key handling and press skip. On Server the handle reports the event (`OnChipEvent`); the module's handling has run, and the handler runs a round trip later without a veto (ADR 01's "C#-origin consumer vetoes" row). The handle is the chip's own, not an index on the Chips' handle, so the decision needs no field beyond `decide`'s. A chip without either handler attaches none. Proven at L3 ("port: a chip handle asks navigate for a key and press for a mousedown, and a stop verdict makes the chips skip their key and their press", "port: on Server a chip handle reports its key and its press, and the chips handle them") and L2 (the twin contract's chip part; "port: a chip's consumer onkeydown and onmousedown run inside its own handle's navigate and press points, and PreventPartHandler() stops the chip's handling", "port: on Server a chip's consumer onkeydown runs from its own handle's report").

**2026-11-11, Escape dismissal counts a press-requested open and an open popup without its node yet, and C# answers a declined press (`menu.others.spec.ts:205` on Server, PR 233's run 42954678792; options (b) and (b1), settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-11 under the user's "Do recommended for decisions"; the user may overturn them).** On Server a dialog opened from a menu item rendered before the forest knew it, and an Escape there closed the menu and the dialog with it.
- *The finding.* A probe at the Escape's keydown under a 250 ms circuit delay found two windows after the dialog's render:
  - the session's own update, which brings C#'s commit of the press, arrives a round trip later;
  - the dialog's node joins the forest with its floating element's handle, later still. At the keydown the session was open and its node absent, so the menu's blocking-child check (FLT-KEY-02) found no child. In Base UI the popup is a node from its render.
- *The `interactions` module.*
  - A press that opens a closed popup records its request (`pressOpen`). While that request is the pending one, the popup counts as open for Escape dismissal (`isOpenForDismissal`), and its session installs its Escape and composition listeners, not its outside-press ones, so the opening press can never count as outside its own popup.
  - The relay's commit, or an answer, ends the count. On WebAssembly the decision answers inside the press, so the count ends there.
  - The keydown's forest record (the Amendments 2026-11-03 (5) rule) records a popup that counts as open for dismissal and has no node yet under the parent token its root sent, so it blocks its parent as Base UI's node does. A kept-mounted popup's existing node counts as open the same way.
  - Outside presses keep the committed state and the live forest.
- *C#, (b1) as built.* A trigger answers a press its session declined, canceled by the consumer's callback or vetoed by its handler, through the session's answered counter, after the press ran (`InteractionsSessionBehavior.AnswerDeclinedPressAsync`; on both modes).
  - An accepted press is answered by its commit's relay.
  - An accepted press that changes nothing, a click on an open NavigationMenu item, is not answered. Its click keeps its upgrade of the open event, as Base UI's `dispatchOpenChange` keeps it (`FloatingRootStore.ts:91-107`). Base UI syncs the open event only after an accepted change (`PopoverStore.ts:139-145`), so a declined press's answer rolling the event back is Base UI's order (gap 16, FLT-STA-03's dated note).
  - The first build answered every press. Full L4 found `navigation-menu.spec.ts:1939` red 5 of 5 on Server, since a redundant click's answer read as a cancel, and the answer was narrowed to declined presses.
  - A trigger reaches its session by the token its handle joined, keyed under the runtime, since tokens are drawn per circuit on Server. No family's wiring changes.
- *Tests.*
  - [L4] "port: does not close the parent menu when Escape is pressed in a nested dialog that has just opened, under circuit latency on Server", Server only.
  - [L3] "port: on Server an Escape while a nested popup's press-requested open awaits C# is that popup's, and once C# cancels the press it is the parent's", and "port: an open nested popup whose floating element's handle has not attached yet blocks its parent's Escape, under the parent token its root sent".
  - [L2] the committed, canceled and vetoed presses of `InteractionsBehaviorTests`.

**2026-11-11, a menubar trigger keeps the tree armed (design point 1 (a) of M5 track 8, [issue 195](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/195); settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-11 under the user's "Do recommended for decisions"; written with `js/interactions.ts` held by session M).** `MenuTriggerOptions.keepArmedWhenClosed` (`InteractionsMenuTriggerOptions.KeepArmedWhenClosed`, sent only when true): when the trigger's open ends, the tree's release slot is not disarmed; the document `mouseup` listener is still removed. Base UI resets `allowMouseUpTriggerRef` for a menu that is not open only when its trigger has no parent (`menu/trigger/MenuTrigger.tsx:120-124`; MBR §16 Q9 replicated). A menubar's trigger sends it, and sends `release` too, so a bar menu has press-drag-release and the hover open's `cancel-open` (MBR-STA-07).

**2026-11-11, a hover handle runs the entry the pointer made before it attached (design point 5 (a) of M5 track 8, [issue 195](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/195); settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-11 under the user's "Do recommended for decisions"; the user may overturn it; written with `js/interactions.ts` held by track 8).**
- *The gap.* On Server a trigger's handle attaches a round trip after its element appears. A probe under a 40 ms one-way delay hovered the Layout submenu trigger of MBR-PTR-07's shared-Root setups 35–46 ms after it entered the page (4 runs). The `mouseover`, `mouseenter` and `mousemove` reached no listener, and nothing opened until the pointer moved again. Base UI attaches its listeners in an effect React flushes before the next input (`useHoverReferenceInteraction.ts:189-397`), so it never misses the entry.
- *The record.* The module keeps one record per document of the last `mouseover`, with the pointer type of the `pointerover` before it (capture listeners, added by the first hover handle in that document). A `mouseout` into an element outside the record's target, or out of the window, clears it.
- *The trigger side.* At its attach only, a hover reference handle whose hover is on takes a record whose target is inside its trigger, and consumes it. It runs its live listeners in the browser's order: the pointer type, the nested-trigger hover, NavigationMenu's lock, the enter, the first-move enter (taken once per install, as the hook's `once` listener), then the move. Each gets a view of the recorded event with the trigger as `currentTarget` and the type the listener handles (`mouseover`, `mouseenter`, `mousemove`). So every live guard applies: the mouse-only pointer type, the disabled gate, the delays and rest time counted from the attach, the delay group and the locks. A reinstall for an update replays nothing. A trigger inserted under a resting pointer gets no `mouseover` until the pointer moves, so it does not open, as in Base UI.
- *The floating side* (session V's note of 2026-11-10). At its attach, a floating hover handle whose target holds the recorded entry runs its enter (FLT-PTR-23), which cancels a close the trigger's leave started. It reads without consuming, so a trigger inside the popup that attaches later still takes the entry.
- *Tests.* [L3] `interactions.test.ts` › `useHoverReferenceInteraction` › "port: an entry before the hover handle attaches" (six titles, each guard paired with a replay that does happen), and › `useHoverFloatingInteraction` "port: a floating handle that attaches after the pointer has entered the popup cancels the pending close". [L4] `menubar.spec.ts` "should respect closeOnClick on nested items when the menu was opened on hover" on the shared-Root setups on Server (the `fixme` lifted).

**2026-11-12, Autocomplete's replicated quirks and Writer rows (M5 track 11, [issue 196](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/196); decision 14 of the M5 plan, chosen by the user 2026-11-03; the Writer tables of Amendments 2026-10-29).** Autocomplete replicates Base UI's §16 quirks: no suffix selection for the completion (Q1, `apple` + `b` → `appleb`); the hidden selection memory's two effects (Q3, the closed Escape's stop and, without `items`, the selected index); `AutoHighlight` with the completion fills the first match (Q4); the completion counts as the value (Q5); an inline list without a Popup never fills (Q7). Its parts' JavaScript-written attributes are Combobox's (`autocomplete.md` §5.1 and §5.5, 21 ownership-registry rows); the Input's `value` is C# → JS, written by the `combobox` module from `update` (Amendments 2026-11-03 (6)), and the inline completion reaches it the same way. No module changes: the `combobox` module serves both families.

**2026-11-18, the M6 module scopes and ownership rows (M6 planning; chosen by the user 2026-11-18, the M6 plan's decisions 4, 5, 6, 7 and 21; ADR 12 Amendments 2026-11-18).** Written before the M6 briefs, so every brief quotes one text. The families still write their own `Writer` cells and ownership-registry entries, and the implementing pull requests name the options, payloads and report fields. The body above is corrected in place where its tables hold the row, each edited cell marked "amended 2026-11-18" or "added 2026-11-18". Those edits are: the `Affects` header; §1's component-module row; §2's Drawer, ScrollArea and Slider rows and its sentence on `style`; §3's decision-point list and fallback policies; and pointers on the `otp-field` and `toast` rows of the 2026-10-18 table and on the `data-swiping` row of the 2026-10-20 table. No module is added: the seven M6 modules are already named, and a module's scope is its table cell.

- *Module scopes (decision 4 (a); the placement rule of Amendments 2026-10-18).* The four cells read as follows; this table supersedes the `otp-field` and `toast` rows of the 2026-10-18 table and refines §1's component-module row for `scroll-area` and `number-field`.

| Module | Owns | Doc |
|---|---|---|
| `toast` (component) | Toast's own swipe gesture and its movement variables; the Root's `auto`-height measurement and the Content observers (`--toast-height`, TST-ANI-03); the Viewport's window `focus` and `blur`, hover, <kbd>F6</kbd>, touch-outside and `:focus-visible` listeners; the focus hand-off. The focus guards stay the `portal` module's, and the `Writer` cells stay as `toast.md` gives them | toast |
| `number-field` (component) | the *input role*: the visible input's `keydown` and `beforeinput` gate, paste, caret, the native `wheel` listener with `{ passive: false }` (NUM-PTR-07), the touch scrub; the *scrub role*: the scrub area's pointer lock and its cursor. Press-and-hold stays the `button` module's (UBT-ALG-07) | number-field |
| `otp-field` (component) | per-slot keydown, paste, composition and input handling: selection ranges, the application of C#'s answer to an edit (C# owns normalization and value distribution, OTP-STA-04/07/08 and `NormalizeValue`; the module reports the edit and writes the slot values C# answers), focus movement, DOM value restore; reports one value change per edit | otp-field |
| `scroll-area` (component) | the measurement of SCR §4.6, run synchronously on each Viewport `scroll` (SCR-PTR-33); the thumb-drag loop (§6.2.1) and its pointer-capture bookkeeping (SCR-PTR-01, -09, -11); the track press and the native `wheel` listener on the Scrollbar (§6.2.2, §6.2.3); the hover and touch-modality tracking and the programmatic-scroll marker (SCR-STA-13..16, SCR-PTR-27..32); the two per-axis 500 ms scrolling timers and the 100 ms restore timer (SCR-STA-11, -14); the `ResizeObserver`s on the Viewport and the Content with their first-delivery rules (SCR-ANI-04, -05); the 0 ms wait over `getFiniteAnimations(viewport, { subtree: true })` (SCR-ANI-06); the scroll-snap save and restore (SCR-PTR-03); the non-inheriting property registration (SCR-DOM-12); and the writes of the ScrollArea rows of §2 | scroll-area |

- *Ownership list (decision 4 (a); corrections of record).* §2's single ScrollArea row claimed `data-hovering` on the Thumb, Viewport and Root and the corner size variables on all three. In 1.9.0 `data-hovering` is on the Scrollbar only (SCR-DOM-16), the thumb size variables sit on the Scrollbar (SCR-DOM-20) and on the Thumb during overscroll (SCR-DOM-25), and the corner variables sit on the Root (SCR-DOM-02). §2 now has one row per part, adding the Scrollbar, Content and Corner rows (`scroll-area.md` §5's `Writer` tables are the effective list). This is the amendment that `scroll-area.md` §16 Q13's "ADR 04 §2 as amended" names. The overflow `data-*` attributes stay C#-projected from mirrored state. §2's Slider row lists the whole `style` of Thumb and Indicator, as `slider.md` §16 Q16 decided on 2026-10-18: the centre-mode properties of SLD-DOM-05 and SLD-DOM-09 are added, and `--end-position` is dropped because the component never sets it (SLD-DOM-06, `slider.md` §16 Q13). The 2026-10-20 `data-swiping` row ("every Drawer part … uniform") is corrected:

| Part(s) | `data-swiping` | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `Drawer.Popup`, `Drawer.SwipeArea` | JavaScript's | mirrored: `swiping` is a state field of both (DRW-DOM-06, DRW-DOM-26) |
| `Drawer.Backdrop` | JavaScript's | imperative: the `drawer` module toggles it during the gesture, and no state field mirrors it (DRW-DOM-20) |
| `Drawer.Root`, `Drawer.Indent`, `Drawer.IndentBackground` | none | the Root renders no element; the Indent states are `{ active }` |

  `--drawer-keyboard-inset` stays JavaScript's (the 2026-10-20 row: the `drawer` module follows `visualViewport`, and C# holds no field for it); `drawer.md`'s `C#` cell is the stale one and carries a dated correction. The `Toast.Root` row is unchanged.

- *The `Affects` header (decision 4 (a)).* It gains TST and OTP, and the eight swipe rows it omitted: PSH-DOM-17, PSH-DOM-18, PSH-EVT-09, PSH-I18N-02 and PSH-EDGE-10..13.

- *Two component bands in `dom`'s style writer (decision 5, revised by the user 2026-11-18 before commit, "Yes, two bands").* `StyleLayerName` gains two shared bands: `'component-under'`, ranked below the consumer band (after `transitions`), and `'component'`, ranked above it (after `aligned`). Each component module writes each `style` property through the band that matches Base UI's precedence for it: a value Base UI passes through the part's merged `style` prop, which the consumer's `style` overrides, goes to `'component-under'` (Slider's Thumb and Indicator, SLD-API-25; ScrollArea's state-rendered corner and thumb-size variables and the Corner's size; Toast's swipe movement, `transition` and `transform`); a value Base UI writes imperatively with `style.setProperty` or `element.style` after render, which overrides the consumer's `style`, goes to `'component'` (ScrollArea's Thumb `transform`, its overflow variables and the Viewport's `scroll-snap-type` during a drag; Toast's measurement `height`). A property Base UI writes both ways (Drawer's `--drawer-swipe-progress`: a resting value in the merged style and an imperative one in the gesture) holds a value in each band, and the writer's rank decides. (Examples corrected 2026-11-18, before any brief was filed: the first text put all of ScrollArea's measured values and Drawer's and Toast's in-gesture values over the consumer, against the rule above; the M6 brief writers read the Base UI source per property, and the rule is unchanged.) Each brief names the band of every property it writes, from the Base UI source. No element carries two component modules' styles, so neither band collides. Track 1 adds both, one `dom.ts` commit with its red test (the plan's decision 3). The single `'component'` band chosen first was dropped because one band cannot rank both below and above the consumer; the other alternatives were one band per module (four edits to a closed union and a longer order) and direct `style.setProperty` outside the writer (a second writer on the element, against §2).

  *As built (M6 track 1, [issue 272](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/272), 2026-11-18; the order chosen by the user 2026-11-18).* `js/dom.ts` holds the bands in two lists, `underConsumer = ['positioning', 'transitions', 'component-under']` and `overConsumer = ['positioning-measured', 'auto-resize', 'aligned', 'component']`. The consumer's rank is `underConsumer.length` and the writer keeps eight sources, so the effective order is `positioning` < `transitions` < `component-under` < consumer < `positioning-measured` < `auto-resize` < `aligned` < `component`. No consumer module changed, and the seven earlier `styleWriter` titles are unchanged and green. Four titles in `js/dom.test.ts` prove the bands: "places the component-under band under the consumer and over the transitions band", "places the component band last: over the consumer, the aligned band and every other band", "keeps a component write over a later consumer style, and yields a component-under write to it" and "shows the component value of a property held in both bands, and falls back to the component-under value when the component band removes it".

- *Optimistic moves and stale C# updates: one rule (decision 6 (a); the class of issue 271; the precedent `CompositeHighlightRequest(int? Index, int Revision)`).* Every module that moves optimistically stamps each report with a revision. Those moves are the Slider thumb, Drawer's swipe close and snap point, Toast's swipe release, NumberField's scrub, and OtpField's typing under variant (ii) below. An `update` older than the module's last applied act is ignored unless it carries a rollback. Each family's first red L4 title is a `holdCircuit` title: hold after a report, act again, release, and assert no snap-back. OtpField takes variant (ii). For built-in validation with no `NormalizeValue` and no controlled value, the module writes the slot, moves focus and reports, and a cancelled or stale request is rolled back by `update`. A controlled or custom-normalized field keeps focus following the committed value (OTP-FOC-10 as written). The ADR 01 relaxation row and the visible deviations on OTP-FOC-10 and OTP-EVT-04 are written by OtpField's implementing pull request. The alternatives were ignoring `update` during a gesture except a cancel (a legitimate consumer correction waits for the release) and no guard (issue 271's defect class on Server in four families).
  - Dated note (2026-11-24, M6 track 10, [issue 271](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/271); the rule as written, so no design point, settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-24): `composite` is the rule's second adopter, for the highlight. Each highlight an event commits is an act, numbered where it is committed, since its report can wait for the registry. Acts are numbered from one module-wide sequence, so the acts of a list attached again come after those of its earlier state, whose highest C# keeps (the gap the orchestrator's verification of PR 348 found). Every `CompositeListHighlight` report carries the count (`Act`), and `ListNavigationBehavior` stamps the highest it received on each `CompositeHighlightRequest(int? Index, int Revision, int? Act)`. The module consumes a request older than its last act without applying it, and reports its own highlight under the request's revision, so C#'s mirror takes it. No request carries a rollback. Red first: [L4] `menu.filter-items.spec.ts` "port: a C# highlight reset held past a user ArrowDown keeps the user's highlight" (Server, held), [L3] `composite-list.test.ts` "port: a C# highlight request made before a newer user highlight loses to it, and one made after it applies".

- *Consumer vetoes inside the M6 loops (decision 7 (a)).* §3 gains three decision points, each with fallback **run**:
  - `snap-point` and `dismiss` (Drawer). The `drawer` module resolves the snap list and owns the release, so the algorithm has one implementation (ADR 01 decision 2). DRW-STA-05/06/07 are retagged `[L3]`, and C# holds only the active snap value. A cancel rolls back through `update`.
  - `edit` (OtpField), answered synchronously on WebAssembly. C# owns normalization and distribution (fact sheet D Q-OTP-1 (c)).

  Slider keeps §3's two points: the thumb `keydown` with fallback **run**, and the Control `pointerdown` with fallback **start**. NumberField's character gate runs in the `number-field` module, fed a C#-computed allowed set on `attach` and `update`, and C# re-validates the text (fact sheet D Q-NUM-4 (a)). ScrollArea gets no decision point and joins ADR 01's C#-origin consumer-veto row. The alternatives were cancels through `update` only for Drawer and OtpField (WebAssembly loses a synchronous `Cancel()`) and C# deciding every OtpField and NumberField key through `decide()` (on Server a rejected character shows for a round trip and the caret jumps).

- *Drawer's exceptions to §2 and its edits to M4 modules (decision 21 (a)).*
  - `interactions` gains an additive export on the session record, `setOutsidePressGate`, which the `drawer` module calls synchronously from the SwipeArea gesture (DLG-PTR-05/11, DRW-DIS-02), with L4 titles under `delayCircuit` on both modes.
  - `transitions` gains an additive `forceEnding(element)`, so the swipe release's `data-ending-style` (DRW-PTR-12) keeps one writer: the `drawer` module calls it and never writes the attribute.
  - The dated §2 exception: the Popup's `--nested-drawers` and `--drawer-frontmost-height` are rendered by C# in the commit path, as `--nested-dialogs` is (Amendments 2026-10-27), and JavaScript owns their later changes. This is an exception to "On an element with JS-owned style properties C# never renders `style`", whose sentence above now names it. An ADR 01 relaxation row covers the residual ordering on Server.
  - Session D is granted `js/interactions.ts` and `js/transitions.ts` in wave 1 for these one-commit changes. Track 10 takes `js/interactions.ts` after Drawer's PR 8b.

  The alternatives were `OutsidePress = false` sent through `update` (on Server the release click dismisses) and the `drawer` module writing `data-ending-style` itself (two writers, which the ownership lint fails).

**2026-11-18, the Drawer track's design points 1, 2 and 9 (M6 track 8, [issue 279](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/279); 1 and 9 settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-18 as the worker recommended, 2 settled as option (b) with the recovery placed in the writer; under decision 21 (a) of Amendments 2026-11-18, the token form of Amendments 2026-10-27 and the single-writer rule; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the implementing pull requests, 8b for point 2).**

1. *Twin hosts for the elementless parts (DRW-STA-13, 15, DOM-32, ALG-04/05, DIS-01).* `DrawerRoot`, `DrawerProvider` and `DrawerVirtualKeyboardProvider` get no twin. Frame-rate values travel JavaScript to JavaScript by element reference in `update` options (a child Popup handle names its parent Popup element; the Indent handles read the store the Provider's first Popup handle exposes); the keyboard role rides the Viewport handle, as Base UI's provider itself takes `viewportElement` as its root (`DrawerVirtualKeyboardProvider.tsx:96`); the CloseWatcher rides the Popup handle; presence, nested swiping and heights are reported to C# and aggregated there. Correction of the worker's premise: an elementless twin has a precedent, the token form `TwinBase.AttachTokenAsync` (`attach(null, …)`, Amendments 2026-10-27), so it would cost no `TwinBase` change; it stays the fallback if a reference-passing case cannot be expressed.
2. *The §2 exception's mechanics: C#-rendered `--nested-drawers` and `--drawer-frontmost-height` beside the style writer.* The `--nested-dialogs` precedent never met the problem: a dialog popup has no JavaScript style band (its `transitions` handle writes no `transition: none`, TRN-DOM-05), so Blazor rewriting its `style` attribute wiped nothing. A Drawer popup's `style` is gesture-written, and a C# re-render of the attribute after attach removes the writer's bands while its `applied` cache still records them. Settled: **the writer absorbs a foreign write.** `dom`'s `styleWriter` installs one `MutationObserver` on the element's `style` attribute when its first layer is written; a record the writer did not cause (the attribute text differs from the text its last write left) is handled before paint by taking the attribute's text as the new consumer text, so C#'s commit-path declarations enter the consumer band, and re-applying every layer's winners from a rebuilt cache; the writer's own writes are ignored. The exception of decision 21 (a) therefore stands as written: C# renders the two declarations in the commit path on both modes, and JavaScript's later changes go to the `'component'` band above the consumer, which the `drawer` module clears when the committed value it reported arrives in `update` (the revision rule), so the C#-rendered value shows again. The change is a granted `js/dom.ts` commit by session D in 8b, after track 1's merge, red first in `dom.test.ts` (a foreign write restores the bands; a self write is ignored; the consumer band holds the rendered declarations). The alternatives were a per-Popup observer in the `drawer` role that re-sets its own layers through the public API (two writers racing on one attribute, the pattern the single-writer rule forbids) and C# rendering the two declarations before attach only (reopens decision 21 (a): the nested count would lag the batch by a round trip on Server, which the exception exists to avoid). *Revised 2026-11-19: the writer absorbs only on opt-in (`absorbForeignWrites()`), which the `drawer` module calls on its Popup, chosen by the user 2026-11-19; the note of that date.*
3. *DRW-FOC-05 and DRW-FOC-11 say "the Root"; DRW-DOM-23, §3.10 and §2 say the Viewport element.* A fidelity correction of record under conventions §3.5 in 8c's documents commit: "the Root" becomes "the Viewport element" with a dated note and no behaviour change (`DrawerVirtualKeyboardProvider.tsx:96`, `:242`, `:311`, `:580`).

**2026-11-18, the design points of M6 tracks 1, 4, 6 and 9 (track 1 [issue 272](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/272) points 1, 2 and 4; Slider [issue 275](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/275) points 1–8; NumberField [issue 277](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/277) points 1–9; Toast [issue 280](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/280) DP2, DP6 with its addendum and DP9; settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-18, each as the worker recommended unless noted, under the M6 plan's decisions and the amendments of 2026-11-18; the user was told and may overturn; written in full by the implementing pull requests). Three points went to the user the same day: the Slider value shape (track 1 design point 3), the Toast generic surface (Toast DP5) and the names of NumberField's decision points.**

- *Track 1.* (1) `SwipeDirection` lives in `Popup/` with an internal `SwipeDirectionLiterals` beside it (the `InteractionType` precedent; PSH §4.7.5 is Popup's contract). (2) `js/swipe.ts` gets no `BlazixJsEntry` row and no twin in track 1; session D adds the entry, any `SwipeTwin` and its `BlazixJsonContext` row with the tracker in 8b, under a dated `js/CONTEXT.md` note. (4) The L4 helpers get no permanent self-titles: each is proven by a scratch run quoted in the pull request (events and `timeStamp`s on both modes), documented in `tests/browser/CONTEXT.md`, and permanently by the first consuming family's red title.
- *Slider.* (1) One `SliderTwin` handle on the Root (the Root-host precedent of Amendments 2026-10-21), the Control passed by id for `pointerdown` and `touchstart`; `keydown`, `input`, `change`, `focusin` and `focusout` listened on the Root, delegated, because SLD-STR-05 makes keyboard work for a thumb anywhere under the Root; one `SliderOptions` record (values, bounds, flags, `ThumbCollisionBehavior`, per-thumb input ids and disabled flags, the revision); C# owns the commit value and fires `OnValueCommitted` from the gesture-end report (SLD-EVT-12). (2) `Rtl` from `DirectionProviderContext.Read` on `attach` and `update` with a listener (the `CompositeRootBehavior` reader); SLD-EDGE-13 holds. (3) One selector subscription per part (ADR 16 rules 7 and 8), proven by `ClassValue` counts. (4) `FormLabelOptions.ControlId` is the field's control id inside a Field, else the lone registered thumb's input id when exactly one thumb is registered, else none; the SLD-STR-05 difference (Base UI counts `input[type=range]` inside the Control, the registry counts thumbs under the Root) is a dated note on SLD-FOC-06. (5) `matchesFocusVisible` is imported from `interactions`, as `select.ts` imports `isVirtualClick`. (6) **`EventTargetInfo` gains `Value : string?` (JSON `value`) and `Name : string?` (JSON `name`)**, filled only by the `slider` module's snapshot of its own input through a target override on `js/event-info.ts`'s snapshot builder; `Value` is the candidate as JSON text (a number literal, or an array literal in range mode); every other family's snapshot leaves them null, which the `WhenWritingNull` policy keeps off the wire; two additive granted commits (`Events/EventSnapshot.cs`, `js/event-info.ts`), red first. SLD-EVT-04 therefore holds (a dated note says "redefined `target`" is the snapshot's target). (7) Three spec files (`slider.root`, `slider.control`, `slider.field`) plus `slider.helpers.ts`; the `input-change` title sets `input.value` and dispatches `input` in the page (the `list-values.spec.ts` autofill precedent), dated note on SLD-EVT-02. (8) The sample: the hero plus a "Try it" block.
- *NumberField.* (1) Decision points, as ADR 01's C#-origin consumer-veto row already requires for NUM-KEY-11, NUM-EVT-14, NUM-PTR-02 and NUM-FOC-04: on the `number-field` twin for the input's `keydown`, `paste`, `focus`, `blur` and `change` (fallback **run**) and for the scrub `pointerdown` (fallback **start**), and on the `button` twin a hold-start point for the stepper `pointerdown` (fallback **run**); the names are the user's and were proposed the same day (`input-key`, `input-paste`, `input-focus`, `input-blur`, `input-change`, `scrub-start`, `hold-start`). (2) 6a ships a minimal `js/number-field.ts` (attach, the `value` handover, focus and blur reports with the `defaultPrevented` checks) and its `BlazixJsEntry`; 6b adds the gate, paste, wheel and scrub. (3) `NumberFormatOptions` carries the five Intl digit options (`MinimumIntegerDigits`, `MinimumFractionDigits`, `MaximumFractionDigits`, `MinimumSignificantDigits`, `MaximumSignificantDigits`) and `CurrencyDisplay`, because the pinned tests pass them; the markup shorthand is `Format="@("#,##0.00")"` (ADR 08 Amendments 2026-11-18). (4) `PlatformFlags.Ios`: one additive line each on `readPlatformFlags` (`js/interactions.ts`) and `PlatformFlagsPayload`, granted to session N and coordinated with session D, which holds `interactions.ts` in wave 1 and rebases over it; `inputmode` is `numeric` until the read lands, under a relaxation row like MNU-A11Y-01's. (5) `list-values.spec.ts:135-154` and its `loaded` filter are restated with a dated note, because the fixture host's full ICU data (decision 11 (a)) makes `tr-TR` predefined on WebAssembly; the root-collation fallback stays a consumer fact in ADR 01; granted on M5 track 4's spec. (6) The `form` module keeps the hidden input's control role unchanged; the `number-field` module takes the autofill `change` (NUM-FRM-05) and the caret-at-end focus redirect (NUM-FOC-01/02); no `form.ts` edit. (7) The visible input's `value` Writer becomes `C# → JS` with a dated correction (as OTP-DOM-08 reads); every input report carries a revision that `update`'s text echoes and the module ignores an older one: decision 6's rule, widened from the scrub to typing. (8) The scrub cursor as specified (NUM-STR-06, NUM-DOM-13/14 relaxation rows). (9) Text-control helpers (`setCaret`, `selectAll`, a value write that survives Blazor's diff, a composition tracker) in `dom.ts`, one granted commit after track 1 merges, no refactor of `combobox.ts`, `menu-filter.ts` or `composite.ts`; sequenced by rebase with session D's writer commit (the Drawer settlement above).
- *Toast.* (DP2) One internal `ToastStore<TData> : ComponentStore<ToastStoreState<TData>>` per Provider: the record holds the list, the derived metadata, `Hovering`, `Focused`, `IsWindowFocused`, `Timeout` and `Limit`; timers, the paused flag, the element handles and the previous focus are non-notifying fields; each Root has its own selectors with an argument. (DP6 and its addendum) Presence tracking: each consumer property's `init` accessor sets its bit in a private presence mask held in a struct whose `Equals` is always true and whose hash is 0, so equality and printing ignore it; the store clears the mask on the copies it stores; updates and upserts apply key by key, as Base UI's spread does (TST-STA-05, STA-27); TST-STA-19's "timeout present" is the `Timeout` bit; the promise success and error updates assign `Timeout` explicitly (STA-16); the add input is the record itself (`Add(new ToastObject { Title = "Saved" })`). The alternatives were replacing every field (a deviation on STA-05) and a separate options type with optional members (a second public shape). (DP9) A port L2 title, "does not sync provider props from a parameter set that fails before OnParametersSet", with dated notes on TST-API-04 and TST-EDGE-09 naming the analogue.

**2026-11-18, NumberField's decision-point names (chosen by the user 2026-11-18, "Do recommended", relayed from the grilling session).** The seven points of the NumberField settlement above are named `input-key`, `input-paste`, `input-focus`, `input-blur`, `input-change` and `scrub-start` on the `number-field` twin, and `hold-start` on the `button` twin; §3's decision-point list reads them as the only names for NumberField. The Slider value shape (track 1 design point 3) stays open with the user.

**2026-11-18, the NavigationMenu trigger's outside-reached guard clears the viewport's `inert` optimistically ([issue 241](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/241), M6 track 3; diagnosed by session U; approved by the orchestrator 2026-11-18 as the worker proposed; the user was told and may overturn; written by the implementing pull request with its red title).** Cause, from an instrumented Server run (3 failures in 220 at 6 workers): a <kbd>Shift+Tab</kbd> from a nested link to its trigger makes the viewport's `focusout` report `inert: true`; the trigger's `focus` runs `clearViewportInert`, finds nothing rendered yet and reports `inert: false`; C# renders `inert` from the first report and removes it only on the next render, about a round trip later; a <kbd>Tab</kbd> inside that window reaches the trigger's trailing guard from outside, whose branch only focused `viewport.guards.before`, into a viewport that is `inert`, and the focus silently failed. Base UI cannot reach the state because its trigger's `onFocus` clears it synchronously. Fix: that branch of the `navigation-menu` module calls `clearViewportInert(menu, channel)` before focusing, as the trigger's other guard branch already does, under Decision 2's optimistic convergent write (C#'s next render removes `inert` anyway). A port title beside the Base UI one, both modes, holds the `"inert": false` report with `delayCircuitMessages`, waits for the rendered `inert` on Server, then Tabs; red 8 of 8 on the base, then green, and the quarantine of issue 241 is lifted. No shared mechanism module changes.

**2026-11-19, the Drawer track's design points 3–8 and 10 (M6 track 8, [issue 279](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/279); chosen by the user 2026-11-19, "Yes, as recommended", each as the worker recommended; under the M6 plan's decisions and the amendments of 2026-11-18; written in full by the implementing pull request).**
- (3) *`swipe` has no twin and no entry.* No part attaches `js/swipe.ts`. esbuild bundles it into the `drawer` and `toast` outputs, and its L3 rows run in `js/swipe.test.ts`. For this module, §1's "one twin per module" reads "one twin per module a part attaches". No `SwipeTwin`, no `BlazixJsEntry` row and no `BlazixJsonContext` row. The alternative was a twin, an entry and an L4 host that no part uses.
- (4) *One `js/drawer.ts`*, CloseWatcher and the virtual-keyboard provider included. A second file would need a `module-graph.lint.ts` row and its own note.
- (5) *The SwipeArea's first paint on Server (DRW-PTR-29).* A `MutationObserver` on the Popup's insertion writes the gesture's movement before paint. A Server L4 title under `delayCircuit` proves it by seeing no frame at rest. If that title cannot be made deterministic, a relaxation row for one frame at rest replaces it (decision 8). C# rendering the transform was rejected.
- (6) *Rows that render another family.* DRW-PTR-19's Slider title runs over a plain `<input type="range">`, with a dated note, while Slider is absent from `main`, and over `Slider` once track 4 lands. DRW-PTR-06 and DRW-PTR-21 run over the real Combobox.
- (7) *Dialog evidence.* The Drawer re-runs its own titles and the closed DLG and ADL clauses, not `dialogRootSharedTests` or `popupFocusPropsTests`.
- (8) *Allocation in the drag loop.* The C# budgets of ADR 16 rule 7 (decision 26) apply, and review checks that the tracker allocates nothing per move. No L3 allocation spy.
- (10) *Controlled rejections with no decision point (DRW-PTR-15's next-frame check; the SwipeArea's open request, DRW-PTR-28).* The module proceeds, and C# rolls it back through `update` under the stale-update revision rule. The Server figures go in the track's relaxation rows. No third Drawer decision point.

**2026-11-19, issue 241 as fixed, and the Select aligned placement survives the items' registration ([issue 252](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/252), M6 track 3; both diagnosed by session U; approved by the orchestrator 2026-11-19 as the worker proposed; the user was told and may overturn; written by the implementing pull request with its red titles).** *Issue 241, as built:* besides the guard's `clearViewportInert` of the note above, the `navigation-menu` module observes the inline Viewport with a `MutationObserver` and lifts an `inert` that C# renders while no set is outstanding (C# renders `inert` only from the module's set report, so such a render is the stale first report landing after the clear); two orders of the same race, two port L4 titles (8 of 8 red on Server with spec-local gates on the page's SignalR messages) and two L3 titles; Server rate 3 in 220 before, 0 in 720 after; the quarantine is lifted. *Issue 252, the cause:* on Server the items' registration reaches the Positioner as an `update` whose `itemsRevision` moves 0 → 1 a round trip after the aligned placement; the `select` module's SEL-POS-13 branch then clears the aligned band's `height` (and the Popup's) and nothing places again, so the final layout is always wrong on Server (`bottom: 0` with no height, where Base UI keeps `height: 462px; bottom: 0`); the title passed by accident in two of three timings because a leftover C#-rendered `top: 0px` stretched the Positioner, and failed (25 in 1,200) when a prior floating pass had wiped that leftover. Base UI runs the same clear in `onMapChange`, but at open the map changes before Floating UI's first pass, so the clear is a no-op there. *Fix:* the `select` module skips the SEL-POS-13 clear when the registration reports the same item elements this open's placement measured; a real replacement (different elements) still clears, so SEL-POS-13 holds as written. A port L4 title asserts the aligned box matches the placement under `holdCircuit` on the revision update, plus an L3 title. Both are the recurring Server cause class of the M6 plan (a registry update a round trip after the batch) in component modules; no shared mechanism module changes.

  *Issue 252, as built (M6 track 3, 2026-11-19; the fix as approved above, its second commit reported to the orchestrator the same day).* Besides the kept heights, `place()` clears the Positioner's and Popup's `height` itself when it runs again within an open (SEL-POS-09: a re-run starts from cleared heights). The registration the fix now keeps had also cleared them before the re-placement that a late-registering selected text runs (SEL-POS-07's port deviation): `select-nav.spec.ts:328`, "aligns the first selected item with the trigger when alignItemWithTrigger is active", failed 3 of 4 without it. Rates on Server, 6 workers: 33 in 1,680 before (10 in 480 in plain runs and 23 in 1,200 instrumented; the 25 in 1,200 above was a miscount of the same runs), 0 in 1,440 after. Every Select spec passes on both modes at `--repeat-each=2` (1,047 passed).

**2026-11-19, the Toast track's 9b design points DP1, DP3, DP4, DP7, DP8 and DP10 (M6 track 9, [issue 280](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/280); chosen by the user 2026-11-19, "Yes, as recommended", each as session T recommended; under the M6 plan's decisions and the amendments of 2026-11-18; written in full by the implementing pull request, which a fresh session opens after 9a).**
- (DP1) *One `ToastTwin`*, with the part literals `viewport`, `root` and `content` and one handle per element. A Viewport render reaches every Root, so a Root sends `update` only when its own options changed; a hover never costs one message per toast. The pull request measures the attach latency of a second toast on Server.
- (DP3) *Focus guards and the hand-off.*
  - *Guards:* one guard renderer, shared with `PopupPortalBehavior` and `NavigationMenuViewport`, is extracted into `Popup/` by grant, in its own commit with its red test. A private copy was rejected.
  - *Hand-off:* C# computes the target id at close (`ToastStore.CloseToast`) and stamps it with a revision. The module focuses it once, after re-checking that the target is connected, not ending, not `inert`, and that focus-visible lies inside the Viewport. The store gains a non-notifying element-handle cell and the previous-focus record.
- (DP4) *No Toast portal context.* `ToastPortal` uses only the shared `PopupPortalContext`, and nothing reads a Toast-specific one. The Positioner and Arrow run over `PositionerBehavior` with `Open`, `Mounted` and `KeepMounted` fixed true and three-level defaults, with one L4 title per mode inside a modal Dialog.
- (DP7) *Window blur at L4.* A second page brought to the front with `bringToFront`, probed first. If headless Chromium does not fire the events, the titles dispatch an in-page `FocusEvent` under a dated ADR 06 §1 exception, written by the same pull request.
- (DP8) *The iframe rows TST-STR-13 and TST-EDGE-08.* Probe a same-origin iframe first. If it cannot host them, the titles are `test.fixme` with an `open` annotation, and the two rows return to the user.
- (DP10) *The sample.* The hero demo lands in 9b, with the variants added as their slices land.

**2026-11-19, issue 176's fix shape: the Viewport handle subscribes to the session's reference moves (M6 track 2, [issue 176](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/176), [issue 273](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/273) design point 1; chosen by the user 2026-11-19, "Yes, subscribe", as session U recommended; written in full by the implementing pull request with its red titles).** Design point 13.13 of 2026-10-28 stands: the morph's trigger comes from the interactions session, not from C#.
- *The subscription.* `popup`'s `attachViewport` subscribes to the session's existing `subscribePositionReference`. That fires on every move of `domReferenceElement`: the session update that changes `activeTriggerId`, a hand-off's `pendingAnchor`, and the anchor's fallback when the request settles.
- *The late morph.* When the reference moves while the popup is open, after a `[data-current]` remount that started no morph, the handle runs `process()` once more. It morphs from the previous trigger to the new reference, cloning the content kept at the remount.
- *What starts nothing.* A move while closed starts nothing. So does a cancelled request whose reference returns to the previous trigger. `lastHandledTrigger` keeps one morph per move.
- *Cost.* One subscription per Viewport handle, and the morph waits for the session's update: the latency 13.13 accepted. No C# change, no family file, no change to `interactions`.
- *The alternatives.* `ActiveTriggerId` on `PopupViewportOptions` would have reversed 13.13 and changed four implemented families and the twin contract. Doing both would have given one fact two sources.

**2026-11-19, issue 176's residual: a trigger change that finishes before the Viewport attaches (M6 track 2, [issue 176](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/176), [issue 273](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/273) design point 2; chosen by the user 2026-11-19, "Yes, marker and row", as session U recommended; written in full by the implementing pull request).** With the subscription above, the handle seeds the previous trigger from the trigger C# still renders `data-popup-open` on, because on the focus families it can attach after the session's reference has moved. That turns three of the four latency titles green. The fourth is the lagging-payload title under `delayCircuit(30)`. There, the Viewport's first `update` lands after the whole switch, C#'s render and the session alike, with no remount, so the handle sees no change and starts no morph.
- *The rule.* The viewport suite waits for the Viewport handle's attach before the trigger switch, through a readiness marker (ADR 06 §2), in the suite or the fixture base.
- *The relaxation.* ADR 01 gains a row: on Server, a trigger change that completes before the Viewport attaches morphs nothing. Its figures are measured at 0, 50 and 150 ms.
- *Unchanged.* 13.13 and every family file. The quarantine is lifted once the marker is in place and the titles are green.
- *The alternatives.* The handle learning the opening trigger at attach, through an `interactions` session export or a C# Viewport option (a partial reversal of 13.13). Re-justifying the quarantine for the lagging title alone.
- *As built (M6 track 2's pull request, 2026-11-19).*
  - The marker is in the suite, so no fixture or family file changes: `waitForViewportAttached` (`tests/browser/suites/viewportConformanceTests.ts`) waits for the handle's first measurement, its `--positioner-width` write on the Positioner. No other handle of these families writes that variable, and C# never renders it. The lagging-payload title waits for it before the switch, since it switches as soon as `[data-current]` is in the page. The other morph titles first wait for the content to show and run unchanged.
  - The attach seed: `findRenderedTrigger()`, the session trigger with `data-popup-open`, else the reference.
  - The late morph: `keptContent`, the `[data-current]` container a remount replaced while the batch had moved `data-popup-open` off the previous trigger, kept until the reference follows. It is cleared on close and when a morph starts.
  - A defect the lifted title found, with no design choice: on Server under latency the previous container's exit ran no transition (the lagging latency title 14 of 32 red, Popover 7, Menu 5, PreviewCard 2 of 8). The remount's measurement resolved the inserted container's style only under its `display: none`, and nothing resolved it shown before the frame that sets `data-ending-style`. That frame now reads `getComputedStyle(previous)` first (PSH-ANI-08, PSH-ANI-09).
  - The latency titles: the three focus-family titles of 282d1023 ("creates morphing containers during transitions", "sets `data-activation-direction` from the previous to the new trigger", "keeps the latest transition active during rapid trigger changes", each "port: …, under circuit latency on Server" at 30 ms one-way) stay, without the wait. There the session's reference moves at the focus, before C#'s render of the switch, and the handle can attach in between, so they prove the attach seed. The lagging title's latency variant is dropped: with the wait, `main`'s module passes it on Tooltip (8 of 8) and PreviewCard (7 of 8), and ADR 11 §3's red check refuses a title that passes on the base. The style read's red is its L3 title. Locally at 30 ms the variant was 14 of 32 red without the read and 32 of 32 green with it.
  - The relaxation row's figures are in ADR 01 Amendments 2026-11-19.

**2026-11-19, untested Base UI quirks in Toast (the 2026-10-18 note extended; the M6 plan's decision 23 (a), chosen by the user 2026-11-18; M6 track 9a, [issue 280](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/280)).** toast.md §16 Q2 (the Viewport's `--toast-frontmost-height` reads the first toast, ending or not), Q7 (the alert region includes ending toasts) and Q8 (an upsert keeps `limited`) are replicated, one dated note each in §16. Q5 and Q6 (the gesture's `movementX/Y` and the hand-off after a timer close) are replicated by M6 track 9b with the module. Q3 waits for track 11's probe.

**2026-11-19, the ScrollArea track's design points 1–15 (M6 track 5, [issue 276](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/276); chosen by the user 2026-11-19, "Yes, as recommended", each as session S recommended; under the M6 plan's decisions and the amendments of 2026-11-18; written in full by the implementing pull request).**
- (1) *Bands.* The Root's `--scroll-area-corner-*`, the Scrollbar's `--scroll-area-thumb-*` and the Corner's `width` and `height` go in `'component-under'`. Base UI passes them through the merged `style` prop, so the consumer's `style` wins. The two-band paragraph's examples name only the imperative writes.
- (2) *The Viewport's `scroll-snap-type`.* A Writer cell `style.scroll-snap-type` `JS` in `'component'`, written during a drag and removed at its end, with an ownership entry. §2's Viewport row gains it.
- (3) *Reports.* Three reports: `ScrollAreaScrollingReport(bool X, bool Y)`, `ScrollAreaHoverReport(bool Hovering)` on the Root handle, and `ScrollAreaOverflowReport(bool HasOverflowX, bool HasOverflowY, bool XStart, bool XEnd, bool YStart, bool YEnd, bool CornerHidden, bool Measured)`. The sizes stay in JavaScript. The options carry the four normalized thresholds, `rtl`, the part literal and orientation, `keepMounted` and the merged style.
- (4) *Overflow `data-*` and `tabindex`.* C# projects them from the overflow report.
- (5) *The first measurement on Server* is accepted and measured as ADR 01 rows. A JS-owned convergent `visibility` follows only if the figures call for it.
- (6) *One handle per part*, resolved to the Root by id or `closest`. Slider's single Root handle stays as built.
- (7) *Reading direction.* `rtl` comes in the options, from `DirectionProviderContext` and a listener, and a change re-measures.
- (8) *The per-scroll measurement* is uncached, as in Base UI, with its figure recorded.
- (9) *The non-inheriting property registration* is module-local: a per-page flag, skipped on WebKit, with an L4 port title.
- (10) *The `Test: none` rows.* L2 port titles for the 15 rows C# asserts, and a prerender fixture for SCR-EDGE-13.
- (11) *UTL-DOM-32/33's ScrollArea clause.* Three L2 port titles on the real `ScrollAreaRoot`.
- (12) *Probe fixtures inside the M5 popups* come last and block nothing. Findings are filed as issues.
- (13) *Sample compositions.* Dialog and Autocomplete pages now; Drawer's in track 8.
- (14) *Input no real device makes.* CDP where it can produce the event, otherwise in-page dispatch, with a dated note for each.
- (15) *Server render batches* ([issue 291](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/291)). The figures are recorded with no ScrollArea-only mitigation, as for Slider. The candidate fix of rendering on a mirrored-only change only when the part has a state function is added to issue 291. (Corrected 2026-11-19, from session S: that "candidate" is the existing pattern. Slider's and Tabs' parts already select their mirrored fields only when they have a state function, and ScrollArea follows it. A `data-scrolling` flip renders no part unless the consumer supplied `ClassValue`, `StyleValue` or a `Render` function, and the Root renders first on an overflow change so its subtree re-renders in one batch. Point 15 decides nothing new.)

**2026-11-18, the `slider` module as built (M6 track 4, [issue 275](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/275); the design points of track 4, chosen by the user 2026-11-18 as recommended).** One handle on the Slider Root element (design point 1 (a')): the Root is the key surface, since a thumb anywhere under it takes keys (SLD-STR-05); the Control and the Indicators cross as references and the thumbs as their inputs' ids by index, each thumb element being its input's parent. The module holds the seven value algorithms of SLD §4.1 (ADR 01 decision 2), the thumb keys, the drag loop on the Control's `pointerdown` and native `touchstart`, the inset pass with its `ResizeObserver` and the extra mount microtask (SLD §16 Q12, replicated), and from the attach the Thumb's and the Indicator's whole style and each input's `value` (SLD-DOM-16's Writer cell `C# → JS`); every property it writes goes through `dom`'s style writer in `'component-under'`, below the consumer's merged `style`, which the options carry per element; nothing goes to `'component'`. The two decision points of §3 are named `key` (the thumb `keydown`, fallback `run`) and `press` (the Control `pointerdown`, fallback `start`). Reports: `OnValueRequest { reason, activeThumbIndex, values, event, revision }` after each optimistic move, `OnGesture { active, dragging, commit, event }`, `OnFocusChange { index, focused, outside, event }` (the blur classification of 2026-10-20), and `OnKeyDown { index, event }` where no C# chain ran inside the dispatch and a thumb's consumer has a C# `onkeydown`. The revision rule: the module counts its acts; an `update` whose `revision` is older than the last act keeps the module's values unless it carries `rollback`, which C# sends for a canceled request and for a controlled value the consumer did not change; a rolled-back swap restores the pressed thumb. The quirks of the 2026-10-18 paragraph: §16 Q4 (capture at `pointerId` 0) and Q6 (a gesture dropped by disabling clears the pressed thumb and the grab offset) are fixed as dated deviations; Q2, Q3, Q5, Q8, Q11, Q12 and Q13 are replicated. The thumb registry is the Root's registration-only composite list, with the explicit index of a thumb that has `Index` (the `Composite/` grant of decision 23: `CompositeListItemBehavior.SetRegistration(id, index)`; the JS registry already took an explicit index).

**2026-11-19, the Drawer track's design point 2 revised: the style writer absorbs a foreign write only on opt-in (M6 track 8b, [issue 279](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/279); chosen by the user 2026-11-19, recorded on issue 279; revises point 2 of the note of 2026-11-18 on the Drawer track's design points 1, 2 and 9).** `StyleWriter` gains `absorbForeignWrites()`. It installs one `MutationObserver` on the element's `style` attribute, once; from then on, a write the writer did not make takes the attribute's text as the consumer's, the cache is rebuilt from the element and every band's winners are written again. Foreign is judged per declaration against the cache: the observer absorbs any difference, and a writer call absorbs first only when one of its own declarations was removed or changed, so Select's in-task measurement (`unsetTransformStyles`) is never taken. A writer that has not opted in behaves as before. Only the `drawer` module opts in, on its Popup, whose `--nested-drawers` and `--drawer-frontmost-height` C# renders in the commit path (§2's dated exception). The settled form, an observer installed at every writer's first layer write, failed in the browser: `popup.ts`'s `override()` (`usePopupAutoResize.ts:175-182`) writes a measurement declaration directly and restores it in a later task, and the observer took it, with the bands present, as the consumer's. In a local L4 sample of 2026-11-19 the always-on form failed 6 titles (the positioner conformance "uses top/left positioning with Viewport" ×3 and Select's "inherits the disabled state from the root" ×2, on both modes) and the opt-in form passed the same sample, 2,369 passed, 24 skipped and 0 failed. L3: `dom.test.ts` `styleWriter › a foreign write` (six titles, among them "port: only an opted-in writer takes a direct override restored in a later task as the consumer style", whose second half, a writer that has not opted in, fails on the always-on form) and `drawer.test.ts` "port: a C# re-render of the popup style during a drag keeps the drag declarations and takes the rendered ones". The other option, keeping the observer on every writer and teaching `popup.ts` to restore its override inside one task, was not taken: it edits a fourth M4 module for a Drawer-only need.

**2026-11-19, the `scroll-area` module as built (M6 track 5, [issue 276](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/276); under the scope cell, the bands paragraph and the ScrollArea rows of 2026-11-18; design points 1, 2, 3, 6 and 7 of the ScrollArea track, chosen by the user 2026-11-19 as recommended (the paragraph above); written by the implementing pull request).**
- *Handles.* One handle per part (design point 6 (a)); each part but the root passes the root element as reference 0. The root's state lives on the root element and is created by whichever handle attaches first, so a part's handle may attach before the root's (the 2026-10-29 attach-overtaken rule). The port L4 title "port: parts whose handles attach before the root's are measured once the root attaches" delays the root's `attach` through a wrapped module; it fails without the rule and passes with it.
- *Options and reports.* Each handle's options are `part` and `style`; a scrollbar or a thumb adds `orientation`, and the root adds `rtl` and the four thresholds C# normalized. Only the root's twin is reported to, once per change, never per frame (design point 3 (a)): `OnOverflow` (overflow presence, the four edge flags, the measured flag), `OnScrolling` (both axes) and `OnHovering`. Thumb and corner sizes stay in JavaScript.
- *The band of each property* (design point 1 (a) for the three state-rendered values).
