  - `'component'`: the Thumb's `transform` and its overscroll `--scroll-area-thumb-height`/`-width`, removed when back in range; the Viewport's four `--scroll-area-overflow-*`; and its `scroll-snap-type: none` during a drag, removed at its end so the consumer's value applies again.
  - `'component-under'`: the Root's `--scroll-area-corner-height`/`-width`, a Scrollbar's `--scroll-area-thumb-height` or `-width`, and the Corner's `width`/`height`.
  - The Content's style is never taken over; C# renders it throughout.
  - The Viewport row of §2 gains `scroll-snap-type` (design point 2 (a)).
- *Quirks replicated* (the 2026-10-18 untested-quirks paragraph, decision 23 of the M6 plan): scroll-area.md §16 Q3 (the unreachable track `visibility`), Q4 (the recorded position updated only by user-driven scrolls), Q5 (`2 × margin-inline-start`), Q6 (the drag reads the rendered `data-orientation`), Q7 (the measured flag never resets), Q8 (the unclamped track-press ratio) and Q9 (a drag at `maxThumbOffset ≤ 0` still cancels and marks), each with a dated note on its row.
- *No decision point* (2026-11-18): a C# consumer handler on a part runs after the module's listener and cannot veto it; ADR 01's C#-origin consumer-veto row names the SCR rows.

**2026-11-19, NumberField's PR 6a as built (M6 track 6, [issue 277](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/277); the NumberField settlement of Amendments 2026-11-18, points 1, 2 (b), 4 (a) and 7 (a), and the names of the line after it; decision 16 (a), chosen by the user 2026-11-18).** The settlement holds; these are the shapes it left to the track.
- *`hold-start` on the `button` twin.* `button.ts` asks `decide(twin, 'hold-start', pointerdown, 'run')` before a press-and-hold press acts. C# answers it through the twin's `ActivationDecider`, which `ButtonBehavior.Decide` routes to the hold-start check by the snapshot's `pointerdown` type, so the twin has no second decider. A separate decider and per-twin hold delegates cost every button twin, and a Combobox keystroke mounts about 20: `ComboboxAllocationBudgetTests` reached 307,344 B against its 294,976 B budget. With the repeater's state in a `ButtonHold` that only steppers create, kept in `ButtonBehavior`'s one extras object beside the hidden-input bridge's members, the keystroke is 294,704 B.
- *The hold options.* The focus target and the stop counter are `PressAndHoldOptions.FocusTarget` and `Stop` (`pressAndHold.focusTarget`, `pressAndHold.stop` in JavaScript), sent only when set. A sequence's first tick carries `start: true` in `ButtonHoldEvent`, so no separate start report exists.
- *The `number-field` module in 6a.* Attach, the visible input's `value` handover with the revision that `update` echoes (point 7 (a)), and the `OnInputFocus` and `OnInputBlur` reports behind `input-focus` and `input-blur`, each skipped when the event's default was prevented. 6b adds `input-key`, `input-paste`, `input-change`, `scrub-start` and the scrub role.
- *iOS (point 4 (a)).* `readPlatformFlags` returns `ios` beside `voiceOver` and `mac`. The root renders `inputmode="numeric"` until the read lands (ADR 01 Amendments 2026-11-19).

**2026-11-19, the `toast` module as built (M6 track 9b, [issue 280](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/280); under the `toast` scope cell, the bands paragraph and the revision rule of Amendments 2026-11-18, chosen by the user 2026-11-18, and the 9b design points of Amendments 2026-11-19, chosen by the user 2026-11-19; written by the implementing pull request).**
- *Twin and options (DP1 (a)).* `ToastTwin`, one per part, part literals `viewport`, `root` and `content`. `ToastOptions : TwinOptions`: `Part`; `Revision` (Viewport and Root, the last report C# applied); `Empty`, `Hovering`, `Focused`, `HasEnding`, `FocusTargetRef` and `HandOff` (Viewport); `Directions` and `Lifecycle` (Root); `RootRef` (Content). `ToastHandOffOptions(int Revision, bool ToPrevious, int? TargetRef)`. Each part sends `update` only when its options or the elements they name changed, and a hover renders no Root's update.
- *Reports.* One `[JSInvokable] OnReport` with `ToastReport(int Revision, string Kind)` and `Height`, `Lifecycle`, `Direction`, `Dismiss`, `Resume`. The Viewport sends `enter`, `leave` (after the deferral of TST-PTR-03/04), `focus`, `blur`, `f6`, `window-blur`, `window-focus` (only when it resumes the timers), `window-focused` (after a 0 ms task, only after a window blur) and `touch-outside`. The Root sends `height` (once per change in a lifecycle), `swipe-start`, `direction`, `release` (with the dismissing direction or `null`), `escape` and `touch-press`. The Content reports nothing; it asks its Root's handle to measure again.
- *The revision rule (decision 6 (a)).* The module's optimistic view of `hovering` and `focused`, and the Root's swipe and freeze, give way to an `update` only once its `Revision` reaches the module's last report. The Root's `Lifecycle` (a new toast id, or a status back to `starting`) is the only rollback: it resets the swipe and measures again, whatever its revision. The first red L4 title, `holdCircuit` over the release report, is `toast.root.spec.ts` "port: a stale update after a swipe dismissal never snaps the toast back".
- *Bands, completing §2's `Toast.Root` row.* `'component-under'` holds `--toast-swipe-movement-x/y` (always, `0px` at rest), and `transition: none` and `transform` while swiping and through the release freeze. `'component'` holds the measurement's transient `height: auto`, removed in the same task. The Root's consumer band is its merged string (`--toast-index`, `--toast-offset-y`, `--toast-height`, then the consumer's `style`), frozen in its render from the attach. The release freeze ends when `data-ending-style` lands on the Root (an attribute `MutationObserver`).
- *Focus (DP3).* The three guards are C#'s, through the one renderer `FocusGuard.Render(builder, type?)` in `Popup/` now shared with `PopupPortalBehavior` and `NavigationMenuViewport` (DP3 (a); the orchestrator's grant). The module routes them: the inner two on the Viewport's `focusin`, the outer one on the document's while toasts exist. The hand-off is computed by `ToastStore.CloseToast` over the list as closed and sent before the store's write, because on WebAssembly that write renders at once and an exit with no animation removes the focused toast within it (found by `toast.root.spec.ts` "closes when pressing escape", red on WebAssembly before the order was changed).
- *Two handles on one element.* The Root's `transitions` handle (`Open` while not ending, the created status as the handover, its exit completion removing the toast) is synced before its `toast` handle. A keyed reorder of a re-added toast moves its element, which cancels its exit transition; `transitions` then completes the exit and hides the element, and the re-open must show it before the new lifecycle measures (found by "keeps stacking intact when re-adding an ending toast among other toasts", 0 px measured before the order). A held `transitions.js` holds the removal and never the measurement or the swipe ("port: a stalled transitions module holds the removal, never the measurement or the swipe").
- *Shared files, each in its own commit as the brief lists them:* `Interop/BlazixJsonContext.cs` (`ToastOptions`, `ToastReport`), the csproj's `BlazixJsEntry` `js/toast.ts` → `js/toast`, `tests/Blazix.Primitives.Fixtures.Client/FixtureCatalog.cs` (`toast-basic`, `toast-anchored`, `toast-dialog`, `toast-portal`), `tests/Blazix.Primitives.Tests/Lints/OwnershipRegistry.cs` (the Positioner's and Arrow's rows) and `samples/Blazix.Primitives.Demo.Client/DemoCatalog.cs` (the `toast` page); `Popup/` by the orchestrator's grant (the guard renderer below).
- *Quirks* (the 2026-10-18 note, extended to Toast, the M6 plan's decision 23 (a)): toast.md §16 Q3 (the forward-Tab bounce, confirmed by track 11's probe of Base UI, 5 of 5), Q5 (`movementX/Y` drive the cancel baseline) and Q6 (a timer's close hands focus off) are replicated, each with a dated note in §16.

**2026-11-19, one focus guard renderer in `Popup/` (M6 track 9b, [issue 280](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/280); DP3 (a) of the Toast track, chosen by the user 2026-11-19; the `Popup/` edit granted by the orchestrator 2026-11-19).** `FocusGuard.Render(RenderTreeBuilder builder, string? type = null)` renders the guard span that `PopupPortalBehavior` (with `type` `outside`) and `NavigationMenuViewport` rendered from private copies, and `ToastViewport` now renders too. Both existing callers' markup is unchanged byte for byte (`<span tabindex="0" data-base-ui-focus-guard="" [data-type="outside"] aria-hidden="true" style="…visually hidden…">`, in a region of its own), pinned by [L2] `Popup/FocusGuardTests.cs` "port: renders a visually hidden tabbable guard with an optional type". The caller's module routes the guard's focus; the span carries no handler.

**2026-11-19, NumberField's PR 6b as built (M6 track 6, [issue 277](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/277); the NumberField settlement of Amendments 2026-11-18 and the names of the line after it: the decision points were chosen by the user 2026-11-18; the rest as built in PR 6b).** The settlement holds; these are the shapes it left to the track.
- *One `attach`, told apart by `part`.* `js/number-field.ts` exports one `attach` (and `getViewportRect(teleportDistance, scrubArea)`); the options' `part` picks the role, as ScrollArea's do. No `part` is the input role (`NumberFieldTwin`, on the visible input). `part: 'scrub'` is a ScrubArea's handle and `part: 'cursor'` a ScrubAreaCursor's, both through `NumberFieldScrubTwin` (`ScrubPart`, `CursorPart`), one handle per part.
- *The input role's gate.* The `keydown` gate and the `input` event's report or revert (NUM-EDGE-06); there is no `beforeinput` listener. The gate's data is the options' nested `input` part (`NumberFieldInputOptions`), sent at attach and with an update that changes it; an update without it keeps the last one. A change whose default was prevented restores the last accepted text.
- *The keydown listener sits on the input's root node.* The module listens for `keydown` on the input's root node (the document or the shadow root) in the bubble phase and acts only when `event.target` is the input. Every listener on the input itself has then run, so `defaultPrevented` carries their verdict whatever the attach order: React's delegated handler order. The case is TLB-KEY-13: the toolbar's disabled input item prevents every key but Tab from the `button` module's handle, which may attach after the `number-field` one (`toolbar.spec.ts` › "port: disabled state with the number-field handle attached first"; L3 `js/number-field.test.ts` › "port: a listener attached after the module that prevents the keydown keeps it from stepping"). The hidden input's focus redirect listens in the capture phase, ahead of the `form` module's FFI-FOC-05 redirect, which then finds the focus moved.
- *The points.* `input-key`, `input-paste` and `input-change` with fallback **run**, beside 6a's `input-focus` and `input-blur`; `scrub-start` with fallback **start**, answered by `NumberFieldScrubTwin.StartDecider`.
- *Consumer handlers the part declines.* Each declined Input handler (`onkeydown`, `onpaste`, `onfocus`, `onblur`, `oninput`) has one marker, so it runs once. Blazor dispatches `onkeydown` (on the document, bubble phase) and `onfocus`/`onblur` (on the document, capture phase) before the module decides, so on WebAssembly the decision finds the handler's verdict already recorded and takes it. The module decides `paste` and `input` first, and the decision runs the handler; Blazor's later dispatch does not run it again. The ScrubArea declines the consumer's `onpointerdown` and runs it in the `scrub-start` decider on WebAssembly. Proven at L2 by `NumberFieldConsumerVetoTests` and `NumberFieldScrubAreaTests`.
- *The scrub options.* `NumberFieldScrubOptions : TwinOptions` with `string Part`, `bool? Vertical`, `double? PixelSensitivity` (null is 2), `double? TeleportDistance`, `bool? Disabled`, `bool? ReadOnly`; unset options are omitted. The cursor sends `{ part: 'cursor', style }`, where `style` is C#'s `position: fixed; top: 0; left: 0; pointer-events: none` merged under the consumer's style and sent as the consumer string.
- *The scrub reports.* `OnScrubStart { cursor, event }` (`cursor`: neither touch nor WebKit), `OnScrubLock { denied }` (only when the outcome differs from the last), `OnScrubStep { delta, revision, event }` (`delta` is `movementX`, or `-movementY` when vertical; `revision` is the input handle's next revision, so a text answer older than the step is not applied, decision 6 (a)), `OnScrubEnd { event }`. C# payloads `NumberFieldScrubStartReport(bool Cursor, EventSnapshot Event)`, `NumberFieldScrubLockReport(bool Denied)`, `NumberFieldScrubStepReport(double Delta, int Revision, EventSnapshot Event)`, `NumberFieldScrubEndReport(EventSnapshot Event)`, each in `BlazixJsonContext`; the twin raises `ScrubStarted`, `LockSettled`, `ScrubStepped` and `ScrubEnded`.
- *The cursor.* Its `transform` goes in the style writer's `'component'` band (decision 5 as revised: Base UI writes it with `style.transform` after render). It renders inside the internal `NumberFieldCursorPortal`, a lite portal (PSH-STR-11) composed over `PopupPortalBehavior`, not a second portal mechanism, so its parent is a `<div data-base-ui-portal>` node appended to the body (number-field.md, NUM-STR-06's port deviation). Its handle attaches beside the portal's move, not after it; the module places the cursor at once and centres it again by its size once it is laid out, from a `ResizeObserver` (design point 8 (a)).

**2026-11-19, OtpField's decision points and module shape (M6 track 7, [issue 278](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/278); design points 1–7, chosen by the user 2026-11-19, "Yes, all seven"; the note is `m6-track-7-otp-field-design-points-2026-11-19.md`, recorded on issue 278).**
- *Decision points (design point 1 (a)).* The `otp-field` twin has five decision points: `edit` (Amendments 2026-11-18; the change, paste and editing-key vetoes) and four more, each with fallback "run": `key` (navigation keys), `press` (`mousedown` on a slot), `focus` and `blur`. Their vetoes are synchronous on WebAssembly (OTP-KEY-01, KEY-13, KEY-19, PTR-02, FOC-08, EVT-16; ADR 01, otp-field.md §9), with one-shot consumer markers, NumberField 6b's model.
- *`edit`'s answer inside the dispatch (2 (a)).* A synchronous `[JSInvokable]` handover, then the `edit` decision; C#'s update reaches the module before the handler returns (the Tabs and ContextMenu precedent).
- *Character test (3 (a)).* C# sends the slot pattern; the module tests one character; C# re-validates (NumberField's gate).
- *A cancelled typed edit (4 (a)).* Only the focus move rolls back; the slot keeps its character and the value commits (ADR 05 item 1), with no completion. Keyboard and paste cancels restore the slots (OTP-EVT-04).
- *Slot `tabindex` (5 (a)).* Module-written after attach, with the focus move: a Writer correction on OTP-FOC-01 and an ownership row.
- *Handles (6 (a)).* One Root handle with delegated listeners; slots are found by registered id.
- *AutoSubmit (7 (a)).* A submit revision in the options; the module calls `requestSubmit()` (the Combobox precedent). `js/form.ts` is unchanged.

**2026-11-19, the render-order guess in `Composite/` (M6 track 7, [issue 278](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/278); Q-OTP-2 (b) of the M6 plan, chosen by the user 2026-11-18; the `Composite/` grant to track 7, the second after track 4's, settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-19).** `CompositeListItemBehavior` gains an opt-in guess, `guess: true` on its constructor. A guessing item without an explicit index takes the next index of its list's render-order counter on its first registration, `CompositeListContext.TakeGuess()`, and keeps it until the module's order report names it. `CompositeListBehavior` restarts the counter at its registered item count on each render of the list (`CompositeList.tsx:85`). The change is additive: an item that does not opt in still reads `-1` until the first publication (CMP-STA-15), and `js/composite.ts` is unchanged. OtpField's slots opt in, so prerender and the first interactive render carry each slot's `id`, `value`, `tabindex` and `maxlength` (OTP-EDGE-10). CMP-STA-16 carries the dated note.

**2026-11-20, the `otp-field` module as built (M6 track 7, [issue 278](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/278); Amendments 2026-11-18 and 2026-11-19, design points 1–7 (a), chosen by the user 2026-11-19).**
- *Listeners.* Delegated from the root node (the document or the shadow root), not the Root element, so Blazor's document-level dispatch of a consumer's slot handler always runs before the module and its `stopPropagation()` never cuts it off (the NumberField keydown precedent). A handled key is therefore hidden only from listeners above the document (OTP-KEY-05; design point 9 of N3, (a), chosen by the user 2026-11-20).
- *Edits.* One report per edit, `{ index, kind, raw, advanced, revision, event }` with kinds `input`, `paste`, `backspace`, `delete` and `clear`; on WebAssembly `OnEditHandover`, then `edit`, whose C# chain runs the slot's consumer handler and the request. C# echoes every report's revision, so an update follows every edit, which restores an ignored or cancelled one (OTP-KEY-17). A vetoed WebAssembly edit restores the module's last act.
- *Focus.* Every slot focus is reported, so the field's focus owner is the slot that last took focus (OTP-FOC-09); a blur only when focus leaves the root. The module owns the slots' `value` and `tabindex` after attach (two ownership rows).
- *Composition.* `dom`'s `trackComposition` per slot; a slot whose `compositionend` the module handled counts as no longer composing until its next `compositionstart`, since on WebAssembly the update lands in the microtasks before the slot's tracker sees the end.
- *Reset.* The form's `reset` rewrites the slots and the hidden input one task later (§16 item 9; the hidden input's `value` ownership row).
- *Quirks replicated* (decision 23 (a); extending the 2026-10-18 note): OTP §16 items 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (filed upstream by the user from the hand-over's draft), 7, 8 and 11.
- *`EnsureGuess()`* (the `Composite/` grant above): `CompositeListItemBehavior.EnsureGuess()` takes the render-order guess before the item registers, for an item whose registered id derives from its index (an OtpField slot); `SetRegistration` then takes none.

**2026-11-20, the Drawer's `snap-point` decision point names its target (M6 track 8b slice 3, [issue 279](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/279); chosen by the user 2026-11-20, recorded on issue 279).** A decision carries only its point name and its event, and the snap point decision has to tell the C# chain which snap point the release asks for. The `drawer` module therefore suffixes the name: `snap-point:<index>`, an index into the snap points the options send, or `snap-point:none` when the release closes the drawer. This is an exception to plain point names, for this point only. `DrawerTwin.OnDecide` parses the suffix and hands the index, or none, to the Viewport's chain. On WebAssembly the chain decides inside the dispatch. Elsewhere the fallback stays **run**, and the module reports a numbered `OnSnapPointRequest` under the revision rule of Amendments 2026-11-18: a request C# cancels rolls back on the next `update`. `TwinBase` and `twin.ts` are unchanged.

**2026-11-21, the Drawer's `close-watcher` decision point (M6 track 8c, [issue 279](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/279); design point D1 (a), chosen by the user 2026-11-21, recorded on issue 279).** §3's decision points gain `close-watcher`, the Drawer's fourth after `snap-point` and `dismiss`. On Android, while the drawer is open and topmost, the Popup's `drawer` handle holds a `CloseWatcher` (DRW-DIS-01; design point 1 (a) of 2026-11-18 put the watcher on that handle).
- *Point and verdict.* The handle asks `decide(twin, 'close-watcher', event, 'prevent')` inside the watcher's `cancel` event when the event is cancelable. On WebAssembly the Popup's chain requests the close with reason `close-watcher`, and the verdict is **prevent** when a consumer canceled it: the module calls `preventDefault()`, so the same watcher stays active (EVD-EVT-12). Otherwise the verdict is **run**: the browser destroys the watcher and fires `close`.
- *Fallback.* Elsewhere the fallback is **prevent**: the watcher stays, and the module reports a numbered `OnCloseWatcherRequest` (`request`, `cancelable`, the event). C# closes the drawer or not a round trip later. The handle destroys the watcher once an `update` says the drawer closed. Nothing rolls back, so the options carry no answer for these requests.
- *A non-cancelable event.* The browser allows one preventable close request per user activation, so a later `cancel` event is not cancelable. The module asks no point for it and reports it with `cancelable: false`. C#'s `cancel()` cannot stop that close (EVD-EVT-10).
- *Server's look* is ADR 01's relaxation row for DRW-DIS-01. A back press that no consumer cancels closes a round trip later. Preventing it spends the page's one preventable close request for that activation even when the consumer would not have canceled. `TwinBase` and `twin.ts` are unchanged.

**2026-11-23, the identity rename ([ADR 17](17-product-name-and-package-identity.md), chosen by the user the same day).** The product is Iodine and this package `Iodine.Headless`. Every live name in this record that carries Blazix (`Blazix.Primitives` as project, package, namespace or path, `AddBlazixPrimitives`, `BlazixSkipJsBuild`, the `Blazix: ` message prefix, `blazix-*` names, `BLZX` diagnostic ids) reads in the form ADR 17 decision 3's table gives; the text above is kept as written.

**2026-11-24, Escape dismissal closes from the document after Blazor's dispatch and marks the key (M6 track 10, [issue 251](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/251); design point 2 (a), chosen by the user 2026-11-24, recorded on issue 251; the refinement in the third bullet, design point 10c-2b (a′), chosen by the user 2026-11-24, recorded on issue 251).** Base UI's `closeOnEscapeKeyDown` (`useDismiss.ts:216-228`) stops only React's propagation, after the consumer's merged `onKeyDown` has run. The port's native `stopPropagation()` in the floating element's listener kept Blazor's document-level dispatcher from delivering Escape to any C# `onkeydown` on a popup part.
- *The claim.* The floating element's keydown listener no longer decides or stops an Escape. It claims the key for the innermost session (`escapeClaimed`). The close runs from the session's document listener. The first session listener on the document runs the claimant's decision before its own, so the innermost popup still decides first, and the keydown's forest record (Amendments 2026-11-03 (5)) is taken before that decision. On WebAssembly a close in the positioner's own listener unmounted the popup before Blazor dispatched the keydown to it.
- *The mark.* Where the verdict stops (`prevent-and-stop` or `stop`, Escape not bubbling), the deciding session marks the keydown taken with its token (`escapeTaken`). Every other session's listener reads the mark and skips the key. The mark is on the event, so a synchronous close that clears the taking session before an outer listener runs keeps it. §3's verdicts and fallbacks are unchanged.
- *The native stop stays where it runs after Blazor (design point 10c-2b (a′), chosen by the user 2026-11-24).* The deciding listener, the trigger's or the document's, still calls `stopPropagation()` with the mark. Base UI's own titles need it: Combobox's "does not bubble Escape key when Empty component is present" (an `onKeyDown` on an ancestor of the input does not hear the key) and Tooltip's "allowPropagation() prevents stopPropagation on Escape while still closing". A full drop of the stop, as design point 2 (a) read, failed them, so the window and a trigger's ancestors stay silent as in Base UI. Two costs are accepted as deviations, recorded on FLT-KEY-01: a document listener registered before the session's still hears an Escape from inside a popup, where React's root-level stop would hide it; and a trigger's or Combobox input's own consumer `onkeydown` still does not hear an Escape that closes the popup (unchanged, outside issue 251).
- *What the order rests on (design point 10c-2c (vii), chosen by the user 2026-11-24, recorded on issue 251).* Blazor adds its document listener for `keydown` when the first element with a C# key handler renders. A library trigger with its own key handler renders it at the page's first render, before any session attaches; but where a popup part's consumer handler is the page's first C# key handler, Blazor adds its listener in the open's render, after a session that attached earlier. So the claim re-adds every live session's document keydown listener, in the order they were added, before the event reaches the document: they then run after Blazor's. L4 "port: a popup whose consumer onkeydown is the page's first C# key handler hears Escape once and still dismisses" (`popover-root?keyless=true`, both modes) was red on WebAssembly 5 of 5 before this; "port: a click-opened popover's consumer onkeydown hears Escape once and the popup still dismisses" covers the trigger-first order.
- Tests: L3 "port: an Escape in the inner popup closes it from the document, marked as taken: the document hears it and the outer session skips it" and the escape-point verdict title "…prevented and taken…" (`interactions.test.ts`); L4 "port: a popup's consumer onkeydown hears Escape once and the popup still dismisses" on Popover and on the filterable Menu, and Base UI's "runs a consumer handler once per interaction on each composed part", both modes.

**2026-11-24, the internal backdrop is cut at insertion (M6 track 10, [issue 242](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/242); design point 10d-4 (b), chosen by the user 2026-11-24, recorded on issue 242).** Base UI computes the backdrop's cut-out during render (`InternalBackdrop.tsx:13-16`); the port's backdrop had mounted uncut until the positioner's `update`. `PopupInternalBackdrop.Render` now takes `cutoutId` and renders `data-iodine-cutout` naming the element the backdrop leaves uncovered: Menu's active trigger or a bar menu's bar, Popover's and Select's trigger, Combobox's input group, input or trigger; Dialog's backdrop names none. The `popup` module keeps one `MutationObserver` per document, installed at its first attach, which cuts each inserted backdrop in the microtask after the batch inserted it, before any hit test; the positioner role's cut follows each pass as before (§2's `clip-path` row is unchanged). Limits: the observer does not see inside shadow roots, so a backdrop portaled into one falls back to its positioner's cut; and a page's first popup, rendered before the module has loaded, is cut at its positioner's attach. L3 "port: a backdrop inserted under a resting pointer is cut before the next hit test" (`popup.test.ts`).

**2026-11-25, a bar trigger's hover enable replays the entry, and a Menu trigger installs the backdrop observer (M6 track 10, [issue 363](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/363); the `dom` move settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-25).** (1) Design point 5 of M5 track 8 replayed the document's recorded hover entry only at a hover handle's attach. It now also replays at an `update` that turns a menubar trigger's hover on, when the entry was recorded after a menu of the same bar was requested open (a bar trigger's `mousedown`, which opens through C#, or a module request) or opened. Base UI commits the bar's flag and its triggers' hover together; the port's trigger gets its hover a round trip later on Server, and the entry made in that window was lost. A bar trigger marks its bar's session as a menubar at its press and its open change as well as at its attach and its updates, because the bar's session can register after its triggers' handles. (2) The insertion cut of 2026-11-24 moves from `popup` to `dom` (`cutoutPolygon`, `observeBackdropInsertions`; `popup` re-exports `cutoutPolygon`), and `createMenuTriggerController` installs the observer at its attach, so a page's first menu is cut at insertion. That entry's limit for a page's first popup now holds for Popover, Select and Combobox only. Tests: L4 "port: a sibling trigger hovers open as soon as a bar menu shows open" (Server) and "port: with js/popup.js stalled the first bar menu of a page is cut out of its backdrop at insertion" (both modes) in `menubar.spec.ts`; L3 the four "port: a bar trigger whose hover an update turns on…" titles (`interactions.test.ts`).

**2026-11-25, an option that turns an interaction off takes effect at the end of the current task, and the list's Shift+Tab reads its return element before `leave` (M6 track 10, [issue 297](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/297); design point 10e-1 (a), chosen by the user 2026-11-25, recorded on issue 297).** Base UI's `useListNavigation.ts:771-792` closes the menu and focuses the return element in one keydown, before React re-renders, so the trigger's focus interaction still has its bar-open props. On WebAssembly C# runs the close inside `decide(LEAVE_POINT)`: the popup unmounts, which clears the active trigger (PSH-STA-45), and `MenuTriggerBehavior` turns the trigger's `focus` option off, both inside the dispatch. (1) `composite`'s `floatingKeyDown` reads `returnFocusElement() ?? referenceElementOf(...)` before the decision. (2) `createFocusController.update()` defers an uninstall (the options now `null`) to a microtask and checks again, so a focus inside the same task still meets the listener; an install stays immediate. Every focus-open trigger (Tooltip, Popover, PreviewCard, NavigationMenu, Menu, Menubar) keeps its listener for that microtask. The L3 list harness plays PSH-STA-45 with `unmountClearsTrigger` (settled by the orchestrator 2026-11-25). Tests: L4 `menubar.spec.ts` "port: Shift+Tab inside an open bar menu closes it with focus-out" (both modes, red on WebAssembly before); L3 "port: on WebAssembly a Shift+Tab close that runs inside leave still returns the focus to a trigger-role reference" (`composite-list.test.ts`) and "port: an update that turns the focus off inside a task keeps the listener until the task ends" (`interactions.test.ts`).

**2026-11-26, a twin handle may answer a read, and the floating list handle reads its items' rendered labels (M6 track 10, [issue 312](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/312); option (1), chosen by the user 2026-11-26).** §3's handle carried only `update(options)` and `dispose()`. SEL-FRM-18's third priority matches a browser autofill against the items' rendered typeahead labels, which only the `composite` module resolves (CMP-STA-06/07, on every rebuild of the registry). Of the three options put to the user (a read on the popup's list handle; the registry reporting the labels to C# on every rebuild, a message per rebuild and an allocation budget at 300 items; the labels in `form`'s autofill report, which M6 leaves unchanged), the user chose the read.
- *The contract.* `TwinHandle<TOptions>.ReadAsync<TResult>(string identifier, JsonTypeInfo<TResult> resultTypeInfo) where TResult : class` calls the named method of the module's handle with no arguments; the method answers a JSON string, which the source-generated context deserializes (ADR 16 rule 4), so the interop result is always a string. A disposed handle, a gone circuit or a `null` answer reads `null`. `js/twin.ts`'s `TwinHandle` is unchanged: a module that answers a read declares its own handle type extending it. Tested by `TwinContractTests` (L2) "port: a handle read calls the module's named method and deserializes its JSON answer; a disposed handle calls nothing".
- *The first read.* `composite`'s list handle (`CompositeListTwinHandle`) gains `labels()`, which answers `CompositeListLabels` (`{ ids: string[], labels: (string | null)[] }`): after the registry's pending rebuild, each attached item's id with its label (the registered label, else the text element's text, else the item's), in the order report's order (CMP-EVT-03); a reference-role or disposed handle answers empty arrays. The ids let C# map each label to its registration whether or not the order report has arrived. `CompositeRootBehavior.ReadLabelsAsync()` waits for the first attach (or the behavior's disposal), sends the registrations the module lacks, then reads. L3 "port: the floating handle reads its items' ids and labels in rendered order, a registered label else the text" (`composite-list.test.ts`).
- *Its use.* `SelectRoot`'s autofill makes the read only when the value/label match and the explicit `Label` match both miss, after the force-mount (SEL-STA-09): one round trip on Server, and only on an autofill. Consequence of that order: Base UI's second pass reads one array in which an explicit label and a rendered text sit at their items' indexes, so an earlier item's rendered text beats a later item's explicit label; the port's explicit-label pass runs first, so the later item's explicit label wins there. The answer travels as one JavaScript → C# message, so a list whose labels pass Server's 32 KB receive limit (some thousands of short labels) loses the read, and with it only the third priority. L4 `select-form.spec.ts` "browser autofill by rendered label" (SEL-FRM-18's three titles, both modes).

**2026-11-26, elements inside a same-origin iframe resolve through a registered frame document, and C# event handlers there do not fire (M6 track 10, [issue 316](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/316); design point 5 (a), chosen by the user 2026-11-26).** Blazor revives an `ElementReference` by a query of the host document, so a part the `portal` module moved into a same-origin iframe arrived as `null` and its handle could not attach ("Iodine: no element for the reference"; on Server the circuit ended). The 2026-10-28 shadow-root lookup now covers frame documents.
- *As built.* `js/twin.ts` gains `registerDocument(frameDocument): () => void` beside `registerShadowRoot`, in the same page-global map, so an id and a capture id resolve inside a registered frame document. The host document is never registered. `resolveElement` accepts an `HTMLElement` of the frame's realm. `js/portal.ts` registers the root its node is moved into when that root is a shadow root (as before) or the document of a frame other than the host's, telling them apart by node type, since a frame's nodes belong to its own realm. `ToastViewport` awaits its portal's move (`PopupPortalContext.WhenMoved`) before it attaches, because the `toast` module reads the viewport's owner window and document at its attach; under load it had attached in the host document first. `Interop/` and `TwinBase` are unchanged: the capture ids already travel with every element attach.
- *Deviation, as issue 146's for shadow roots (Amendments 2026-10-29).* Blazor's event delegation listens at the host document, and an event raised inside an iframe never reaches it, so no C# handler on an element inside the iframe fires: a `@onclick` on a part or a consumer element there does nothing. The modules listen on the elements themselves, so the behaviour they own works inside the frame: the Viewport's owner-document listeners (TST-STR-13, TST-EDGE-08), F6, hover and focus. The documents tell a consumer who portals into an iframe that C# handlers inside it do not fire, as for a shadow-root container.
- *Rejected.* (b) a host-document anchor resolved into `contentDocument` by Toast's module alone, since every part inside the frame attaches its own handle; and (c) leaving the clauses open past 1.0.0.
- Tests: L3 "port: an element revived as null resolves by its capture id, and an id by itself, in a registered frame document until it is released" (`twin.test.ts`) and "port: a node moved into a same-origin iframe registers its document, so a part inside resolves by its capture id until the node leaves" (`portal.test.ts`); L4 `toast.viewport.spec.ts` "rebinds owner-document listeners once across empty store cycles" (both modes).

**2026-11-27, a collapsed reopen takes no initial focus (M6 track 10, [issue 372](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/372); design point 10l-1 (a), chosen by the user 2026-11-26).** Shift+Tab inside an open bar menu closes it (`focus-out`, `useListNavigation.ts:771-792`) and focuses its trigger, whose focus reopens it (`trigger-focus`) in the same native event. React batches the two `setOpen` calls, so `open` never commits `false` and `FloatingFocusManager`'s initial-focus effect (`FloatingFocusManager.tsx:679-735`) does not run again: the focus stays on the trigger. The port commits the close and the reopen apart on both modes, and the reopened menu took initial focus. (1) `interactions` exports `noteCloseRequest(session)`, which marks the session for the rest of the task; its `requestOpenChange(false)` notes itself, and `composite`'s Shift+Tab notes its `focus-out` before the `leave` decision. The focus manager's own `focus-out` close, through `claimSessionClose`, does not note itself (wording corrected 2026-11-27, PR 400's verifier nit). (2) An open request made while the mark holds stores `{ event }` (`{ event, reason }` from the later bullet below) under `session.dataRef.current['iodine.interactions.collapsedReopen']` (`COLLAPSED_REOPEN_KEY`, `CollapsedReopen`); every open request replaces or clears it. (3) The focus manager's initial-focus effect spends the mark and moves no focus when the session's recorded open event is the mark's event, or a relayed stand-in of the same type (on Server the close's commit ends the reopen request's pairing). (Superseded by the later bullet below: identity only, with the stand-in settled onto the mark at the commit.) It applies to every popup family whose close and reopen share a task, as Base UI's batching does. No C# change. Tests: L4 `menubar.spec.ts` "port: Shift+Tab inside an open bar menu closes it with focus-out" (the focus stays on File's trigger; red 9/10 on `main`); L3 "port: a focus open requested in the task of its session's close request is marked a collapsed reopen; a later task's is not" (`interactions.test.ts`) and "port: an open marked a collapsed reopen takes no initial focus; the next open does" (`focus-manager.test.ts`).
- *Later 2026-11-27 (PR 400's verifier nits, [PR 410](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/410)): the mark is settled at the open's commit.* The mark is `{ event, reason }`, the reason being the reopen request's. When a relayed commit opens the session, `interactions` keeps the mark for the reopen's own event. It moves the mark onto a relayed stand-in of the same type and reason (Server), and clears it for any other open, so a C#-controlled or imperative open never meets a stale mark. A committed close keeps it, because on Server the close commits first. An update that moves only the answered counter keeps the mark: C# moves the counter when a request arrives, so on Server such an update can land between the close's commit and the reopen's (PR 410's verifier). A reopen C# canceled leaves its mark for the next open's commit to clear. A close dropped by `claimSessionClose` no longer notes itself. Point (3) then reads: the focus manager spends the mark and moves no focus only when the recorded open event is the mark's event (identity). Tests: L3 `interactions.test.ts` "port: a relayed stand-in of the reopen keeps its collapsed-reopen mark with the request's reason, and the mark then names it", "port: an answered-only update between the close's commit and the reopen's keeps the collapsed-reopen mark for the reopen", "port: an open committed for another reason clears the collapsed-reopen mark it does not name" and "port: a close request that is dropped marks no collapsed reopen"; `focus-manager.test.ts` "port: an open whose recorded event the collapsed-reopen mark does not hold takes its initial focus" (each red on `main` b7d4033b).

**2026-11-27, a dialog-family popup's initial focus waits for its composite-key stop, and a replaced portal node resets its move first (M6 track 10, [issue 381](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/381); design point 10m-1 (a), chosen by the user 2026-11-27).** Base UI stops the composite keys with the popup's own `onKeyDown` (`DialogPopup.tsx:82-86`), which is in place from the open's commit. The port's stop is the `popup` module's Popup role (`PopupElementOptions.StopCompositeKeys`), and `DialogPopupBehavior` started its attach together with the focus manager's. When the focus manager answered first, focus sat in the popup before the stop was armed. In the window, an arrow key, Home or End reached a toolbar around a popup portaled inside it, and the toolbar moved focus to its last item (`toolbar.spec.ts:1123` [server], quarantined until 2026-12-10).
- *The order.* The focus manager's first attach, which applies the initial focus, is sent only after the popup module's first attach. Both go to the module in the order they are sent, so the stop is armed before the focus manager's attach runs. The popup module is imported while the portal moves (`TwinBase.PrepareAsync`, issue 147's precedent), so its attach goes out as soon as the move completes, and the focus manager's attach follows at once. Later renders send the focus manager's options at once. This is the rule that the attach order of two handles on one element is pinned by a test. An order by answer (waiting for `PopupModuleAttachment.Attached`) was measured first. On Server it added about two round trips to a newly created popup's focus and outside `aria-hidden`: focus median 292 / 771 ms at a 50 / 150 ms round trip, against ADR 01's 177 / 444. The order by send measured 179 / 482, with `aria-hidden` at 171 / 474 (local, 2026-11-27, `--workers 1`), so ADR 01's dialog row keeps its figures.
- *The move reset (found by the order).* `PopupPortalBehavior.SyncNodeAsync` reset the context's move only after the old handle's disposal round trip when a reopen rendered a new portal node. A render during that round trip attached the new node and completed the old, already completed move; the late reset then left a move that nothing completed. Before the order, every popup handle captured the stale completed task, so nothing waited on it. With the order, the focus manager awaited the fresh one, and the second open of `dialog.spec.ts`'s gap measurement got no focus or outside `aria-hidden` at +50 and +150 ms. The reset now runs before the round trip, as `DetachNodeAsync` already did.
- *Scope.* Dialog, AlertDialog and Drawer, which compose `DialogPopupBehavior`. Popover, Menu and Select set the same `StopCompositeKeys` option and get the same order in a follow-up pull request (granted by the orchestrator 2026-11-27). Later 2026-11-27: the follow-up landed with [PR 402](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/pull/402), inside a toolbar only, where those parts stop the keys. They order within their own after-render method rather than through a gate like `DialogPopupBehavior`'s `keyStopSent`: the method awaits the popup module's prepared import, then calls `SyncAsync`, which sends the attach at once, and only then starts the focus manager's attach. While `js/popup.js` is slow, that await also delays what the method starts after it: the list handle's sync (`listSync`, Menu and Select), Menu's `filterSync` and the batch boundary. Outside a toolbar nothing is prepared and nothing waits.
- Tests: L4 `toolbar.spec.ts` "port: a dialog popup inside the toolbar takes focus only once its composite keydowns are stopped" (`js/popup.js` stalled 600 ms; red on the base 8/8 on both modes, End moved focus to the toolbar's "empty" button), with the quarantine on "port: a dialog popup portaled into a container inside the toolbar stops the composite keydowns" lifted; L2 `PopupPortalTests` "port: a node replaced while its old handle's disposal is in flight completes the new move once a later render attaches it" (red on the base: the move stays pending). Later 2026-11-27 (PR 401's verifier nits): L2 `DialogPopupTests` "port: the focus manager's first attach is sent after the popup module's attach" and "port: a popup module import that fails lets the focus manager attach", coverage ports of PR 401 whose reds are planted faults (the gate removed; the fallback's release removed). The gate waits for the send, not the answer, so a popup attach that throws in JavaScript lets focus proceed, as before the order.

**2026-11-27, a bar trigger's blur close waits for its menu's initial focus (M6 track 10, [issue 367](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/367); design point 10-367a (1), chosen by the user 2026-11-27).** A bar menu opened by a press is visible, and cut out of its backdrop, as soon as it is inserted (2026-11-25 above), while its popup's `focus-manager` takes the initial focus a round trip or more later on Server (ADR 01's row of 2026-11-25). In Base UI the popup has the focus from the open's commit, so a blur of the trigger never closes the menu (`useFocus.ts:185-233` sees the focus move into the floating element). In the port the trigger kept the focus in that window, and a hover onto a sibling trigger (the bar's hover highlight, MBR-PTR-06) or a press on the backdrop closed the menu with `trigger-focus`, before its `sibling-open` (which a cancel could then not keep open, MBR-EVT-01) or its `outside-press` (MBR-PTR-09).
- *The window.* A Menu trigger whose menu belongs to a bar (its session's parent is the bar's) starts the window at its opening `mousedown` (the press that requests the open through C#, where issue 363's `noteBarMenuOpenRequest` runs), and its session's press-opened open (`openchange` with `trigger-press`) adopts it or starts one. On Server the menu is inserted before the session's relay brings the open, and the trigger's focus option can arrive first. Any other open change of the session ends it, and so does the `focus-manager` module's initial-focus effect, which calls the new export `noteInitialFocusArmed(session)` before it reads or moves the focus. While the window lasts, a capture `pointerdown` listener on the trigger's document records the last non-touch press outside the trigger, with the composed path of its dispatch.
- *The hold.* `createFocusController`'s blur close, after its existing checks, is held instead of requested while the window lasts, with the press recorded before the blur.
- *The re-judgement at the arm,* from where the focus is before the initial focus moves:
  - in the popup, on the trigger or on a trigger of any menu of the bar: the close is dropped (Base UI's blur went to the popup; the bar's `sibling-open` then closes the menu with its own reason);
  - a press outside moved the focus: the recorded `pointerdown` runs through the session's outside-press listener (`replayOutsidePress`), with the same gates (the press type, the tree, the outside-press gate, the bubbling), as soon as the floating handle installs that listener (2026-11-24 above), or at once if it is installed; any open change of the session drops a waiting replay;
  - otherwise (a keyboard move to an unrelated element): the close is requested with `trigger-focus`, as before.
- *A press with no held close* (PR 414's CI, run 46036554991). On Server a trigger's focus option can arrive after a press in the window: the trace of `menubar.relaxations.spec.ts`'s 50 ms title shows the open's relay before the backdrop press and the option after it, so the press's blur met no listener and nothing was held, while the outside-press listener was not installed yet either; the menu stayed open. At the arm, a press the window recorded with no held close runs through the outside-press path too, whose gates tell an inside press from an outside one, and the arm moves no focus when the press moved it away from the trigger.
- *No initial focus after the move.* Whenever the arm finds a held close and the focus away from the trigger (every case above but the focus back on the trigger), the export returns `true` and the initial-focus effect moves no focus: Base UI's popup took its initial focus before the move the hold records. Without this, a hover onto a sibling trigger in the window had its close dropped, and then File's initial focus pulled the focus from the sibling's trigger into File's menu, closing the sibling's hover-opened menu with `trigger-focus` (`menubar.spec.ts` "should open submenus on hover when another submenu is already open", "should respect closeOnClick on nested items when the menu was opened on hover" and "correctly opens new menu on hover after clicking on its trigger and entering from hover (#2222)", Server, contained triggers: 28 of 90 failed before it; `main` passes them 90/90, and so does this change).
- *Its end without an arm* (PR 414's verifier). The window also ends when the trigger that started it detaches (`createMenuTriggerController`'s dispose) and when its session is released (`releaseSession`), which removes its `pointerdown` listener and drops a held close. A window a press started and whose open never came waits while the menu is closed; the press-open that adopts it drops the press it recorded meanwhile (a blur before the adoption took its own press). A session whose outside press is off, or that has no dismissal, gets the held close with `trigger-focus` instead of a replay.
- *Scope.* Bar menus opened by a press only. A touch press is not replayed (its outside press is judged at `touchend`); its held close is requested with `trigger-focus`. Every other focus-open trigger (Tooltip, Popover, PreviewCard, NavigationMenu, a plain Menu) and a bar menu opened by the keyboard, a focus or a hover keep the immediate close. No C# change.
- Tests: L3 `interactions.test.ts` "port: a bar trigger's blur before its press-opened menu's initial focus arms is held, and sent with trigger-focus at the arm when focus is outside", "port: a held bar trigger blur is dropped at the arm when focus is then on a sibling trigger of the bar or in the popup", "port: a held bar trigger blur that a press outside caused is replayed as an outside press once the floating handle attaches", "port: a bar trigger's blur before its press-requested open arrives is held from the press, and its outside press replayed at the arm" "port: a held bar trigger blur is dropped when the menu closes or reopens before the arm", "port: a bar trigger's blur after its menu's initial focus armed closes at once with trigger-focus", and, from PR 414's verifier, "port: a bar trigger detached in the window ends it: its press listener is removed and its held close is never sent", "port: a bar menu session released in the window ends it: its press listener is removed and its held close is never sent", "port: a press recorded while a press-started window waited for an open that never came is not replayed after a later press-open", "port: a touch press outside is not replayed: the held close is sent with trigger-focus at the arm", "port: a held bar trigger blur that a press outside caused is replayed at the arm when the floating handle attached before it" and "port: a held bar trigger blur that a press outside caused is sent with trigger-focus when the session's outside press is off" and, from PR 414's CI, "port: a press outside recorded in the window, whose blur no focus listener saw, is replayed as an outside press at the arm" (red on `main` b32d48c0); `focus-manager.test.ts` "port: the initial-focus arm sends a bar trigger's held blur close when focus is outside the popup and the bar" and "port: the initial-focus arm takes no initial focus when a held bar trigger blur moved the focus to a sibling trigger" (red on `main`). L4 `menubar.spec.ts` "port: while a modal bar menu is open the bar is cut out of the backdrop, and a press elsewhere is an outside press" and "port: a canceled sibling-open close leaves two bar menus open" now act as soon as the menu shows, without waiting for the popup's focus (red 20/20 each on `main`'s `src/`, Server at 40 ms one way; figures in ADR 01 Amendments 2026-11-27).

**2026-11-28, a re-attach while the attach is in flight awaits it and forwards its options (M6 lane B, [issue 132](https://github.com/JakeMurrayDev/Iodine.Headless/issues/132); design point 10-132a (b), chosen by the user 2026-11-28).** `TwinBase.AttachAsync` and `AttachTokenAsync` stored the handle before the attach completed and returned it at once to a repeated call for the same element or token, so a part whose render landed during its first attach re-attached, got the pending handle and signalled "attached" before the module ran; and the repeated call's options, which the part then counted as sent, were dropped. M6 lane B's audit of `main` found 71 attach methods: 41 dedupe their own attach in flight (every popup family, Composite, NavigationMenu, Drawer, Toast), 25 signal after an unguarded re-attach (5 in production: `ButtonBehavior`, which through `ToolbarButton` released `ToolbarRootContext.ItemAttachesAsync` early, the TLB-STA-04 trap; `OtpFieldField`, `SelectField`, `NumberFieldField` and `FieldControlBehavior`'s hand-overs; 19 fixture-only `TwinAttached` sources), and 4 only lost the update.
- *The rule.* A repeated call made while the attach is in flight, or after it while an earlier forward is still sending (PR 427's verifier, finding 1), awaits it, its failure included, then sends its own options to the handle's `update` when their JSON or their references argument differs from the last sent in that window; each forward waits for the one before it, so they keep call order. A repeated call after the attach completed returns the handle and sends nothing, as before. Unchanged options send nothing (ADR 16 rule 7). The per-part guards stay; standing rule "a part that signals 'attached' awaits the first attach" is now the base's guarantee.
- *A forward older than the part's update (PR 427's CI, run 46175183428).* A forward that resumes after the part sent newer options through the handle's `update` is dropped: the options it carries are a render older. Without this, `menu.filter-submenu.spec.ts` "stops parent navigation at the boundary when loopFocus is disabled" failed on Server 5 of 20 locally (an item's forward restored the default `loopFocus`), against 0 of 660 on main; with it, 3 of 600. The remainder comes from the await itself (a part's later after-render work, here the submenu trigger's module sync, runs a round trip later, so a key pressed right after the fixture's `data-attached` can land before it): measured with the await alone 1 of 400, with the forward alone 0 of 400; a fixture readiness question for the Menu family, reported to the orchestrator.
- *Not covered.* A part that builds a new twin on every call (`OtpFieldInput`'s label twin) never reaches the rule; that is filed separately by the orchestrator.
- Tests: L2 `TwinReattachTests` (eight titles: a queued forward older than the part's update dropped, the forward of changed options, no update for unchanged ones, the order and the references of several forwards, the shared failure, a call while a forward is still sending, the A-B-A order, a twin disposed while a re-attach waits); L4 `twin-reattach.spec.ts` "port: a render while the button handles attach does not release the toolbar before they attach, so a substituted disabled toggle keeps the initial tab stop", both modes, red on the base (Server 10 of 10 with four barrier round trips before the module's release, WebAssembly 3 of 3).
